NOBODY IS SAFE · SALES DESK

generated 2026-08-26 from the sales arsenal · every number traces to an _ammo receipt · zero invented figures

NIS Sales Playbook

Internal desk document. The full sales motion for the Nobody Is Safe desk: who we go after, how we talk to them, how a conversation becomes a booked free audit, and what a close is. Rebased 2026-08-26 onto the RankReallyHigh book's active-99 base and the owner's stated objective.

Truth rule for this document and every conversation it governs: every number a rep says traces to a stat id in sales/_ammo/eyecarepro.json, sales/_ammo/demandboost.json or sales/_ammo/rankreallyhigh.json, or to a named column of the prospect's own row in the RankReallyHigh leadsheet (Marketing/rankreallyhigh-roster/outreach/leadsheet-rrh.csv, 99 rows, 68 columns, README beside it). Each ammo stat carries the on-disk source file and the exact extraction command that produced it. If a number is not in an ammo sheet or in the row, it is not said. Citations below use the forms (ammo: <slug>/<stat-id>) and (leadsheet: <column>).

Three more desk laws ride on the truth rule:

  1. A figure published under the 99-base ruling is said with its measured twin the first time it appears in a document: "98 of 99 sites; 76 of the 77 that returned a load time" (ammo: rankreallyhigh/speed-lcp-over-google-bar).
  2. Storefronts, sites and clients are three different units. 99 clients, 99 sites, 280 storefronts. Never swap one for another (ammo: rankreallyhigh/book-scope-clients, book-scope-storefronts).
  3. The call this desk offers is a free audit. That is its only name. The word this desk retired on 2026-08-21 does not appear in any script, email, call or deliverable, and a rep who uses it is reading a stale page.

Companion documents:

  • sales/TRAINING.md : how a rep learns the three books cold and gets certified
  • sales/EDUCATION.md : local-maps mechanics, how to read a book row, what the measured agencies actually failed to do
  • sales/agencies/<slug>/call-scripts.md, rebuttals.md, fence-sitter-questions.md : the per-agency working scripts. This playbook sets the motion; those files carry the words.
  • Marketing/rankreallyhigh-roster/outreach/nis-rrh-blitz-campaign.md and PAIN-INDEX.md : the 90-day RankReallyHigh program. The pain-point index is the strategy backbone; the ladder is generated from it.
  • Marketing/rankreallyhigh-roster/outreach/leadsheet-rrh.csv and leadsheet-rrh-README.md : the 99 rows, the contact ladder, and the truth gates per row.

Where a companion document still carries the retired base or the retired word, this playbook and the ammo sheet win until that document is rebased.

1. What this desk sells

The desk publishes measured records of marketing agencies' client books. Three books are filed: 906 businesses measured, 131,285 map points across the three (ammo: rankreallyhigh/hub-businesses, hub-map-points, hub-books). The cannabis book is the live campaign: RankReallyHigh, 99 clients, 99 sites, 280 storefronts, 33,880 map points, filed 2026-08-09 and amended 2026-08-26 (ammo: rankreallyhigh/book-scope-clients, book-scope-storefronts, book-scope-map-points, book-filed). Its docket code is RRH-146, a docket number, not a count (ammo: rankreallyhigh/book-docket-code).

The integrity spine, published on the hub and repeated verbatim in every script:

"Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included."
"Independent of every agency it measures: this desk is not affiliated with, endorsed by, or compensated by any agency named in these files, and nobody named in a file can pay to change one."

(ammo: rankreallyhigh/integrity-anchor-verbatim, integrity-independence-verbatim). The files are free. Nobody named in a file can pay to change one. The desk is a declared competitor: the RankReallyHigh book's own footer reads verbatim "An independent performance analysis of the Rank Really High client roster, not published by or affiliated with Rank Really High." (ammo: rankreallyhigh/book-boilerplate-verbatim), and the EyeCarePro book's boilerplate reads verbatim "not affiliated with, endorsed by, or compensated by any agency named in these files; operated by a competitor who publishes the receipts" (ammo: eyecarepro/anchor-verbatim).

The product is the free audit. We do not sell calls. We give full audits, second opinions, for free. We can show a prospect the entire data set of why they rank where they rank, locally and in traditional search. Taking the audit call does not mean they get pitched: we do not work with everyone, only when it is truly a good fit for both parties, and the prospect gets the benefit of the call (ammo: rankreallyhigh/funnel.offer). Two formats, their choice:

  1. The 15-minute quick audit. Every way RankReallyHigh is failing them, off their own row and the whole book.
  2. The 1-hour full audit. Education, the entire data set, and a presentation of our services ONLY IF the fit is real, decided in the first part of the call.

The owner's shape for the close, used on every conversation, verbatim:

"We can do this two ways. If you want the quick version, I can show you in 15 minutes every way RankReallyHigh is failing you. If you've got more time, I can tell you more about us, why we're auditing other agencies' entire books of business, and how we can help fix the problems RRH is charging you for."

What "we have the solution" means, and the rep says it without a flinch. The audit is run by James Compton's team. James owns marketing agencies and built SGEN, the software stack that runs marketing for businesses like the ones in these files; more than a billion dollars a year in client sales runs through those agencies, by their own books ("$1B+ client sales a year"); one of them has built and run 500-plus dispensary sites on SGEN; and SGEN is "the entire WordPress ecosystem, rebuilt from scratch" (About page, "Where I stand" and "The record behind the desk"). A RankReallyHigh client is being read by an operation that builds and ranks dispensary sites at scale, every day, and that competes with RankReallyHigh directly. That is the disclosure, and it is also the credential. Never humble about it, never hedging.

The win. The rep's product is a booked free audit: the right person, their row already read, their receipts already in hand. The desk's win is the client leaving RankReallyHigh for a fix that works, when the fit is real: a signed engagement with James's team or SGEN that replaces what RankReallyHigh was charging them for. The file stays free either way, and stays identical either way.

We never ask anyone to trust us. We hand them their own receipt.

nobodyissafe.com is dark. The preview tree above is the only reachable surface, and the book source carries noindex, so a prospect cannot Google their way to it (ammo: rankreallyhigh/funnel.honesty). The rep reads the address aloud or sends it warm; nobody finds it on their own.

3. Who we go after, and who arrives

A. Clients of a measured agency. The primary audience, and now the pursued one. The 99 RankReallyHigh rows are the live list: every one of them gets the 90-day program (section 8). Their business is one of the 906 rows on the hub; their row already contains their verdict and their finding lines, so every conversation starts personalized because the record already is (ammo: eyecarepro/every-listing-has-findings). The 710 EyeCarePro and 97 DemandBoost rows arrive through the hub and get the same closer posture with their own book's ammo sheet. The person we want is the decision maker: the owner, or whoever runs marketing. On the 2 armed rows (sunburncannabis.com, terrabis.co) the leadsheet carries a named person on a verified personal mailbox; on the 32 navigator rows it carries a verified business inbox and the first job is routing to the decision maker (leadsheet: contact_name, tier, email_status) (README, "The sendable count").

B. Agencies. The measured agencies themselves, or other agencies who have seen the hub. They are not a sales target and they are not treated as one. See Posture B.

4. The two conversation postures

Posture A: the closer posture (the client of a measured agency)

You are the person who can pull their file, and you are there to book the audit. Both, on every conversation. The objective of every touch, every call and every reply is a booked free audit; the desk's win is the client leaving RankReallyHigh for a fix that works, when the fit is real. Ruthless in pursuit and in the prosecution of RankReallyHigh; honest and generous inside the audit itself. Rules:

  1. The agency is on trial, never the reader. The reader's own row is proof of the pattern, never the reader's deficiency. It is never "your site is bad"; it is "this is what they sold you, same as the other 98". Whole-book evidence on every touch; their row as the exhibit.
  2. Say the thing plainly. The rep is armed to say, in those words, that the client is paying RankReallyHigh every month for something that is holding their business back, and that we have the solution. Then the register line, verbatim: "This is not opinion and it is not a personal attack on anyone, it just is what it is; once you see the data you cannot unsee it."
  3. Evidence, not opinion. Never editorialize RankReallyHigh as bad people. Present the measurement and let it stand. Everything negative we say is a measured, re-runnable number with a source; nothing else gets said.
  4. Read the file, do not perform it. Quote the book's own published lines (section 2). Quoting a published record beats asserting an opinion every time.
  5. Receipts before claims, meaning before receipts. The order on a call is: pull their listing page, read their finding lines, open their competitor report, then talk. The order in a sentence is the reverse: say what the number means (money, customers, pride), then offer the data as proof. Raw metrics never reach a prospect's ear: not a load time in milliseconds, not a PageSpeed score, not an index value. "Slow" is not a claim either; "a phone waits 9.2 seconds for a typical RankReallyHigh site to show anything, and a customer is gone in three" is (ammo: rankreallyhigh/speed-median-load) (the "gone in three" line is the owner-approved voice sample).
  6. Concede what the file concedes. The ECP book shows 352 of 710 practices in the top 3 somewhere (ammo: eyecarepro/in-top3). The DB book names 13 winners (ammo: demandboost/winners-13). The RRH book names all 5 storefronts that rank, with the count of their 121 spots that found them (ammo: rankreallyhigh/the-only-five-found), and then prints that all 5 of those clients still run a failing website (ammo: rankreallyhigh/rankers-still-failing-product). Winners are called winners in the file, and their remaining findings still print (ammo: demandboost/winner-cards-still-flagged). Concede the check RankReallyHigh mostly passes, LocalBusiness schema on 97 of 99 sites (ammo: rankreallyhigh/schema-localbusiness-present); it makes the rest land harder. A rep who hides the concessions breaks the desk's whole position.
  7. The door is always the same door. Free audit, 15 minutes or an hour, their choice, "yes?". Nothing else is on the menu, and the rep never offers to leave.

The voice (owner-approved, Marketing/rankreallyhigh-roster/outreach/VOICE-SAMPLE-for-approval.md): name-first on armed rows, business-name-first on unarmed rows; RankReallyHigh named as the villain every time; ONE savage true line per touch, weaponized to the row; short (an opener is about five sentences, a bump is one or two lines); the same door every close; a curiosity gap (you are on a public list, the stats are ugly, want to see?). Never humble, never hedging. Every email passes one test: would the author of the voice sample send this?

Phrase language. In prospect copy the phrase is "the search your customers actually type" or plainly "the weed search". Never list an unmeasured phrase as measured. The banned jargon in prospect copy: pins, grid, ranking points, data points, search rankings, long tail, keyphrases, review velocity, profile signals. The doctrine word for a pin is "spots".

The aggressiveness ladder

Level 1, first contact: the file and the one-line knife. Whole book only; no per-row claim yet (emails 1 and 2 go to every contactable row identically; the row narrows in at email 3). Name or business name first. RankReallyHigh named. One number at most. The knife, the owner's model line re-based to the book:

"Your business isn't the problem, it's RankReallyHigh, they are failing almost every single client they manage, and we've got the data to prove it: all 280 storefronts they have under service fully audited and almost the entire book is failing online." (ammo: rankreallyhigh/book-scope-storefronts, headline-storefronts-invisible)

Then the door: "Want to see the rest of what we measured? Fifteen minutes, free. Just say yes." Never at level 1: an opt-out invitation, a preemptive rebuttal, a link, an attachment, a raw metric, a second number.

Level 2, the receipt in front of them. Narrow to their row. On a call this is the pull (section 5, step 2); in email it is the third touch onward. Each touch adds one NEW receipt, never a repeat, in the chapter order: their place in the file; the website they sold them, by week 2; the phrase flip; who is beating them, by name; the pattern, the desk, and the open door. The website wound carries the owner's line: "I can send you the list of sites literally identical to yours, same theme, same broken stats, and RRH doesn't care." (96 of 99 client sites are the same build with the logo swapped: ammo: rankreallyhigh/template-family-01). Who is beating them is read off their row (leadsheet: grid_leader, grid_leader_coverage_pct); the whole-book version is that a company that mows lawns tops 11 of RankReallyHigh's client maps and a client tops its own map exactly once (ammo: rankreallyhigh/grid-leader-weed-man-lawn-care, grid-leader-client-self). Resources are described in text and delivered on reply or at the audit; cold email carries no links and no media.

Level 3, the direct ask, twice, two formats. The two-ways shape from section 1, verbatim. Ask. Handle whatever comes back with the receipts and rebuttals.md. Ask again, both formats, "yes?". A conversation that ends without the ask made twice is a rep failure, not a prospect decision.

Level 4, persistence. The email law, mirrored on the phone and in every reply: 39 emails and 8 call slots over 13 weeks to a silent row, 3 a week rising to 4 in the late chapters, never two sends in one day to a silent contact, a fresh subject per chapter thread and real threading only (campaign file, section 6). ANY reply flips the row to the aggressive daily sequence, 2 to 5 touches a day, until BOOKED or STOP. STOP means instant, permanent suppression, written in the ledger the minute it happens. Nobody ever invites it: the voice never offers to leave, never says "last one from me", never announces its own end; the day-89 touch still asks. The CAN-SPAM minimum lives only in the muted signature line, never in the prose. Links ladder warm, in this order and never earlier: rung 1 their own listing page; rung 2 a screenshot of their row; rung 3 the third-party reports; rung 4 the booking.

Truth-gated swaps. One evergreen ladder for all 99 rows, personalization through merge fields only. The only forks are armed (first-name knife) versus unarmed (business-name knife), plus the navigator forward line on verified business inboxes ("who runs marketing at {{business}}? forward this"). A line is swapped only where it would otherwise be false: the 5 FOUND rows (leadsheet: segment) get the concession form, one of the five that show up at all and still failing the website; cann-studio.com gets the honest not-scanned form (no storefront listing could be resolved, so no map was ever run; never imply a scan failure); the self-lead row quincycannabis.co never casts its own listing as the rival (leadsheet: grid_leader) (ammo: rankreallyhigh/grid-leader-quincy); the 22 rows with a blank phone load time hold the speed line (leadsheet: mobile_lcp_ms); the 59 single-location rows take singular phrasing and the 39 multi-location rows take plural (leadsheet: locations_count) (ammo: rankreallyhigh/multi-location-all-invisible). Reply stage only, never in cold copy beside the storefront headline: the double-fail cut, 93 of 99 clients invisible everywhere AND failing the website (measured 73 of 98) (ammo: rankreallyhigh/crossover-invisible-and-failing).

Posture B: an agency (the registrar posture)

When a measured agency, or anyone speaking for one, contacts the desk:

  1. The file is not negotiable and not for sale. Say the anchor verbatim: "Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included." The files are free and nobody named in one can pay to change one.
  2. Disputes go to the data, not to a rep. Every number in every book carries an extraction command that re-runs against the source, and every scanned business links a third-party scan report. A rep's job is to point at the receipt, not to argue.
  3. Do not pitch them. Do not counsel them. No selling SGEN to a measured agency, no advice on how to look better in the next scan. Either reads as the record being for sale.
  4. Escalate. Anything past "here is the receipt" goes to the owner. Reps do not speak for the desk on record contents, corrections, or legal noise. If asked whether the desk competes with them, the answer is yes and it is printed on the About page; that is the disclosure, and the numbers do not move.
5. The audit flow: from first touch to close

The mechanics that exist today, and only those. What exists: the hub v2 tree with the three books and 906 listing pages, the per-listing competitor-report and scan-report links, the booking popup on the hub and books, and the 90-day RankReallyHigh program on disk (the ladder, the call slots, the reply system, the merge-field table, and a private simulator). What does not exist yet: the real domain (nobodyissafe.com is dark; DNS and hosting are owner-owed), the form delivery endpoint (the shipped contact page declares an empty endpoint, so a popup submission is delivered nowhere and the scheduler link does not render), the desk email inbox, and the sending domain and warm-up, so the ladder sends nothing until the owner's sending infrastructure lands (ammo: rankreallyhigh/funnel.honesty, demandboost/funnel.honesty, eyecarepro/funnel.honesty). Until then the reps' own mailboxes and phones are the desk. Scripts and reps promise no mechanic off this list.

Step 1: First touch. Two doors. Outbound: the ladder and the call slots, to every contactable row (section 8). Inbound: a prospect who saw a shared link and reached out through a channel the owner personally provided; the popup delivers nowhere, so nobody arrives through it. Treat every arrival as warm and every reply as the flip: their row already exists.

Step 2: Pull the row, live. Go straight to l/rankreallyhigh/<domain>.html on the preview tree, or find them in the hub finder. This happens in front of them or on the call, not before, whenever possible. Watching their own name come up with findings attached is the moment the desk stops being a stranger. The training bar is any listing in under 60 seconds (sales/TRAINING.md).

Step 3: Read it together. The pattern first: the whole book, the argument line verbatim, "The agency optimized the smaller term." (ammo: rankreallyhigh/argument-smaller-term-verbatim). Then their row as the exhibit: verdict word in plain speech, their finding lines verbatim off the card, then their competitor report, a third-party page, not ours, showing who actually holds their map. Meaning first on every line: not a score, what the score costs them.

Step 4: The two-ways offer. The owner's shape, verbatim (section 1). Ask, handle, ask again. Let them pick the format and the time. Honesty rule: no scheduler exists, so the rep records the requested time in the ledger (section 7) and personally carries the booking to James's team. Make no promise a human will not personally keep.

Step 5: The audit. Run by James's team. The 15-minute quick audit shows them every way RankReallyHigh is failing them, off their row and the whole book. The 1-hour full audit adds the education, the entire data set of why they rank where they rank, locally and in traditional search, and a presentation of our services ONLY IF the fit is real, decided in the first part of the call. Taking the audit never means a pitch; the prospect gets the benefit either way. The rep's product is the booked audit: right person, row already read, receipts in hand. James's team owns everything from there, including fit, scope and terms.

Step 6: The close. The client moves off RankReallyHigh onto our fix, signed. Not a nice conversation, not a booked slot, not a cancelled contract on its own. A signed engagement with James's team or SGEN that replaces what RankReallyHigh was charging for. Everything before that is pipeline.

Not ready? The ladder does not stop and the rep does not go quiet. Arm them with the questions in sales/agencies/<slug>/fence-sitter-questions.md, the ones their incumbent will struggle to answer, and read them their listing address. The file does the waiting; the desk keeps asking. No breakup email exists and none is written; the door stays open and the ask stays live at day 89.

6. The pricing conversation

The posture is simple because the truth is simple:

  • The file is free. Every file, every listing, every competitor report link. Forever. That is the anchor and it is non-negotiable: "Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included."
  • The audit is free. Both formats. The prospect gets the benefit of it and owes nothing for it.
  • Nothing about the record is for sale. Not removal, not softening, not a rescan on a friendlier phrase. A rep who hints otherwise has ended their usefulness to this desk.
  • Services are presented only inside the 1-hour audit, and only when the fit is real. Decided in the first part of that call, by James's team, never by a rep.
  • Reps quote no prices. No price list exists on disk, and inventing one violates the truth rule. The honest answer to "what does it cost" is: "The file is free. The audit is free, fifteen minutes or an hour. If it turns out we are a fit, the hour is where you hear what fixing it costs, next to what RankReallyHigh is charging you now for it not being fixed."
7. State honesty: running a desk with no CRM

There is no CRM. Nothing tracks a prospect automatically, and pretending otherwise loses deals and breaks the desk's own truth law. Until the owner stands up tooling, state lives in a manual ledger the rep owns.

Keep one file per rep, sales/ledger/<username>.md, append-only, one line per event:

YYYY-MM-DD | business name | agency slug | event | next personal commitment (if any)

Events worth a line: touch sent (email or call, with the ladder slot), reply received (the flip), file pulled live, audit booked (with format and requested time), audit held (15 or 60), moved off RankReallyHigh and signed, STOP. A STOP line is written the minute it happens and the row never gets another touch. A personal commitment written in the ledger is a promise; keep it or do not make it.

The discipline this buys: any rep can say exactly what has and has not happened with a prospect, and nobody ever claims a follow-up mechanism that does not exist.

8. Objectives, and the metrics that count

The mission (owner, 2026-08-26): arm the sales people to go ruthlessly to acquire this business. The outreach exists to get appointments booked to get them to leave RankReallyHigh, because they are paying for something that is holding their business back and we have the solution.

The book objective. Every one of the 99 RankReallyHigh clients gets the full 90-day program, and every contactable row gets a live human touch (a call or a human-written reply, not only the generated ladder). The list as it stands today, every count from the leadsheet's own README recount:

Row classCountWhat it gets
Rows with a verified email56 of 99The ladder from day 1 (leadsheet: email_status)
of which armed (named person, verified personal mailbox)2 (sunburncannabis.com, terrabis.co)The first-name knife, plus a personal call from the rep (leadsheet: contact_name, tier)
of which navigator (verified business inbox, T3)32The forward line until a name lands; then the row flips to armed (leadsheet: tier, email_status)
of which verified personal mailboxes (T1 and T2)24The business-name knife on a person's own inbox, the first-name knife on the 2 armed rows (leadsheet: email_status, tier)
catch_all emails28Verify on send, then the ladder (leadsheet: email_status)
published (observed, not verified) emails8Not sendable until a verification pass upgrades them (README)
no email of any kind75 carry a phone and get the call ladder only; cann-studio.com and thegrasscab.com carry neither and cannot be touched until a contact lands (leadsheet: email, phone)
phones on file13The 8 call slots (leadsheet: phone)
suppressed at send time1 (theniagaraherbalist.com)CASL (leadsheet: canada_flag)

So 97 of 99 rows carry an email or a phone (derived: 99 minus the 2 with neither), 96 can be touched today (derived: 97 minus the suppressed row), and 56 are in the ladder from day 1, growing to 92 as the 28 catch_all and 8 published rows verify (derived: 56 + 28 + 8). Contact coverage, not deliverability, is the binding constraint; the README names a contact-waterfall pass over the 15 published and none rows as the highest-leverage next action.

The rep daily objective, derived from the cadence and the list. The cadence: 39 emails and 8 call slots per row across 13 weeks; 3 emails a week in weeks 1 to 8, 4 in weeks 9 to 11, 2 in week 12, 1 in week 13 (39 in all; campaign file, section 4); one touch per day maximum to a silent row; any reply flips the row to 2 to 5 touches a day until BOOKED or STOP (campaign file, section 6). Applied to the list:

  1. Ladder sends. 56 rows at 3 a week is 168 emails a week (derived: 56 x 3), about 34 per working day over 5 days; the 4-a-week weeks run 224 (derived: 56 x 4), about 45 a day. At 92 rows the 3-a-week figure becomes 276 (derived: 92 x 3). These are merge-field sends from human mailboxes; the rep's labor is reading every reply the same day.
  2. Calls. 13 phone rows at 8 slots each is 104 call attempts over the 90 days (derived: 13 x 8), 8 a week (derived: 104 / 13), about 2 per working day. Every call is logged. None of the 13 phones sits on a navigator row (leadsheet: phone, tier), so the decision-maker hunt on the 32 navigator rows runs by the forward line until numbers land in the pipeline and the leadsheet is re-run.
  3. Pulls. The row is pulled live on every conversation, under 60 seconds, no exceptions.
  4. Replies. Every reply is answered the same day and the row moves to the daily sequence: 2 to 5 touches a day, the ask made twice per conversation, until BOOKED or STOP.
  5. The ledger. One line per event, written the day it happens.

Targets the owner must set. There is no historical baseline for this desk, so this playbook sets no conversion targets; the first cohort of ledger data becomes the baseline. The following are marked for the owner and are blank until ruled:

  • [OWNER TO SET] audits booked per week, by format
  • [OWNER TO SET] audits held per week, and the held-to-signed rate the desk is judged on
  • [OWNER TO SET] moved-off-RankReallyHigh signings per quarter
  • [OWNER TO SET] how the 99 rows split across reps, and the reply-handling capacity per rep per day
  • [OWNER TO RULE] whether the 28 catch_all rows enter the ladder before a verification pass, or after
  • [OWNER TO RULE] funding a contact pass for the 32 navigator rows (phones) and the 15 published and none rows

Metrics. Count only events that actually occur. Per rep, per week, from the ledger:

  1. Touches sent (email and call, by ladder slot)
  2. Replies received (flips)
  3. Files pulled live in front of a prospect
  4. Audits booked (by format, with requested time)
  5. Audits held
  6. Moved off RankReallyHigh and signed (closes)
  7. STOPs (logged, never counted as pipeline; posture B contacts are logged but not counted either)

Secondary reads once volume exists: touch-to-reply rate, reply-to-booked rate, booked-to-held rate, held-to-signed rate, and the split by row class (armed, navigator, personal, phone-only), because those are structurally different conversations.

9. What can sink this desk

Four failure modes, all rep-controllable:

  1. An invented number. One unverifiable stat in one call gives RankReallyHigh the rebuttal it wants. If it is not in an ammo sheet or in the row, it is not said. A ruled figure without its measured twin, a storefront count called a client count, or a raw metric read to a prospect in place of what it means, each counts as an invented number.
  2. A paid-file rumor. Any behavior that lets anyone claim the record is purchasable. The anchor is the defense; behave so it stays true.
  3. A promised mechanism that does not exist. "You'll get a confirmation email" when no endpoint is wired; "the system will follow up" when no sending domain exists. Every broken micro-promise spends the desk's only asset, which is that its claims check out.
  4. PII leakage. No client emails, no phone numbers, no owner personal names in any conversation or deliverable: not in this playbook, not in TRAINING, EDUCATION, the scripts or the video scripts. Business names and public measured results only. The leadsheet's contact columns are pipeline data for the sender system; only the campaign file and the private simulator may carry contact merge fields (ammo: demandboost/laws.pii) (leadsheet README, "PII discipline").

NIS Sales Training

Internal desk document. How a new rep goes from zero to certified: knowing all three books cold, pulling any of the 906 receipts live in under 60 seconds, delivering the one savage true line and the two-ways ask on any RankReallyHigh row in the same 60 seconds, and never saying a number that does not trace.

Prerequisites: read sales/PLAYBOOK.md (the motion) and sales/EDUCATION.md (the mechanics) first, then the owner's campaign brief sales/_campaign-brief-audit-outreach.md (the offer and every law, in the owner's own words) and the approved voice sample Marketing/rankreallyhigh-roster/outreach/VOICE-SAMPLE-for-approval.md (the register every prospect-facing sentence is measured against). This document assumes all four.

The one law of training: in every drill and every role-play, an invented or misremembered number is a hard fail of that attempt. Not a deduction, a fail. The desk's entire position is that its claims check out; training is where that reflex is built. Every number in this document carries an ammo pointer of the form (ammo: <slug>/<stat-id>) into sales/_ammo/*.json, where the on-disk source and extraction command live, or names the leadsheet column it was read from (Marketing/rankreallyhigh-roster/outreach/leadsheet-rrh.csv, 99 rows, 68 columns).

Three habits ride on that law and are drilled as hard as the numbers:

  1. The measured twin. The RankReallyHigh book is published under the 2026-08-24 ruling: 99 active clients is the base, and a client that could not be scanned is scored as failing what it could not be scanned for. Every figure that carries that ruling is said WITH its measured twin the first time it appears in any document or any conversation: "94 of 99; 93 of the 98 that were scanned, plus the one that could not be" (ammo: rankreallyhigh/clients-invisible-everywhere). The ammo sheet prints both halves in the value field; a rep who says the ruled half alone has said a number that does not trace.
  2. Three units, never swapped. A client is one dispensary business with one website (99). A site is one client website (99, one per client). A storefront is one physical location with its own 121-pin map grid (280). "275 of 280" is storefronts; "94 of 99" is clients; "96 of 99" is sites (ammo: rankreallyhigh/book-scope-clients, book-scope-storefronts, book-scope-pins-per-grid).
  3. RRH-146 is a docket number. The book's code is a case number, not a count of anything. The book is 99 clients (ammo: rankreallyhigh/book-docket-code).
1. The certification bar (read this first, train toward it)

A rep is certified when they pass all seven gates in section 7, and the headline gate is this one:

Given any business name from any of the three books, the rep pulls that business's live receipt in under 60 seconds: its hub listing page, its verdict word, its finding lines read aloud, and its competitor-report link opened. Any of the 906. Cold.

The second bar sits beside it, for the RankReallyHigh book specifically:

Given any row of the RankReallyHigh leadsheet, the rep delivers, in under 60 seconds, the one savage true line that row supports and the two-ways ask, word-perfect, and can answer "says who?" for the line by naming the column or the stat id.

Everything below builds to those two.

2. The three books: quick-reference cards

Memorize these cards. They are the skeleton; the books themselves are the flesh.

Card 1: EyeCarePro (ECP-710), optometry

  • Scope: 710 practices, every one measured on "glasses near me", 121 pins each, 85,910 map points (ammo: eyecarepro/total-practices, one-phrase, pins-per-grid, map-points)
  • Published headline, quote it verbatim: "358 of 710 EyeCarePro practices sit outside the top 3 on their own neighborhood map, and 288 of the 710 maps are led by a national chain. 710 practices, 85,910 map points, one dossier." (ammo: eyecarepro/headline-verbatim)
  • Map verdict words: NOWHERE 175, POCKET_NO_REACH 183, PACK_WITH_TURF 338, PACK_NEVER_FIRST 14 (ammo: eyecarepro/verdict-distribution)
  • Product verdict words: SOUND 0, WEAK 107, FAILING 377, BROKEN 226 (ammo: eyecarepro/zero-sound-sites)
  • The absolute to remember: zero SOUND websites in the whole book, and 710 of 710 clients have at least one measured deficiency (ammo: eyecarepro/both-fail)
  • Trends pair: "glasses near me" 71 vs "optometrist near me" 23, and the bigger term won all 53 of 53 weeks (ammo: eyecarepro/trends-demand-gap, trends-53-of-53)
  • Book: eyecarepro/

Card 2: DemandBoost (DB-097), chiropractic

  • Scope: 97 clients, 95 scanned on "chiropractor near me", 121 pins each (ammo: demandboost/clients-total, grid-methodology)
  • Published headline, quote it verbatim: "One agency, 97 chiropractic clients, one recycled template. 82 of the 95 clients scanned do not own 'chiropractor near me' on their own local map, and the median scanned client holds a 5.8% share of it." (ammo: demandboost/book-headline-verbatim)
  • Verdict words: WINNER 13, PARTIAL 46, INVISIBLE 36, plus 2 not measured (ammo: demandboost/segment-split)
  • The absolute to remember: all 97 of 97 client sites fail Google's 2.5 second phone bar; the fastest is 2.87s (ammo: demandboost/speed-all-97-fail)
  • The signature pattern: the photocopier. 83 of 97 sites on 12 shared templates, and the agency's own homepage runs the largest family (ammo: demandboost/templates-83-of-97, agency-on-same-template)
  • Trends pair: "chiropractor near me" 56 vs "back pain near me" 29, bigger term won 48 of 53 weeks (ammo: demandboost/trends-category-vs-symptom, trends-weeks-lead)
  • Book: demandboost/

Card 3: RankReallyHigh (docket RRH-146, a case number, not a count), cannabis retail

  • Scope: 99 active clients, 99 sites, one website each; 280 storefronts, every storefront its own 121-pin grid on "weed near me"; 33,880 map points. Filed 2026-08-09, amended 2026-08-26 when the book was rebuilt on the active client set (ammo: rankreallyhigh/book-scope-clients, book-scope-storefronts, book-scope-pins-per-grid, book-scope-map-points, book-filed, book-docket-code)
  • Published headline, quote it verbatim from the book's argument paragraph: "On "weed near me", worth roughly twice as much, and the word customers actually type, 275 of 280 (98.2%) are invisible and only 5 rank." The argument opens with one sentence, also verbatim: "The agency optimized the smaller term." (ammo: rankreallyhigh/argument-verbatim, argument-smaller-term-verbatim, headline-storefronts-invisible, headline-storefronts-found, headline-invisible-share)
  • Verdict words, as published: NOWHERE 94, PACK_WITH_TURF 5. Said with the twin, every first time: 94 of 99 is 93 of the 98 clients that were scanned, plus the 1 that could not be; 5 of 99 rank somewhere, measured 5 of 98. The one is cann-studio.com: no storefront listing could be resolved, so no "weed near me" grid was ever run for it. Its measured verdict is NOT_MEASURED, and under the ruling its published row reads NOWHERE. The book says so on the row itself, verbatim: "No storefront was resolved, so no "weed near me" grid was ever run and nothing on this dispensary's map was measured. Under the stated rule a client that could not be scanned is scored as failing what it could not be scanned for, so it is counted with the clients invisible everywhere rather than set aside. That is a ruling, not a reading." Never call it a scan failure; nothing was scanned. (ammo: rankreallyhigh/clients-invisible-everywhere, clients-ranking-somewhere, client-never-scanned, client-never-scanned-honest-line)
  • The absolute to remember: the five that rank, by name and by how many of their 121 pins found them: Roots LA 121, Roslindale Cannabis 121, Nile Cannabis 116, Quincy Cannabis Co 115, Clouditude 40. All five still run a failing or broken website (4 FAILING, 1 BROKEN), and 0 of 99 clients (measured 0 of 98) both rank somewhere on "weed near me" and pass a majority of the six product checks (ammo: rankreallyhigh/the-only-five-found, rankers-still-failing-product, rank-and-pass-zero)
  • The signature pattern: the flip. The same 280 storefronts, the same Google place IDs, scanned on two phrases. On "dispensary near me", the phrase the agency chose to chase, 139 of 280 reach the top 3 of their own grid and 67 of 280 hold number one; 141 of 280 sit at rank 4 or worse or never crack a top 3 at all. On "weed near me", the phrase customers type, 5 of 280 rank anywhere. A broken scan cannot do that; the difference is the phrase (ammo: rankreallyhigh/flip-same-storefronts-both-phrases, dispensary-top3, dispensary-rank-one, dispensary-beaten, headline-storefronts-found)
  • Trends set, Google's 0-100 index, US, past 12 months: weed 66, dispensary 35, cannabis 21. Weed out-scored dispensary 53 of 53 weeks; the peak week was 2026-04-19, weed at 100 against dispensary at 42. In prospect copy the sweep is said as "every single week for the past year, not one exception"; the deck keeps the raw 53 of 53 (ammo: rankreallyhigh/trends-weed-average, trends-dispensary-average, trends-cannabis-average, trends-weed-wins-every-week, trends-peak-week)
  • Who leads the grids: 153 different businesses lead at least one of the 279 grids that returned a leader; a client dispensary leads its own grid exactly once in the whole book. Weed Man Lawn Care, a lawn-care franchise, leads 11 grids and is the named top result on the flagship grid of 4 clients (Thrive Dispensary Metropolis, Cookies Peoria Heights, Cookies Bloomington, Terrabis - Grayville). A spam listing whose name is typed in look-alike foreign characters leads 10. The listing "Quincy Cannabis Co Weed Dispensary" leads 9, and the roster's own Quincy Cannabis Co is one of the five (ammo: rankreallyhigh/grid-leaders-distinct, grid-leader-client-self, grid-leader-weed-man-lawn-care, grid-leader-weed-man-flagships, grid-leader-spoofed-listing, grid-leader-quincy)
  • Speed: 98 of 99 sites fail Google's 2.5 second phone bar (measured 76 of the 77 that returned a load time). The median site takes 9.2 seconds, 3.7 times the bar, and 35 sites take more than 10 seconds (ammo: rankreallyhigh/speed-lcp-over-google-bar, speed-median-load, speed-bar-multiple, speed-over-10-seconds)
  • Template: 96 of 99 sites are the same build, template family-01, the WordPress theme "weedpress", logo swapped. 95 of 99 run the same menu plugin, joint-ecommerce, and 92 sit on the exact same version, 1.38.1 (ammo: rankreallyhigh/template-family-01, template-theme-weedpress, plugin-joint-ecommerce, plugin-version-cohort)
  • The multi-location knife: 39 of 39 clients that run two or more storefronts are invisible on "weed near me" at every one of them. The four largest chains, 15 to 18 storefronts each (FlynnStoned 18, Nirvana MI 18, Star Buds IL 17, Urban Wellness 15), were scanned at every storefront and found at none; the two largest alone are 36 storefronts, 0 found (ammo: rankreallyhigh/multi-location-all-invisible, chains-largest-four-nowhere, chains-two-largest-zero)
  • Product: 98 of 99 sites fail a majority of the six product checks (measured 78 of 99) (ammo: rankreallyhigh/product-failing-verdict)
  • Book: rankreallyhigh/ and every client's own row at l/rankreallyhigh/<domain>.html (a template; put the client's domain in place of <domain>)
3. The learning path

Seven modules, in order. A rep who cannot pass a module's exit check repeats the module, not the check.

Module 1: The desk itself (half a day). Read the hub front page and the dossiers index. Learn the anchor verbatim: "Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included." Learn the independence line and both non-affiliation boilerplates (ammo: eyecarepro/anchor-verbatim, rankreallyhigh/integrity-anchor-verbatim, integrity-independence-verbatim, book-boilerplate-verbatim). Learn what exists and what is owner-owed: the booking form's endpoint is empty, the scheduler link is blank, nobodyissafe.com is dark, and no sending infrastructure exists; all four are owner-owed (the funnel honesty list in sales/_ammo/rankreallyhigh.json). Read section 5.1 to 5.4 of this document on day one as well: the objective, what ruthless means, the offer, and the rule against inviting anyone to go away; the drills that go with them wait for Module 5. Exit check: recite the anchor and the funnel honesty list from memory, word-perfect on the anchor.

Module 2: Mechanics (one day). Work through sales/EDUCATION.md end to end: what a geo-grid scan is, what 121 pins mean, why the map pack is the battleground for near-me phrases, what the six product checks are, and what each of the three agencies actually failed to do. Exit check: explain a pin grid and a share-of-voice number to a non-technical listener in under three minutes, no notes.

Module 3: Book deep dives (one day per book). Read each book top to bottom on its live surface (the nis-v2 addresses on the cards). Copy each card in section 2 by hand, then verify every number on the card against the rendered book and the ammo sheet. Exit check per book: recite the card's headline verbatim and every count on it, with its measured twin where the card carries one, then answer three spot questions from the trainer against the open book.

Module 4: Receipt-pull drills (repeat daily until certified). Section 4 below.

Module 5: The objective and aggressiveness (one day, then daily drills). Section 5 below. What the desk is for, what ruthless means here and what it never means, the two-ways offer word-perfect, the never-invite-go-away rule, the four triggers and the receipt that fires each, the escalation ladder, and the two drills that build the reflex.

Module 6: Role-play ladder (after Modules 4 and 5 reach par). Section 6 below.

Module 7: Certification. Section 7 below.

4. Drills against real listings

All drills run on the live hub finder and the real per-listing pages. Never on screenshots, never on a summary. The point is muscle memory in the actual surface a prospect will watch you drive.

Drill A: The pull (the 60-second core)

Trainer names a real business; rep starts from the hub front page and must reach, in order: the listing row in the finder, the listing's page at /l/<agency>/<domain>.html on the nis-v2 surface, verdict word spoken in plain speech, every finding line read aloud, competitor-report link opened. Timed.

Ladder:

  1. A1, book named: "EyeCarePro: Elkton Eyecare." Par 60 seconds.
  2. A2, book not named: "Puff Cannabis." Rep must know or deduce the book. Par 60 seconds.
  3. A3, domain only: "shoppuff.com." Par 60 seconds.
  4. A4, mixed deck: ten names across all three books, drawn at random from the finder itself. Par: 10 of 10 under 60 seconds each.
  5. A5, the row with no receipt to open: "cann-studio.com." The rep reaches the row, reads the book's own ruling line aloud, and says plainly that there is no competitor report for this client because no grid was ever run; a rep who hunts for a report that does not exist, or calls it a failed scan, fails the attempt (ammo: rankreallyhigh/client-never-scanned, client-never-scanned-honest-line).

Drill B: Finding-line fluency

Every listing card carries typed finding lines. The rep must translate each archetype into plain speech without weakening or inflating it. Reference example, the ECP book's own first row, 20/20 iCare Tyler (Tyler, TX), whose five lines read (ammo: eyecarepro/pattern-opening-row):

  • "Ranks #8 of 51 on 'glasses near me', first at 0 of 121 spots"
  • "On one shared template, one of 1220 measured sites"
  • "No llms.txt, invisible to answer engines"
  • "Mobile PageSpeed 47 of 100, under the 50 bar"
  • "Phone load 14.2s, past Google's 2.5s bar"

Exit standard: given any listing, the rep renders each line as one plain sentence a practice owner immediately understands, and can answer "says who?" for each line by pointing at the receipt.

RankReallyHigh rows add four translation rules, because the meaning law governs every sentence a prospect hears:

  • The map line ("Never found on "weed near me" at any of their 5 locations", leadsheet findings) renders as what it means: "the search your customers actually type cannot find any of your five stores from their own block". Say "the weed search" or "the search your customers actually type"; never list a phrase the book did not measure as measured.
  • The speed line ("Phone load 12.9s, past Google's 2.5s bar") renders as seconds of blank screen: "a phone customer stares at a blank screen for nearly thirteen seconds; Google's bar is about two and a half, and most customers are gone at three". Milliseconds and PageSpeed scores never enter a prospect sentence; the receipts keep them (leadsheet mobile_lcp_ms, mobile_psi).
  • The template line ("On the shared "weedpress" theme, one of 96 measured sites") renders as the owner's line: the site they were sold as theirs is the same site nearly every other client got, logo swapped (ammo: rankreallyhigh/template-family-01).
  • The llms.txt line is a presence check and nothing more: 99 of 99 sites lack the file. The rep may say "the site publishes no llms.txt file, and neither does any other site in the book"; the rep never sells the absence as invisibility to AI, whatever the card's wording (ammo: rankreallyhigh/llms-txt-absent).

Drill C: The receipt chain

For a named business, walk the full evidence chain out loud: listing row, listing page, the book section its numbers come from, the third-party competitor report. The rep must be able to say, at every hop, what the source is and why a hostile listener cannot dismiss it. Par: full chain under three minutes.

On the RankReallyHigh book the last hop is one of 98 published competitor reports, one for every client that was scanned, and behind it 560 independently openable scan reports, one per storefront per phrase, all under one scan tag on localrankingtracker.com (ammo: rankreallyhigh/receipts-competitor-reports, receipts-scan-reports, receipts-scan-tag). For the one never-scanned client the chain ends at the book's own ruling line, and the rep says so.

Drill D: Number decks

Flashcard recall, run daily, five minutes. The deck is exactly the three cards in section 2 plus these cross-cutting singles:

  • 906 businesses, 131,285 map points, 3 books (ammo: rankreallyhigh/hub-businesses, hub-map-points, hub-books)
  • ECP: median phone paint 9,387 ms; 702 of 706 over Google's bar (ammo: eyecarepro/median-mobile-lcp, lcp-over-google-bar)
  • ECP: 300 practices rank top-3 AND run a failing or broken site (ammo: eyecarepro/winners-who-should-still-leave)
  • DB: median map position #8.86; 48 of 95 never hit #1 at even one pin (ammo: demandboost/median-position, never-rank-one)
  • DB: winners' median 200 reviews vs roster median 49 (ammo: demandboost/winners-carried-by-reviews)
  • RRH: 275 of 280 storefronts invisible, 5 of 280 found, 98.2% (ammo: rankreallyhigh/headline-storefronts-invisible, headline-storefronts-found, headline-invisible-share)
  • RRH: 94 of 99 clients invisible everywhere, measured 93 of 98; 5 of 99 rank somewhere, measured 5 of 98; 1 client never scanned, cann-studio.com (ammo: rankreallyhigh/clients-invisible-everywhere, clients-ranking-somewhere, client-never-scanned)
  • RRH: the flip, 139 of 280 in the top 3 on "dispensary near me" and 67 of 280 at number one, against 5 of 280 on "weed near me" (ammo: rankreallyhigh/dispensary-top3, dispensary-rank-one, headline-storefronts-found)
  • RRH: trends 66 / 35 / 21; weed over dispensary 53 of 53 weeks (ammo: rankreallyhigh/trends-weed-average, trends-dispensary-average, trends-cannabis-average, trends-weed-wins-every-week)
  • RRH: 153 grid leaders; a client leads its own grid once; Weed Man Lawn Care 11 grids; the spoofed listing 10; the Quincy listing 9 (ammo: rankreallyhigh/grid-leaders-distinct, grid-leader-client-self, grid-leader-weed-man-lawn-care, grid-leader-spoofed-listing, grid-leader-quincy)
  • RRH: 98 of 99 sites over Google's 2.5 second bar, measured 76 of 77; median 9.2 seconds; 3.7 times the bar; 35 sites past 10 seconds (ammo: rankreallyhigh/speed-lcp-over-google-bar, speed-median-load, speed-bar-multiple, speed-over-10-seconds)
  • RRH: 96 of 99 sites on one template; 95 of 99 on one menu plugin; 92 on version 1.38.1 (ammo: rankreallyhigh/template-family-01, plugin-joint-ecommerce, plugin-version-cohort)
  • RRH: 39 of 39 multi-location clients invisible at every storefront; the two largest chains, 36 storefronts, 0 found (ammo: rankreallyhigh/multi-location-all-invisible, chains-two-largest-zero)
  • RRH: Puff Cannabis, 4.9 stars, 7,746 reviews, 10 stores, 0 pins (ammo: rankreallyhigh/pattern-reviews-do-not-rescue)
  • RRH: 93 of 99 clients invisible everywhere AND failing product, measured 73 of 98. Reply-stage only: this figure never appears in cold copy and never beside the storefront headline (ammo: rankreallyhigh/crossover-invisible-and-failing)
  • RRH: 98 competitor reports, 560 scan reports, one scan tag (ammo: rankreallyhigh/receipts-competitor-reports, receipts-scan-reports, receipts-scan-tag)
  • RRH leadsheet: 99 rows; email status verified 56, catch-all 28, published 8, none 7; 2 armed rows (a named contact on a verified personal mailbox); 32 navigator rows (verified T3 business inboxes); 13 rows with a phone; 22 rows with a blank speed column; 59 single-location rows and 39 multi-location rows; 1 CASL row, theniagaraherbalist.com, suppressed at send time (leadsheet columns email_status, contact_name, tier, phone, mobile_lcp_ms, locations_count, canada_flag)

Standard: any card, exact numbers, no hesitation, twin attached wherever the ammo value carries one. A rep who rounds 9,387 to "about nine and a half thousand" passes; a rep who says "over ten thousand" fails the card. A rep who says "94 of 99" and stops has failed the card; "94 of 99, measured 93 of 98" passes.

Drill E: The honesty catechism

Rapid-fire, trainer asks, rep answers from memory:

  1. What does a file cost? Nothing. Ever.
  2. Can the agency pay to change their file? No. "Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included."
  3. Are we affiliated with any agency named? No; quote the boilerplate: "An independent performance analysis of the Rank Really High client roster, not published by or affiliated with Rank Really High." (ammo: rankreallyhigh/book-boilerplate-verbatim)
  4. What happens after the popup booking is submitted? The rep carries it by hand; the endpoint is empty and no automated anything exists.
  5. What URL do we send, and when? The nis-v2 preview address only, and only after a reply, as the first rung of the links ladder. Never a link in a cold email. nobodyissafe.com is dark until the owner wires it.
  6. What may we never send or say? Client emails, phone numbers, owner personal names in any deliverable; anything not traceable to an ammo stat or a named leadsheet column.
  7. What do we sell on the call? Nothing. The call is a free audit, a second opinion on their own row, in two formats of their choice: 15 minutes or an hour. Taking it never means being pitched. Services come up only in the hour, only if the fit is real, and the fit is decided in the first part of that call. No price list exists and the rep quotes no price, ever.
  8. What is 94 of 99? 93 of the 98 clients that were scanned, plus the one that could not be, scored as failing under the ruling. Say both halves, first time, every time.
  9. What is the one client that could not be scanned, and what do we say about it? cann-studio.com. No storefront listing could be resolved, so no grid was ever run. It is not a failed scan; nothing was scanned, and the book says so on the row.
  10. What is RRH-146? A docket number. The book is 99 clients, 99 sites, 280 storefronts.
  11. When does outreach stop? When they say stop, in any words, which is instant and permanent, or when they book. Never before, and never announced.
  12. What is the llms.txt line? A presence check: 99 of 99 sites lack the file. Never pitched as invisibility to AI.
  13. Which figure is reply-stage only? The double fail, 93 of 99 clients invisible everywhere and failing product, measured 73 of 98. Never in cold copy, never beside the storefront headline.
5. The objective and aggressiveness

This module is the why behind every drill in this document. A rep who can pull any receipt in 60 seconds but cannot say what the receipt is for, or who softens the ask because the receipt felt harsh, is not certified.

5.1 What the desk is for

The owner's words, 2026-08-26: the arsenal exists to arm the sales people "to go ruthlessly to acquire this business", and the outreach exists "to get appointments booked to get them to leave RRH because they are paying for something that is holding their business back and we have the solution."

Three sentences follow from that and every rep can recite them:

  1. The rep's product is a booked free audit. Not a warm conversation, not a link opened, not a promise to think about it. A 15-minute or 1-hour audit, booked, in the ledger.
  2. The desk's win is a client leaving RankReallyHigh for a fix that works, when the fit is real. Fit is decided in the first part of the hour. It is never assumed and never forced; we do not work with everyone.
  3. The audit is the prospect's benefit, not ours. It is free, it is honest, it concedes what the file concedes, and taking it never means being pitched.

5.2 What ruthless means here, and what it never means

Ruthless is two things and only two:

  • Pursuit. The cadence does not stop, does not thin out, and never announces its own end. Every touch carries a new receipt. A silent contact gets the full 90-day ladder; a contact who replies gets the aggressive daily sequence until they book or say stop. The rep never offers to leave.
  • Prosecution. RankReallyHigh is on trial, never the reader. Every touch carries whole-book evidence; the reader's own row is proof of the pattern, never the reader's deficiency. The frame is "this is what they sold you, same as the other 98", not "your site is bad". The reader is being taken advantage of, and the culprit is the agency they hired; the rep is ruthlessly forward and direct about exactly that.

Ruthless never means rudeness, insults, editorializing about the people at the agency, threats, invented numbers, or a link nobody asked for. The register is evidence, not opinion. Say the owner's line when the prosecution lands hard: "this is not opinion and it is not a personal attack on anyone, it just is what it is; once you see the data you cannot unsee it."

The register test for any sentence a prospect hears: it is a measurement with a source, the plain-English meaning of a measurement (money, customers, pride), an honest concession, or the ask. Anything else is cut.

The owner's model line, the shape every whole-book sentence takes (one number, the rest plain English): "Your business isn't the problem, it's RRH, they are failing almost every single client they manage, and we've got the data to prove it: all 280 storefronts they have under service fully audited and almost the entire book is failing online." (ammo: rankreallyhigh/book-scope-storefronts, headline-storefronts-invisible)

5.3 The two-ways offer, word-perfect

The call this desk offers has one name, a free audit. The word the desk retired on 2026-08-21 (the paid-advice-call word every other agency uses) does not exist here: not in an email, not on a call, not in a script, not in a note, and a rep who says it is reading a stale page. The call is a free audit, a second opinion, in two formats of the prospect's choice: a 15-minute quick audit, or a 1-hour full audit that includes education and a presentation of our services only if the fit is real, decided in the first part of the call (ammo: rankreallyhigh/funnel.offer).

The close, in the owner's shape, is recited word-perfect at certification:

"We can do this two ways. If you want the quick version, I can show you in 15 minutes every way RankReallyHigh is failing you. If you've got more time, I can tell you more about us, why we're auditing other agencies' entire books of business, and how we can help fix the problems RRH is charging you for."

In an email the offer ends on one word: "Yes?" In an email the fifteen is written out in words so the email's one number belongs to the knife (section 5.5). Every close is this door. Not a different door for a skeptic, not a softer door for a friendly one, not a link instead of the ask.

5.4 The never-invite-go-away rule

  • No opt-out invitations in sales prose, ever. "Say so once and I'll disappear" is inviting the reader to kick rocks, and it is weak salesmanship. The legal minimum lives in one place, the muted signature line {{sender.optout_line}}, and nowhere else.
  • No finality, ever. No "last one from me", no breakup email, no "closing the file", no walkaway framing. The door stays open and the ask stays live at day 89. Follow-ups are continuing personal outreach and may openly count the touches before them.
  • Two stops, and only two. STOP, in any words that mean stop, is instant and permanent suppression; the rep never argues with it and never sends after it. BOOKED ends the sequence and starts the ledger. Nothing else stops a sequence: not silence, not a bounce-back, not a rep's discomfort. The one CASL row, theniagaraherbalist.com, is suppressed at send time and never enters a sequence (leadsheet canada_flag).
  • No preemptive rebuttals in cold copy. "In case RankReallyHigh told you the scan is broken" before they have seen anything is nonsense on the page. Rebuttals live in reply handling, after evidence has been shown.
  • Whole book first. Emails 1 and 2 of the ladder carry the agency's whole book failing and no per-row claim; they go to every contactable row identically. Email 3 narrows to the reader's own row. The knife drill below runs on the email-3 shape and on the call.

5.5 The four emotional triggers and the receipt that fires each

The meaning law names three things a number can mean to a dispensary owner: money, customers, pride. The approved voice adds the fourth lever, the curiosity gap: you are on a public list, the stats are ugly, want to see? Those four are the triggers; every savage true line fires exactly one of them, and every trigger has a receipt behind it. (This table is derived from the meaning law and the voice sample; it is the desk's working reading, not an owner list.)

TriggerWhat the reader feelsThe receipt that fires itThe plain-English form (one number at most, the rest words)
MoneyI am paying every month for nothing275 of 280 storefronts invisible (ammo: rankreallyhigh/headline-storefronts-invisible); 98 of 99 sites fail a majority of the product checks, measured 78 of 99 (ammo: rankreallyhigh/product-failing-verdict); the row's own product_verdict and product_fail_count"You did not get bad results. You got no results, on an invoice." The owner's model line in 5.2.
CustomersSomeone else is serving the people searching from my blockthe row's grid_leader and grid_leader_coverage_pct, backed by its competitor report; median 9.2 seconds on a phone (ammo: rankreallyhigh/speed-median-load); customers reach for weed roughly twice as often as dispensary, every week of the year (ammo: rankreallyhigh/trends-weed-average, trends-dispensary-average, trends-weed-wins-every-week)"The record names who collects the customers searching from your own neighborhood, and how much of your map they hold." "A phone customer stares at a blank screen for nine seconds; most are gone at three, into whichever dispensary painted first."
PrideI earned the stars; they wasted themthe row's google_rating and google_reviews (never both in one email); Puff Cannabis, 4.9 stars, 7,746 reviews, 0 pins (ammo: rankreallyhigh/pattern-reviews-do-not-rescue); a lawn-care franchise leads 11 grids (ammo: rankreallyhigh/grid-leader-weed-man-lawn-care); a spoofed spam listing leads 10 (ammo: rankreallyhigh/grid-leader-spoofed-listing)"Your customers gave you the stars. Your agency gave them a storefront the map never shows." "The business beating licensed dispensaries on the weed search mows lawns."
CuriosityI am on a public list and I have not seen my pagethe row's own listing page, described in text cold and delivered on reply as rung 1 of the links ladder; the five that rank, by name, and the reader's name is not among them (ammo: rankreallyhigh/the-only-five-found); the identical-sites list, 96 of 99 on one build (ammo: rankreallyhigh/template-family-01)"You are not going to like your page." "I can send you the list of sites literally identical to yours, same theme, same broken stats, and RRH doesn't care."

Two rules ride on the table. Raw metrics never fire a trigger: milliseconds, PageSpeed scores and index values stay in the receipts and their translations are mandatory in the sentence. And the double fail (93 of 99 clients invisible everywhere and failing product, measured 73 of 98) fires nothing cold; it is reply-stage ammunition only (ammo: rankreallyhigh/crossover-invisible-and-failing).

5.6 The escalation ladder

The playbook's aggressiveness ladder (sales/PLAYBOOK.md, Posture A) has four levels, and every rep climbs them in order on every conversation. The campaign file Marketing/rankreallyhigh-roster/outreach/nis-rrh-blitz-campaign.md carries the words for the email side, and the pain index beside it (PAIN-INDEX.md) is the backbone the ladder is generated from. The shape every rep knows cold:

Level 1, first contact: the file and the one-line knife. Whole book only; no per-row claim yet. Emails 1 and 2 go to every contactable row identically; the row narrows in at email 3. Name or business name first, RankReallyHigh named, one number at most, the owner's model line from 5.2, then the door. Never at level 1: an opt-out invitation, a preemptive rebuttal, a link, an attachment, a raw metric, a second number.

Level 2, the receipt in front of them. Narrow to their row. On a call this is the pull (Drill A); in email it is the third touch onward. Each touch adds one NEW receipt, never a repeat, in the five-chapter order the silent ladder runs over 90 days: 39 emails and 8 call slots; 3 emails a week rising to 4 a week late; one send per day at most to a silent contact; a fresh subject per chapter and real threading inside a chapter; calls on the 13 rows that carry a phone, on non-email days.

  1. You are in the file (week 1). Whole book only in emails 1 and 2: every client RankReallyHigh has was audited and almost the entire book is failing. Email 3 narrows to the reader's row.
  2. The website they sold you (from week 2, by owner law). The seconds of blank screen in the reader's own number, then the owner's identical-sites line, then the invitation to run Google's public speed tool themselves.
  3. The phrase flip (weeks 4 and 5). The two doors; which half of the agency's book holds the reader; the five, named, and the fact that all five still fail product.
  4. Who is beating you, by name (weeks 6 to 8). Their own map's leader with coverage; the chains at zero and the 39 of 39 knife for multi-location rows; the lawn company; the spoofed listing; Puff and the reviews that could not buy a pin.
  5. The pattern, the desk, and the open door (weeks 9 to 13). The demand was public; who publishes the file and why, the anchor verbatim, files free, nobody named can pay to change one; the two-ways offer in the owner's shape; the reader's own row read back in the book's sentence; and at day 89 the door still open and the ask still live.

Level 3, the direct ask, twice, two formats. The two-ways shape from 5.3, verbatim. Ask. Handle whatever comes back with the receipts (Drill G). Ask again, both formats, "Yes?". A conversation that ends without the ask made twice is a rep failure, not a prospect decision.

Level 4, persistence. Any reply flips the row: any human reply, any intent except STOP (suppresses) and an out-of-office robot (pauses), moves the contact the same day out of the chapter schedule and into the aggressive daily sequence: 2 to 5 touches a day, email plus calls, until BOOKED or STOP. Links become legal only now, and only up the ladder: rung 1 their own listing page, rung 2 their row screenshot, rung 3 the third-party competitor and scan reports, rung 4 the booking link. Never a shortener, never a tracking domain, never an attachment before a reply.

Navigator rows route, they do not convert. On the 32 verified T3 business inboxes the sequence's one job is putting the file on the decision maker's desk: every chapter opener carries the forward-this line ("if marketing is not your desk, forward this to whoever signs that invoice"), the co-caller phones in parallel asking who runs marketing, and the moment a name or a direct mailbox lands in the sheet the row flips to the armed treatment at the next chapter boundary, first-name knife included. Personal-inbox rows never see the forward line.

Past the rep. A price pushed twice in writing goes to a human decision; the rep never quotes one. Legal noise or a regulatory threat goes to the owner immediately, sends paused. The measured agency itself, or anyone speaking for it, gets the registrar posture and the owner; anything past "here is the receipt" is not the rep's to say.

Drill F: The 60-second knife

The trainer names a leadsheet row and reads its columns aloud: business_name, locations_count, segment, grid_leader with grid_leader_coverage_pct, google_rating and google_reviews, mobile_lcp_ms, dispensary_standing, product_verdict with product_fail_count, tier and whether contact_name is filled. The rep has 60 seconds to deliver, aloud or typed, in the approved voice:

  1. Name-first on an armed row (spoken as the merge token {{contact.contact_first}}; personal names never appear in a training transcript), business-name-first on an unarmed row.
  2. RankReallyHigh named as the villain.
  3. ONE savage true line weaponized to the row, firing exactly one trigger from 5.5, carrying at most one number, everything else plain English.
  4. The two-ways ask, word-perfect, ending "Yes?"

Then the trainer asks "says who?" and the rep names the column or the stat id the line rests on.

Truth-gated swaps the rep must apply without being told:

  • A FOUND row (5 of 99) gets the concession form: the reader ranks, and all five that rank still fail product; the rank survives the website (ammo: rankreallyhigh/the-only-five-found, rankers-still-failing-product).
  • The never-scanned row, cann-studio.com, gets the honest form: not ranked low, not there, nothing solid enough to measure, and the book says so; never a scan failure (ammo: rankreallyhigh/client-never-scanned-honest-line).
  • The self-lead row, quincycannabis.co, never casts its own listing as the rival; its grid_leader is itself.
  • A blank mobile_lcp_ms holds the speed line entirely; no seconds are invented.
  • A single-location row (59 of 99) gets singular phrasing; a multi-location row (39 of 99) may carry the 39 of 39 knife.
  • A crawl-blocked row (shop.starbuds.us, thrivedispensaries.com) carries no on-page line from the crawl; the map line and the speed line, where the column is filled, still stand.

Hard fails, any one of them: a raw metric in a sentence (milliseconds, a PageSpeed score, an index value); two numbers; an opt-out invitation; a preemptive rebuttal; a link offered cold; the retired word for the call (anything but "audit"); a phrase the book did not measure presented as measured; a personal name in the transcript; a swap missed.

Worked examples, business names and public measured results only, every figure read from the named column this turn:

  • Sunburn Cannabis Pensacola Dispensary (armed T1 row; locations_count 14; grid_leader TEL Staffing & HR at 91.7 percent; weed_invisible_all_markets true). Trigger: money. "{{contact.contact_first}}, Sunburn is in the file. NobodyIsSafe audited every client RankReallyHigh has, and you are on the list. Your line: across every storefront you run, the business that can be found on the weed search from your own block is a staffing agency. You did not get bad results. You got no results, on an invoice. We can do this two ways. If you want the quick version, I can show you in fifteen minutes every way RankReallyHigh is failing you. If you've got more time, I can tell you more about us, why we're auditing other agencies' entire books of business, and how we can help fix the problems RRH is charging you for. Yes?" Says who: grid_leader, weed_invisible_all_markets. The one number is none; that is legal.
  • Floyd's Cannabis: Pullman (unarmed; locations_count 5; mobile_lcp_ms 12,931, rendered 12.9 seconds; grid_leader Happy Time Weed Dispensary Pullman at 69.4 percent). Trigger: customers. "Floyd's Cannabis is in the file. NobodyIsSafe audited every client RankReallyHigh has, and whoever signs the marketing invoice at Floyd's needs to see the row. One line: on a phone, your site keeps a customer staring at a blank screen for 12.9 seconds. Most of them are gone at three, into whichever dispensary painted first. That is not your staff and it is not your stores; that is the website RankReallyHigh sold you, same as nearly every client they have. We can do this two ways ... Yes?" Says who: mobile_lcp_ms; the "same as nearly every client" clause rests on 98 of 99 over the bar, measured 76 of 77 (ammo: rankreallyhigh/speed-lcp-over-google-bar).
  • Puff Cannabis: Monroe (unarmed; locations_count 10; google_rating 4.9; google_reviews 7,746; weed_best_found_pins 0; mobile_lcp_ms blank, so the speed line holds). Trigger: pride. "Puff Cannabis is in the file. 7,746 people gave you the stars. Your agency gave them ten storefronts the weed search cannot find, not one of them, not from a single spot we measured. That is not your reputation failing; that is what RankReallyHigh sold you. We can do this two ways ... Yes?" Says who: google_reviews, locations_count, weed_best_found_pins (ammo: rankreallyhigh/pattern-reviews-do-not-rescue). The rating stays out; one raw payload per email.
  • Quincy Cannabis Co (FOUND, self-lead; weed_best_found_pins 115; product_verdict FAILING, product_fail_count 4). Concession form. "Quincy Cannabis Co is in the file, and yours is one of the five rows in the whole book that the weed search can find. Read the rest of the row before you frame that: the website RankReallyHigh sold you fails 4 of the six checks we run, and not one of the five that rank passes. Your rank is surviving your website, not being produced by it. We can do this two ways ... Yes?" Says who: segment, product_fail_count (ammo: rankreallyhigh/the-only-five-found, rankers-still-failing-product). The grid leader is never named as a rival on this row.
  • cann-studio.com (NOT_MEASURED; on_shared_template true). Honest form. "cann-studio.com is in the file. Not ranked low. Not there: the scan looked for a storefront on the map and found nothing solid enough to measure, and the book says exactly that on your row instead of guessing. What it could measure was the website RankReallyHigh sold you, and it is the same site nearly every other client got with the logo swapped. We can do this two ways ... Yes?" Says who: segment, data_gaps, on_shared_template (ammo: rankreallyhigh/client-never-scanned-honest-line, template-family-01). No number at all; legal.
  • Star Buds IL: Burbank (unarmed; locations_count 17; weed_invisible_all_markets true; crawl_blocked_403 true, so no on-page line). Trigger: money, multi-location knife. "Star Buds is in the file. Seventeen storefronts, and the weed search cannot find one of them from its own block. Every chain in RankReallyHigh's book came back the same way, 39 of 39; size bought nothing. We can do this two ways ... Yes?" Says who: locations_count, weed_invisible_all_markets (ammo: rankreallyhigh/multi-location-all-invisible).
  • A navigator row (verified T3 business inbox, no name). Same knife, business-name-first, plus the forward line before the ask: "If marketing is not your desk, forward this to whoever signs that invoice."

Par: 60 seconds, all four parts, one trigger, one number at most, "says who?" answered. Run daily on three rows drawn at random from the leadsheet; the trainer includes a swap row at least once a week.

Drill G: Objection-to-ask

Every objection ends in the ask. The rep answers from the file in at most three sentences, never argues past the map, never quotes a price, never invites the prospect to go away, and lands the two-ways door. In a live conversation the ask is made twice, both formats: ask, handle, ask again (playbook ladder, level 3); a conversation that ends after one ask is a rep failure. The trainer fires these in random order; a rep who ends an answer anywhere but the ask fails the attempt.

ObjectionThe receiptThe shape, ending in the ask
"Rankings fluctuate."275 of 280 storefronts, 33,880 measured points (ammo: rankreallyhigh/headline-storefronts-invisible, book-scope-map-points)Fluctuation moves a 3 to a 5; it does not erase 275 of 280 storefronts from every point we measured. I can show you your own grid in fifteen minutes. Two ways ... yes?
"We rank fine on dispensary near me."the flip (ammo: rankreallyhigh/dispensary-top3, headline-storefronts-found, trends-note-verbatim)Same storefronts, same place IDs, two phrases: their book does half decent on the phrase they chose and 5 of 280 on the one your customers type, which draws roughly twice the search interest every week of the year. They optimized the smaller door and billed you for the building. Two ways ... yes?
"Our site is custom."96 of 99 on one build (ammo: rankreallyhigh/template-family-01)It is one of 96 in their book on the same theme with the logo swapped. I can send you the list of sites literally identical to yours, same theme, same broken stats, and RRH doesn't care. Two ways ... yes?
"RankReallyHigh says your scan is broken."560 third-party scan reports, both phrases (ammo: rankreallyhigh/receipts-scan-reports, flip-same-storefronts-both-phrases)A broken scan cannot rank you on one phrase and erase you on the other; every scan is a third-party page you can open yourself. Let me open yours with you. Two ways ... yes?
"Send me the link."rung 1 of the links ladder, legal on a replySent: your own page, nothing else. It is one row of the whole book, and the rest is what the fifteen minutes is for. Two ways ... yes?
"What does it cost?"the offer (ammo: rankreallyhigh/funnel.offer)The audit is free and there is no price list; whether we are even a fit is decided in the first part of the hour, and if we are not, you still leave with the whole data set. Two ways ... yes? (Pushed twice in writing: escalate to a human; the rep never quotes a number.)
"We're under contract."the row's product_verdict and the map lineThe audit costs nothing and binds nothing, and knowing exactly what that contract is buying is the whole point of it. Two ways ... yes?
"Not interested."one new receipt from the ladderOn a call: one new receipt, then the ask, once; a second no is a stop and the rep honors it. In writing: a decline is a stop, classified STOP by the reply system and suppressed the same day; the rep never argues with a stop.
"Who are you? Why trust this?"the anchor and the desk (ammo: rankreallyhigh/integrity-anchor-verbatim, integrity-independence-verbatim, hub-businesses, hub-books)Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included; the files are free, nobody named in one can pay to change it, and yours is one row of 906 businesses across three agency books measured with one instrument. Two ways ... yes?
"I'll pass it to our marketing person."the navigator objectivePerfect; who is that, and what is the best way to reach them directly? I will send the row to them today. Two ways, for whichever of you takes it ... yes?
"One of your five is a client, so it works."the five, and 0 of 99 with both (ammo: rankreallyhigh/the-only-five-found, rank-and-pass-zero)The file names all five, and all five still run a failing or broken website; one name out of 280 storefronts is not a contradiction, it is the base rate. Two ways ... yes?
"Our reviews bring customers in."Puff Cannabis (ammo: rankreallyhigh/pattern-reviews-do-not-rescue)Puff Cannabis holds 4.9 stars across thousands of reviews and appears at zero points on every one of its maps; the stars are yours, the map is what RankReallyHigh was paid for. Two ways ... yes?
"The site loads fine for me."the live re-run, and the median (ammo: rankreallyhigh/speed-median-load)Run Google's public speed tool with me, sixty seconds, your phone view; the typical site in their book is 9.2 seconds against a bar of about two and a half, and if your run beats the file, the file gets corrected. Two ways ... yes?

Par: ten objections in a row, each ending in the ask, zero prices, zero go-away invitations, zero arguments past the map, and the call named as an audit every time.

6. The role-play ladder

Run after Drills A to G reach par. Trainer plays the counterpart; scripts and objection banks live in sales/agencies/<slug>/call-scripts.md and sales/agencies/<slug>/rebuttals.md; the arming questions live in sales/agencies/<slug>/fence-sitter-questions.md. Every level is recorded (audio or transcript) and scored against three axes: motion (did the rep follow the PLAYBOOK motion, climb its four-level aggressiveness ladder, and make the two-ways ask twice), truth (zero untraceable claims, twins attached), and register (evidence, not opinion; ruthless in pursuit, never rude; no go-away invitation).

  • L1, the curious fence-sitter. Friendly client of a measured agency, mostly listens. Pass: rep pulls the file live, reads it calmly, names RankReallyHigh as the one on trial, makes the two-ways offer word-perfect, promises nothing that does not exist.
  • L2, the skeptical owner. "Says who?" on every number. Pass: every challenged number answered with its receipt and its twin where it carries one; no filler, no retreat, no inflation.
