A real answer looks like: a number of seconds, read out loud, next to Google's two-and-a-half-second bar, with a target and a date attached. Field data is a fair instrument too, so if that is their answer, hold them to the same standard: your site's field numbers, on screen, on the record. One thing does not move either way: 98 of 99 sites on this roster fail the same test in the same direction. A methodology quibble explains one site's number. It does not explain a fleet. Refusing to time a site you pay them to maintain is itself the answer.

14. "Search my menu from Google and show me one of my product pages appearing."

The receipt: on 54 of 99 client sites a search engine following links never reaches a single product page, 52 of the 96 menus that could be read. On those 52, the product cards are drawn in by script inside boxes that are not links, so Google never sees a product. Where a product page is reachable at all, the typical site exposes 12. Of 14,060 menu pages in the book, 752 clear the five basics: indexable, own title, own description, a heading and 200 words. The typical menu page carries 26 words. 1 of 99 homepages tells Google what the store sells, and in a live probe of 10 real menu pages, 0 tagged a product. [zero-products-fault] [zero-products-mechanism] [product-pages-median] [menu-pages-total] [menu-pass-basics] [menu-word-median] [schema-homepage-product-tagging] [schema-menu-probe]

The predicted dodge: "the menu is powered by the menu platform, that's separate from SEO", or a screenshot of your menu as it looks to a human, which is not the question.

A real answer looks like: a Google search for one of your products by name and your store, landing on a page of your own site that shows that product. If the menu they built cannot produce that once, your products exist only for customers who already found you. That is the product you pay for every month.

15. "Send me the list of your other clients whose site is identical to mine: same theme, same plugin version, same broken numbers."

The receipt: 96 of 99 client sites are one build, "weedpress", with the logo swapped; 92 sites sit on the exact same plugin version, 1.38.1; 95 of 99 sites repeat one page title across their own pages, 93 of 96 crawled, and the worst case is one title on 275 pages of a single site. The desk's own offer on this, in plain words: we can send you the list of sites literally identical to yours, same theme, same broken stats, and RankReallyHigh does not care. [template-family-01] [template-theme-weedpress] [plugin-version-cohort] [onpage-duplicate-title-sites] [onpage-worst-title-repeat]

The predicted dodge: "every site is customized to the brand", or "we can't share other clients' information", said about sites that are public and can be opened by anyone.

A real answer looks like: the list, or an admission that there is one. The sites are public. If they will not name them, open three yourself: the theme name is in the page source and the layout is the same with the colors changed. Then ask what, specifically, you paid for that the rest of the book did not.

What to do with what you hear

Count the dodges. Every question above has a real answer that fits in one breath and points at something you can see: a map, a version number, a stopwatch, a page count, a line item, a date. If what you got instead was dashboards, bundles, and "give it time", your agency has answered a different question: whether staying is worth what you pay them.

Your file does not move either way. It is free, it is public, and nobody named in it can pay to change it. The headline off the page: 275 of 280 RankReallyHigh storefronts invisible on the search their customers actually type. [headline-storefronts-invisible]

The second opinion on your own page is free, and we can do it two ways. If you want the quick version, 15 minutes shows you every way RankReallyHigh is failing you. If you have more time, an hour adds who we are, why we audit other agencies' entire books of business, and how the problems they are charging you for get fixed, with our services on the table only if the first part shows a real fit for both sides. It is booked on a call, person to person, with the desk contact who gave you this list, at the time you set with them. No forms. Just the call.

Rebuttals

RankReallyHigh rebuttals

Fifteen objections a RankReallyHigh client will actually raise, on a cold call, an inbound call, or the free audit. Each entry carries the scripted rebuttal, the ammo stat behind it, the live-receipt move (what to open on the call so the prospect reads the answer off a page instead of taking your word), and the ask, because every rebuttal on this desk ends at the same door: the free audit, 15 minutes or an hour.

Rebuttals are reply-stage material. They are never sent cold and never volunteered before an objection exists. Once the objection is on the table, the rep answers it in full, with the receipt, and asks.

Every number traces to a stat id in sales/_ammo/rankreallyhigh.json or to a named column of the prospect's own leadsheet row. The standing live-receipt move for this agency is always available: open THE PROSPECT'S OWN page at l/rankreallyhigh/<domain>.html, then the competitor report linked from it, pattern https://www.localrankingtracker.com/competitor-report/{gridId}/fe26e61e3a458c7/. The book publishes 98 of these, one per scanned client, and 560 scan reports, one per storefront on each of the two searches, all under one scan tag. [receipts-competitor-reports] [receipts-scan-reports] [receipts-scan-tag]

Register discipline: evidence, not opinion. Never editorialize RankReallyHigh as bad people; present the measurement and let it stand: "This is not opinion and it is not a personal attack on anyone, it just is what it is. Once you see the data you cannot unsee it." Concede what the file concedes (objections 9, 10, 12 and 14 carry deliberate concessions). A rebuttal that overclaims loses the room and violates house law. Ruled figures are said with their measured twin the first time they appear ("98 of 99 sites; 76 of the 77 that returned a load time").

Plain English in the prospect's ear: "spots", not pins; "the map around your store", not the grid; "the search your customers actually type" or "the weed search", not the quoted phrase every sentence; seconds, not milliseconds. The raw form stays in the receipt line.

1. "My agency says your data is wrong. The scan is broken."

Rebuttal:

Then RankReallyHigh has to explain one thing. The same 280 storefronts, the same Google listings, were scanned on two searches. On "dispensary near me", the phrase they built for, 139 of 280 storefronts crack the top 3. On the search your customers actually type, 5 of 280 show up at all. A broken scan cannot rank you on one phrase and erase you on the other. Same spots, same tool, same listings. The flip is the phrase, not an error.
If their next move is a mechanism, that the weed results reward stuffing "weed" into the listing name and a clean listing cannot compete there, the winner list already answers it. Two of the five storefronts that do rank, Roots LA and Roslindale Cannabis, are found at all 121 of their 121 spots, and the names printed on their rows carry no "weed" at all. The search can be ranked clean. Their own two clients prove it; the other 275 storefronts got neither.

Receipt: overlap 280, same place IDs true; 139 of 280 top-3 on the smaller phrase vs 5 of 280 found on "weed near me"; Roots LA 121, Roslindale Cannabis 121. [flip-same-storefronts-both-phrases] [dispensary-top3] [headline-storefronts-found] [the-only-five-found]

Live move: open the prospect's two scan reports side by side, one per search. Let them watch their own listing appear on one map and vanish on the other. [receipts-scan-reports]

The ask: "Fifteen minutes and I show you both maps for your own store, side by side, free. Or take the hour. Which one, and what day?"

2. "Your data is stale. Rankings fluctuate. That was one day."

Rebuttal:

Fluctuation moves a rank 3 to a rank 5. It does not erase a storefront from all 121 spots of its own neighborhood, and it did that to 275 of 280 storefronts across 33,880 measured spots. If that is a fluctuation, ask RankReallyHigh to name the day the book looked different and show you the map from that day.
The file is dated on its face: filed 2026-08-09, amended 2026-08-26 when it was rebuilt on their active clients. Two dated readings, same result. And every scan link stays open. You can reopen yours tomorrow, next month, or the day their contract renews.

Receipt: 275 of 280 invisible across 33,880 map points; filed 2026-08-09, amended 2026-08-26; 560 scan reports and 98 competitor reports open, one scan tag. [headline-storefronts-invisible] [book-scope-map-points] [book-filed] [receipts-scan-reports] [receipts-competitor-reports] [receipts-scan-tag]

Live move: open the prospect's competitor report and point at the date on the third-party page, then at their empty map. A stale claim dies against a timestamped instrument they can reopen tomorrow.

The ask: "The audit re-runs your numbers live, in front of you, on the day you pick. Fifteen minutes or an hour. What day?"

3. "They do more than maps. SEO is holistic: the website, the content, everything."

Rebuttal:

Good. Then judge the everything. The website: 98 of 99 client sites fail most of the checks they were measured on, 78 of 99 on the checks that returned a reading. The "custom" build: 96 of 99 sites are one WordPress theme, "weedpress". The stack: 95 of 99 run the same menu plugin and 92 of them sit on the exact same version, 1.38.1. The content: we read 24,229 pages across the 96 sites that let us in, and 97 of 99 sites serve pages that tell Google not to index them, 95 of the 96 we crawled. Holistic is a claim about breadth. The file measured the breadth. It fails on every front it touches.

Receipt: 98 of 99 (measured 78/99) failing verdict; 96 of 99 family-01; weedpress on 96; 95 of 99 joint-ecommerce, 92 on 1.38.1; 24,229 pages on 96 sites; 97 of 99 (measured 95/96) nonindexable. [product-failing-verdict] [template-family-01] [template-theme-weedpress] [plugin-joint-ecommerce] [plugin-version-cohort] [onpage-pages-crawled] [onpage-nonindexable-sites]

Live move: open the prospect's page for the map front, then their row's website lines for the site front. Two fronts, one card, both measured.

The ask: "Every front, on your own row, in 15 minutes. Or the hour, where I also show you what a fix looks like. Which one?"

4. "We DO rank. My agency shows me a report where we're number one."

Rebuttal:

That report is probably real. Here is what it is probably measuring. On "dispensary near me", 67 of 280 storefronts in RankReallyHigh's book hit number one somewhere on their own map. On the search your customers actually type, the one Google says carries about twice the demand, 5 of 280 storefronts show up at all, and a client leads its own map exactly once in the entire book. Your number one exists on the smaller door. Ask which phrase the report is built on, and where on the map the number one actually holds.

Receipt: 67 of 280 rank-one on the smaller phrase; 5 of 280 found on "weed near me"; a client leads 1 map; weed 66 vs dispensary 35 on the interest index. [dispensary-rank-one] [headline-storefronts-found] [grid-leader-client-self] [trends-weed-average] [trends-dispensary-average]

Live move: open their competitor report on the weed search next to whatever ranking report RankReallyHigh sent them. Same store, two instruments. One of them shows who actually holds their block.

The ask: "Bring their report to the audit. I put it next to the map, live, and you decide which one your customers are using. Fifteen minutes or an hour?"

5. "Nobody searches 'weed near me'. Dispensary is the real term."

Rebuttal:

Google's own data says otherwise, every single week for the past year, not one exception. Customers type "weed" about twice as often as "dispensary", and "cannabis" trails both. The biggest week of the year was 2026-04-19, and that week the gap was widest. The book's note reads: "weed draws roughly twice the search interest of dispensary, the agency optimized the smaller term."
If the smarter version of this objection arrives, that Google Trends indexes the word "weed", not the phrase, and an index is not search volume: correct, and the file cites it as exactly that, Google's published term-level interest, source printed. Then hold the question. Ask RankReallyHigh for their phrase-level volumes, from their own tool, in writing, next to the weed search. And park the volume debate against the map result that does not depend on it: on the phrase they did build for, 141 of 280 storefronts sit at rank 4 or worse. Winning a volume argument does not rank the losing half of their own book.

Receipt: weed 66 / dispensary 35 / cannabis 21; weed wins 53 of 53 weekly points; peak 100 on 2026-04-19 with dispensary at 42; verbatim trends note; 141 of 280 beaten on the smaller phrase. [trends-weed-average] [trends-dispensary-average] [trends-cannabis-average] [trends-weed-wins-every-week] [trends-peak-week] [trends-note-verbatim] [dispensary-beaten]

Live move: open the book's demand exhibit on the call, then their competitor report. Demand on one screen, their absence from it on the other.

The ask: "The audit shows you the demand and your map on the same screen. Fifteen minutes. Or the hour. What day?"

6. "Switching is painful. We'd lose our website and have to start over."

Rebuttal:

Walk through what you would actually be leaving. A website that is one of 96 on the same theme with the logo swapped. A menu plugin on the exact version that 92 sites in the book share. A site that, on a typical phone, shows nine seconds of blank screen before the main content appears, against Google's bar of two and a half; 35 sites in the book take more than 10 seconds. The pain of switching is real and I will not pretend otherwise. Weigh it against the pain of staying: 93 of the 99 clients on this roster are invisible on the search their customers type AND paying monthly for a website that fails its checks; 73 of the 98 that could be scanned. You are not the exception. You are the pattern.

Receipt: 96 of 99 family-01; 92 sites on 1.38.1; 9.2 s median load (77 measured), 2.5 s bar; 35 sites over 10 s; 93 of 99 (measured 73/98) invisible AND failing. [template-family-01] [plugin-version-cohort] [speed-median-load] [speed-lcp-over-google-bar] [speed-over-10-seconds] [crossover-invisible-and-failing]

Live move: open their competitor report, then their row's website lines. The thing switching would "cost" them is itemized on the card, with its failure count.

The ask: "The audit prices both pains on your own numbers, free. Fifteen minutes or an hour. Which one?"

7. "We're one of their biggest clients. We get real attention."

Rebuttal:

Size bought nothing in this book. The four largest chains, 15 to 18 storefronts each, were scanned at every storefront they run and not one of those storefronts is found on the weed search: FlynnStoned with 18, Nirvana MI with 18, Star Buds IL with 17, Urban Wellness with 15. The two largest were scanned at all 36 of their storefronts and found at none. Widen it: every one of the 39 clients that runs two or more storefronts is invisible at every single one of them. The typical client in the book runs 1 storefront, the largest runs 18, and the file already contains your size class at zero. Whatever the biggest clients are getting, it is not the map.

Receipt: 4 chains, 15 to 18 storefronts each, 0 found; 36 storefronts, 0 found; 39 of 39 multi-location clients invisible everywhere; median 1 storefront, largest 18. [chains-largest-four-nowhere] [chains-two-largest-zero] [multi-location-all-invisible] [roster-median-storefronts] [roster-largest-client]

Live move: open the competitor report for one of the prospect's own storefronts. If they are a chain, offer to open a second and a third; the pattern holds at every location.

The ask: "Every one of your storefronts, on the map, in the audit. The hour if you run more than a couple. Which day?"

8. "Customers find us anyway. We're busy."

Rebuttal:

Busy is not the same as not losing. The customers the file counts are the ones you never see: the people who typed the weed search from your own block and went where the map sent them. Somebody is collecting them, and the file prints who. Across the 280 maps, 153 different businesses lead somewhere; on yours it is [grid_leader], holding [grid_leader_coverage_pct] percent of the map around your store. The typical map has 39 competing businesses on it, so this is not an empty market waiting for you. And the search that sends those customers is the bigger one, every week of the year, with the biggest week of the year landing in April. You are busy on the customers who already knew you. RankReallyHigh was hired for the ones who did not.

Receipt: 153 distinct leaders (279 of 280 maps read); the prospect's grid_leader and grid_leader_coverage_pct columns; median 39 competitors; weed wins 53 of 53 weeks; peak 2026-04-19. [grid-leaders-distinct] [dispensary-median-competitors] [trends-weed-wins-every-week] [trends-peak-week]

Live move: open their competitor report and read the leader's name and coverage aloud. The customers they "find anyway" are on that map, going to that name. (Self-lead and name-share gates from the call scripts apply.)

The ask: "Fifteen minutes and I show you exactly who is taking the customers who search from your block, and how many spots they hold. Free. What day?"

9. "You're a competitor. This whole file is a hit piece."

Rebuttal:

We are a competitor, and the file says so in its own boilerplate: "not published by or affiliated with Rank Really High." So do not trust us. Click. Every storefront claim resolves to a localrankingtracker.com report, a third-party instrument, 98 of them, one per scanned client, and 560 scan reports underneath. And a hit piece does not print the other side's wins. The file states that 97 of 99 sites DO carry LocalBusiness schema, the one check RankReallyHigh mostly passes. It prints that 6 of 99 sites pass Google's phone test, 6 of the 82 that got a score. It prints that 46 of 99 sites score 90 or better on accessibility, 46 of the 80 that returned a score. And it names the 5 clients that rank. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included. The files are free, and nobody named in one can pay to change it.

Receipt: verbatim non-affiliation line; 98 competitor reports; 560 scan reports; schema concession 97 of 99; PageSpeed pass 6 of 99 (measured 6/82); accessibility pass 46 of 99 (measured 46/80); the five winners named; the anchor and independence lines. [book-boilerplate-verbatim] [receipts-competitor-reports] [receipts-scan-reports] [schema-localbusiness-present] [psi-pass] [a11y-pass-bar] [the-only-five-found] [integrity-anchor-verbatim] [integrity-independence-verbatim]

Live move: open THEIR competitor report and point out the domain in the address bar: localrankingtracker.com, not our site. The evidence does not live on the accuser's server.

The ask: "Audit us, then. Fifteen minutes, your own row, every link third-party. If a number does not hold, I say so out loud. What day?"

10. "Maps are unwinnable here anyway. It's all spam and franchises."

Rebuttal:

The maps say the opposite: they are winnable, just not by this roster. Across the 280 maps, 153 different businesses hold the top spot somewhere. Someone wins every map. The typical map has 39 competitors on it, and these markets produce a winner every time. Yes, some winners are junk: a spam listing typed in look-alike foreign characters leads 10 maps, and Weed Man Lawn Care, an actual lawn-care franchise, is the top weed-search result on 11 maps, ahead of paying dispensaries. If a lawn company and a fake-character listing can take maps, the barrier is not the market.
Expect the polished version of this objection: "every winner on that search is stuffing 'weed' into its listing name, that violates Google's naming guidelines, and we will not gamble a licensed dispensary's listing on name-spam." Concede what is true: the one RankReallyHigh client that leads its own map does it under a listing named "Quincy Cannabis Co Weed Dispensary", and that listing leads 9 maps. Then open the winner list. Two of the five storefronts that rank, Roots LA and Roslindale Cannabis, are found at all 121 of their 121 spots, and the names printed on their rows carry no "weed" at all. Both are this agency's own clients. Ranking on the customer search did not require gaming the name field; it required work the other 275 storefronts never got.

Receipt: 153 distinct leaders; a client leads 1 map; median 39 competitors; spoofed listing leads 10 maps; Weed Man Lawn Care leads 11 maps and is the named leader on 4 client flagships (Thrive Dispensary Metropolis, Cookies Peoria Heights, Cookies Bloomington, Terrabis - Grayville); Quincy Cannabis Co Weed Dispensary leads 9; Roots LA 121, Roslindale Cannabis 121. [grid-leaders-distinct] [grid-leader-client-self] [dispensary-median-competitors] [grid-leader-spoofed-listing] [grid-leader-weed-man-lawn-care] [grid-leader-weed-man-flagships] [grid-leader-quincy] [the-only-five-found]

Live move: open their competitor report and read out who actually leads their map, by name. Winnability stops being abstract when the winner has a name and a coverage percentage.

The ask: "Your map has a winner. The audit shows you who, and what they did that RankReallyHigh did not. Fifteen minutes or an hour?"

11. "Your sample is too small to judge a whole agency."

Rebuttal:

The file is not a sample. It is the roster: all 99 active clients, 99 websites, 280 storefronts, 33,880 measured spots, every scan on the same search with the map radius sized per market and printed: 1 mile for 33 maps, 2 miles for 51, 5 miles for 140, 10 miles for 55, 15 miles for 1. And inside the file nothing is dropped. The one client whose storefront could not be resolved is printed as never scanned, in the book's own words, rather than quietly removed. A cherry-picked file does not print its own gap.

Receipt: 99 clients, 99 sites; 280 storefronts; 33,880 points; radius table 1mi 33 / 2mi 51 / 5mi 140 / 10mi 55 / 15mi 1; 1 client never scanned (cann-studio.com) with the book's own honest line. [book-scope-clients] [book-scope-storefronts] [book-scope-map-points] [scan-radii] [client-never-scanned] [client-never-scanned-honest-line]

Live move: open the book's verdict table, then the prospect's own competitor report. Whole-roster numbers first, then their personal row inside them.

The ask: "Whole roster, then your row inside it, in 15 minutes. Or the hour. What day?"

12. "The website is just a brochure. The menu is where we sell, and that's a plugin."

Rebuttal:

The menu is exactly what the file measured, because the menu is the product. 95 of 99 client sites run the same menu plugin. On 54 of 99 sites a search engine following links never reaches a single product page, 52 of the 96 menus we could read; on those 52, the product cards are drawn in by script inside boxes that are not links, so Google never sees a product. Where a product page is reachable at all, the typical site exposes 12. Of the 14,060 menu pages in the book, 752 clear five basics at once. The typical menu page carries 26 words: a product name and a price. 1 of 99 homepages tells Google what the store sells, and in a live probe of 10 real menu pages, 0 tagged a product. The concession, because it is true: 78 of 99 sites have at least one menu page that clears the basics, 78 of 96 crawled. One. Your menu serves the customers who already found you. It does not bring one in.

Receipt: 95 of 99 joint-ecommerce; 54 of 99 (measured 52/96) zero products; mechanism on 52 of 96 menus read; median 12 product pages on 44 sites; 752 of 14,060 menu pages; 26 words median; 1 of 99 homepages; 0 of 10 sampled; 78 of 99 (measured 78/96) with a passing page. [plugin-joint-ecommerce] [zero-products-fault] [zero-products-mechanism] [product-pages-median] [menu-pages-total] [menu-pass-basics] [menu-word-median] [schema-homepage-product-tagging] [schema-menu-probe] [menu-sites-with-passing-page]

13. "Our site is custom."

Rebuttal:

Then it is custom in the same way the rest of the book is custom. 96 of 99 RankReallyHigh client sites are one build, template family-01, the WordPress theme "weedpress", with the logo swapped. 95 of 99 run the same menu plugin and 92 of them sit on the exact same version, 1.38.1. That is a fleet, not a bespoke build. The worst case in the crawl is one page title repeated across 275 pages of a single site. I can send you the list of sites literally identical to yours, same theme, same broken stats, and RRH doesn't care.

14. "Our site is fine. I've looked at it."

Rebuttal:

Then let's measure it right now instead of debating it. Google publishes the test. Across this roster, 98 of 99 sites miss Google's two-and-a-half-second bar for showing the main content on a phone, 76 of the 77 that returned a load time; the typical site takes 9.2 seconds, and 35 sites take more than 10. 93 of 99 sites either fail Google's phone test or never painted a screen for it at all, 76 of the 82 that got a score plus 17 that showed Google nothing. On 54 of 99 sites a search engine following links never reaches a product, 52 of the 96 menus we could read. 97 of 99 sites have pages with no description under the blue link, 95 of the 96 we crawled; 4,335 pages. If your site is the exception, the test will say so in 60 seconds and I will say so with it. The file plays it straight: 6 of 99 sites do pass the phone test, 6 of the 82 scored.

Receipt: 98 of 99 (measured 76/77) over the 2.5 s bar; 9.2 s median; 35 sites over 10 s; 93 of 99 (measured 76/82) PageSpeed fail; 17 never painted; 54 of 99 (measured 52/96) zero products; 97 of 99 (measured 95/96) missing description, 4,335 pages; 6 of 99 (measured 6/82) pass. [speed-lcp-over-google-bar] [speed-median-load] [speed-over-10-seconds] [psi-mobile-fail] [psi-never-painted] [zero-products-fault] [onpage-missing-description-sites] [onpage-missing-description-pages] [psi-pass]

Live move: load the prospect's own site on a phone, timed, together, and read the seconds aloud. Then run Google's phone test on their domain and read that aloud. Then open their competitor report so both fronts sit on the table at once.

15. "We have great reviews."

Rebuttal:

The file contains the exact counterexample, on this same roster. Puff Cannabis: 4.9 Google stars, 7,746 reviews, 10 storefronts. It appears at zero spots on the weed search at every single one of them. Its flagship map is led by Uniq Cannabis Weed Dispensary Monroe at 100 percent coverage. A near-perfect rating and thousands of reviews did not buy one spot. Your reviews are real and they are yours. That is exactly why this stings: a business with that reputation should not be invisible, and the reason it is has a name on the invoice.

Receipt: 7,746 reviews, 4.9 stars, 10 stores, 0 pins; flagship leader Uniq Cannabis Weed Dispensary Monroe at 100 (the shoppuff.com row's grid_leader and grid_leader_coverage_pct columns). [pattern-reviews-do-not-rescue]

Live move: open the prospect's own Google rating next to their competitor report. Their stars on one screen, their empty map on the other. Their own google_rating and google_reviews columns give the rep the numbers to read.

The ask: "Your reviews stay exactly where they are either way. The audit shows you why they are not turning into map customers. Fifteen minutes or an hour. What day?"

When a call needs an ending

Say the agency name, read the headline off the page, 275 of 280 storefronts invisible on the search their customers actually type, and stop talking. Then ask the two-ways question one more time. The rep never ends a call on the kicker alone; the rep ends it on a booked time or a named callback day. [headline-storefronts-invisible]

Call scripts

RankReallyHigh call scripts

Desk: Nobody Is Safe. Case RRH-146 (a docket number, not a count), cannabis retail. Book filed 2026-08-09, amended 2026-08-26 when it was rebuilt on the agency's active client set. [book-docket-code] [book-filed]

Scope of the record: 99 clients, 99 sites, 280 storefronts, 33,880 measured map points on one search, "weed near me". [book-scope-clients] [book-scope-storefronts] [book-scope-map-points]

Every number in these scripts traces to a stat id in sales/_ammo/rankreallyhigh.json or to a named column of the prospect's own row in the RankReallyHigh leadsheet. If a number is not in one of those two places, it is not said. Stat ids appear in brackets like [headline-storefronts-invisible] and are indexed in the ledger at the bottom of this file.

What the rep sells, in one paragraph

The rep's product is a booked free audit. Not a sales call, not a pitch: a full second opinion on the prospect's own row, showing the entire data set of why they rank where they rank, locally and in regular search. Two formats, their choice: a 15-minute quick audit, or a 1-hour full audit that adds education and a presentation of our services only if the fit is real, decided in the first part of the call. We do not work with everyone. The prospect gets the benefit of the call either way. The desk's win is the client leaving RankReallyHigh for a fix that works, when the fit is real. The word that used to describe the call is retired from this desk; the offer is called a free audit, a second opinion, a quick audit, a full audit, or an audit call, and nothing else.

Ground rules for every call
  1. Truth. Say only numbers from the ammo sheet or the prospect's own row. Never round up, never improvise. A figure ruled on the 99 base is said with its measured twin the first time it comes up on a call ("98 of 99 sites; 76 of the 77 that returned a load time"). [speed-lcp-over-google-bar]
  2. Units. Storefronts, sites and clients are three different things. 280 storefronts, 99 sites, 99 clients. Never swap one for another. [book-scope-storefronts] [book-scope-clients]
  3. The agency is on trial, never the prospect. Whole-book evidence on every call. The prospect's row is proof of the pattern: "this is what they sold you, same as the other 98." It is never "your site is bad." It is "this is what RankReallyHigh built you and billed you for."
  4. Evidence, not opinion. Never editorialize RankReallyHigh as bad people. Present the measurement and let it stand. The register line, said on every call where the data lands: "This is not opinion and it is not a personal attack on anyone, it just is what it is. Once you see the data you cannot unsee it."
  5. The anchors, verbatim where echoed. "Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included." [integrity-anchor-verbatim] The files are free. Nobody named in a file can pay to change one. [integrity-independence-verbatim] The book's own boilerplate: "not published by or affiliated with Rank Really High." [book-boilerplate-verbatim]
  6. Persistence, never finality. The rep never announces an end, never says "last call", never offers to go away, never says "keep the address and call us." If the audit is not booked, the rep names the day of the callback and calls back on that day. We stop when they say stop. A stop is honored instantly and permanently.
  7. Funnel honesty. The on-site booking form does not yet deliver submissions (its endpoint is owner-owed), so nobody is routed to it. Audits are booked verbally, on the call, both sides write it down, and the rep confirms by hand. Promise only what the rep personally does ("I will call you Thursday"), never an automated confirmation or reminder.
  8. The live surface. The book is served at rankreallyhigh/ and every client has its own page at l/rankreallyhigh/<domain>.html. The book carries noindex by design, so prospects cannot Google their way to it: read the address aloud or they will not find it. nobodyissafe.com is dark; never cite it as reachable.
  9. No PII. Ask for "the owner, or whoever signs the RankReallyHigh invoice." Business names and public measured results only in every note and deliverable. The two rows in the leadsheet that carry a contact name (the armed rows) let the rep greet a person by first name on the call and nowhere else.
  10. Plain English in every spoken line. Say what the data means (money, customers, pride), then offer the data as proof. Raw metrics stay in the rep's notes; their translations go in the prospect's ear:
Rep's notesProspect hears
grid, pins, map pointsthe map around your store; spots on it; 121 spots per store [book-scope-pins-per-grid]
"weed near me" as a measured phrasethe search your customers actually type; the weed search
"dispensary near me"the industry's word; the phrase RankReallyHigh built for; the smaller door
LCP 9,208 msnine seconds of blank screen on a phone; Google's bar is two and a half [speed-median-load] [speed-lcp-over-google-bar]
mobile PageSpeed 31.5 medianhalf the sites Google could score, Google colors red [psi-median-score]
product verdict FAILING, 4 of 6the website they built you fails most of the checks it was measured on
zero_products truea search engine following links never reaches a single product on your site [zero-products-fault]
Trends index 66 vs 35customers type "weed" about twice as often as "dispensary", every week of the past year, not one exception [trends-weed-average] [trends-dispensary-average] [trends-weed-wins-every-week]
Pre-call prep (five minutes, mandatory)
  1. Open the prospect's own page at the per-client address above. Write down from their row: measured verdict (ranking_verdict), storefronts scanned, invisible and found (storefronts_scanned, storefronts_invisible, storefronts_found), who leads their map and how much of it (grid_leader, grid_leader_coverage_pct), the website grade and fail count (product_verdict, product_fail_count), phone load in seconds (mobile_lcp_ms, divide by 1,000; blank means hold the speed line entirely), whether a product page is reachable (zero_products, read zero_products_basis before quoting the mechanism), the theme (template_theme), and the storefront count (locations_count; 1 means singular phrasing throughout).
  2. Open their competitor report in a second tab (the competitor_report_url column; pattern https://www.localrankingtracker.com/competitor-report/{gridId}/fe26e61e3a458c7/). The book publishes 98 of these, one per scanned client. [receipts-competitor-reports] [receipts-scan-tag]
  3. Pick the READ branch by measured verdict. Across the 99: NOWHERE 93, PACK_WITH_TURF 5, NOT_MEASURED 1 (the published verdict scores the never-scanned client NOWHERE under the 99-base ruling, so the published split is 94 and 5). [clients-invisible-everywhere] [clients-ranking-somewhere] [client-never-scanned]
  4. Truth-gated swaps, checked before dialing:
  • Self-lead row (quincycannabis.co): its own listing leads its map. Never cast that listing as the rival. [grid-leader-client-self]
  • Leader shares the client's name (Nile Cannabis's map is led by a listing named Nile Dispensary & Weed Delivery): do not cast it as a rival; say the file prints which listing leads and let the prospect say whether it is theirs.
  • Blank speed column (mobile_lcp_ms empty): the speed line is not said to that prospect. The book-level speed lines still stand.
  • Crawl-blocked rows (shop.starbuds.us, thrivedispensaries.com; crawl_blocked_403 true): their crawl columns are blank because the site refused the crawler; the book scores that as a failure, but no page-level line is quoted to them.
  • Never-scanned row (cann-studio.com): no map line of any kind. See the NOT_MEASURED read.
  • Single storefront: "your store", "your location", singular, everywhere.
  • CASL row (theniagaraherbalist.com; canada_flag true): suppressed from every email send. No cold email to it, ever.
  • Lawn-company flagships (Cookies Bloomington, Cookies Peoria Heights, Terrabis - Grayville, Thrive Dispensary Metropolis): the leader on their own map is Weed Man Lawn Care; the lawn line is theirs by right. [grid-leader-weed-man-flagships]
  1. Armed or unarmed: if the row carries a contact name, greet by first name; if not, the business name is the knife. That is the only difference between the two calls.
Script 1: Cold call to a listed business

Posture: closer. The rep is calling to book a free audit, not to leave a message about a file. Every branch ends at THE CLOSE, and THE CLOSE ends in a booked time or a named callback day.

Opener

Hi, this is [your first name] from the Nobody Is Safe desk. Am I talking to the owner, or whoever signs the RankReallyHigh invoice at [business name]?
Here is why I am calling. [Business name] is one of 99 dispensaries in a public file about RankReallyHigh's client book, and the file is not kind. One line from it, then you decide if you want the rest. [book-scope-clients]

The one-line knife, by verdict (from prep step 3):

NOWHERE:

We ran the search your customers actually type from 121 spots around your store[s], and [business name] never came up. Not ranked low. Not there. [book-scope-pins-per-grid]

PACK_WITH_TURF (the 5 found rows):

You are one of only 5 storefronts in RankReallyHigh's whole book that show up at all on the search your customers type. The same file grades the website they built you failing. [headline-storefronts-found] [rankers-still-failing-product]

NOT_MEASURED (cann-studio.com only):

The scan went looking for a storefront to measure for [business name] and could not find one solid enough to stand on, so nothing on your map was ever measured, and the file says so. Your website was measured. [client-never-scanned]

Then, all three:

Want the rest? It takes a minute.

Branch on the response:

Branch A: "Who are you? Is this a sales call?"

Straight answer. We compete with RankReallyHigh, and the file says so on its face: "not published by or affiliated with Rank Really High." The file is free, it stays up whether we ever talk again or not, and nobody named in it can pay to change it. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included. [book-boilerplate-verbatim] [integrity-anchor-verbatim]
What I am offering is a free audit of your own row, quick or long, your choice. Not a pitch. We do not work with everyone. First let me read you the line that matters.

Then go to THE READ.

Branch B: "Not interested" / "I'm busy"

Whole-book first, then the row, then the callback. The middle sentence is verdict-gated: "your storefront[s] came back empty too" is true only for a NOWHERE row and is never said to the other six.

Ten seconds. RankReallyHigh has 280 storefronts under service. We measured every one of them on the search your customers actually type, and 275 never appear. That is nearly every client they have, and [business name] is in the same file. [book-scope-storefronts] [headline-storefronts-invisible]
If NOWHERE (93 rows): Your storefront[s] came back empty too.
If PACK_WITH_TURF or NOT_MEASURED (the 5 found rows and the 1 never-scanned row): Your row is on the same page.
Your business is not the problem. RankReallyHigh is failing almost every single client they manage, and we have the data to prove it. I will call you back [day] at [time]. If a different day is better, tell me which.

Log the callback day. Call back on that day. Do not offer to go away, do not announce a last attempt, do not leave the address as a substitute for the callback.

Branch C: "Go ahead"

Go to THE READ, using the branch that matches their measured verdict.

THE READ, by verdict

Method first, one breath, so the numbers mean something:

One search, the one your customers actually type. 121 spots laid over each storefront's own neighborhood. All 99 clients, all 280 storefronts. Filed 2026-08-09, re-checked and amended 2026-08-26. [book-scope-pins-per-grid] [book-scope-clients] [book-scope-storefronts] [book-filed]

NOWHERE (93 rows):

Across the book: 275 of 280 storefronts never appear anywhere on their own map. Counting businesses instead of storefronts, 94 of the 99 are invisible at every location they run; that is 93 of the 98 that could be scanned, plus the one that could not. [headline-storefronts-invisible] [clients-invisible-everywhere]
Your row: [storefronts_scanned] storefront[s] scanned, found at none. The name on top of your map is [grid_leader], holding [grid_leader_coverage_pct] percent of it. That business is collecting the customers who search from your own block, and the invoice RankReallyHigh sends you is for that map.
Lawn-company flagships only (prep step 4): The business beating you on the weed search mows lawns. Weed Man Lawn Care. A company that cuts grass owns the map you pay RankReallyHigh to own. [grid-leader-weed-man-flagships]
This is not opinion and it is not a personal attack on anyone, it just is what it is. Once you see the data you cannot unsee it.

Go to THE LIVE RECEIPT.

PACK_WITH_TURF (5 rows: Roots LA, Roslindale Cannabis, Nile Cannabis, Quincy Cannabis Co, Clouditude), the concession form:

First the good news, and it is real: out of 280 storefronts in RankReallyHigh's book, 5 show up at all on the search your customers type, and you are one of them. The file names all five: Roots LA, Roslindale Cannabis, Nile Cannabis, Quincy Cannabis Co, Clouditude. [headline-storefronts-found] [the-only-five-found]
Now the other line on your card. All five of you are running a website that fails most of the checks it was measured on: four fail 4 of 6, one fails 5 of 6. Not one client in the book gets both the map and a working website: 0 of 99, and 0 of the 98 that were scanned. Your rank is surviving the website RankReallyHigh built you. It is not being produced by it. [rankers-still-failing-product] [rank-and-pass-zero]
Self-lead swap (quincycannabis.co): And one more thing the file prints: your own listing leads your own map, the only client in the whole book that does. That is you doing that, not them. [grid-leader-client-self]
All other found rows: The file also prints which listing leads your map and how much of it: [grid_leader] at [grid_leader_coverage_pct] percent. You show up on the map; that listing owns it. (Name-share gate from prep step 4 applies.)

Go to THE LIVE RECEIPT.

NOT_MEASURED (cann-studio.com, 1 row), the honest not-scanned form:

Honest disclosure first. The scan could not resolve a storefront listing for [business name], so no map was ever run for you, and your row says exactly that. Under the book's stated rule, a client that could not be scanned is scored as failing what it could not be scanned for, and the page prints that as a ruling, not a reading. I will not pretend to have map numbers for you. [client-never-scanned] [client-never-scanned-honest-line]
What was measured is the roster you are on, and your website. The roster: 94 of 99 clients invisible everywhere on the search their customers type, 93 of the 98 that could be scanned plus you. 98 of 99 sites fail most of their product checks, 78 of 99 on the checks that returned a reading. [clients-invisible-everywhere] [product-failing-verdict]
Your site is the one site in the book that does not grade failing under that rule. It still sits on the same theme as 96 of the 99, and a search engine following links reaches no product page on it. [template-family-01] [zero-products-fault]

Truth gates for this row: its phone load passes Google's bar, so the speed line is never said to it; its website grade is WEAK, 2 of 6, so it is never called failing.

Go to THE LIVE RECEIPT (book-level: the verdict table and the five-winner list rather than a personal competitor report, which does not exist for this row).

THE LIVE RECEIPT

Are you near a computer, or on your phone? I do not want you to take my word for any of this. Your own page is at [read the per-client address aloud, slowly, twice]. On it: your verdict, your storefronts, who leads your map, your website grade. Under it there is a link marked competitor report. That is localrankingtracker.com, a third-party tool, not our server. You are looking at every spot we measured around your own store and who holds each one. [receipts-competitor-reports]

Branch on what happens next:

Branch D1: "This is real." / silence / swearing

Go to THE CLOSE.

Branch D2: "My agency will say this is wrong."

They might. Ask them one thing. The same 280 storefronts, the same Google listings, were scanned on two searches. On "dispensary near me", the phrase RankReallyHigh built for, 139 of 280 crack the top 3. On the search your customers actually type, 5 of 280 show up at all. A broken scan cannot rank you on one phrase and erase you on the other. Same spots, same tool, same listings. [flip-same-storefronts-both-phrases] [dispensary-top3] [headline-storefronts-found]

Full objection handling lives in rebuttals.md. Then go to THE CLOSE.

Branch D3: "I need to talk to my agency first."

You should. Take one question into that conversation: "Show me, on a live map, where my store ranks for the weed search at my front door and a mile out, today." A map with your store on it is an answer. A dashboard, a keyword list, or "rankings fluctuate" is a dodge. The full question list is what the audit hands you.

Then go to THE CLOSE. (The arming questions are in fence-sitter-questions.md; they are delivered at the audit, never read cold.)

THE CLOSE

The offer, word-perfect, every time:

We can do this two ways. If you want the quick version, I can show you in 15 minutes every way RankReallyHigh is failing you. If you've got more time, I can tell you more about us, why we're auditing other agencies' entire books of business, and how we can help fix the problems RRH is charging you for.
Which one, and what day?

If they hesitate, ask a second time, same door, shorter:

Fifteen minutes or an hour. Both free, and neither one is a pitch; we only talk about working together if it is a real fit for both of us. [Day] at [time], or [day] at [time]?

If booked: both sides write it down, repeat it back, done. The rep confirms by hand. No promised automated confirmation or reminder; none exists.

If not booked, the persistence rule:

Then I will call you [day]. Your page does not move between now and then.

Log the callback day. Call back on that day. Never "keep the address and call us." Never "last one from me." The door stays open and the ask stays live on day 89 exactly as it did on day 1.

Script 2: Inbound call, "I saw the file"

An inbound call is a reply. The moment it lands, the row leaves the silent-contact schedule and enters the daily sequence until the audit is BOOKED or they say STOP. The rep logs it as such.

Answer frame:

You've reached the desk that published it. Which business are you calling about?

Confirm the business name only. Record no personal details.

Branch on caller type:

Branch A: Curious or spooked ("Is this real? Why is my shop in this?")

It is real, and here is exactly what it is. We measured every active RankReallyHigh client: 99 dispensaries, 99 websites, 280 storefronts, 121 spots around each one, 33,880 measured spots on the search your customers actually type. You are on RankReallyHigh's roster, so you are in the file. Nothing about you specifically put you there. [book-scope-clients] [book-scope-storefronts] [book-scope-pins-per-grid] [book-scope-map-points]
Pull your page up while we talk and I will walk you through your own numbers, including the third-party report, so you are not trusting me on anything.

Walk the card per THE READ. Then go to Script 1's THE CLOSE.

Branch B: Angry ("Take my listing down.")

I hear you, and I will give you the straight policy rather than a runaround. The file is a measured record of public map results. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included. There is no removal price, for anyone, in either direction. That cuts both ways: RankReallyHigh cannot pay us to soften it, and nobody can pay us to sharpen it. [integrity-anchor-verbatim] [integrity-independence-verbatim]
Every number on your card comes from the third-party report linked on the card itself. If you think a number is wrong, open that link with me right now and we will read the source together.

If they walk the receipt and stay angry: let them go politely. Do not argue past the data. Log the call. If they say stop, that is a STOP: suppression, instant and permanent.

If the receipt lands: offer the audit per THE CLOSE.

Branch C: Suspicious ("You're just trying to poach RankReallyHigh's clients.")

We compete with them, yes, and the file says so in plain text: "not published by or affiliated with Rank Really High." That is exactly why nothing in it asks for your trust. Every storefront claim resolves to a localrankingtracker.com report you can open yourself: 98 competitor reports, 560 scan reports, one per storefront on each of the two searches. [book-boilerplate-verbatim] [receipts-competitor-reports] [receipts-scan-reports]
And the file prints what RankReallyHigh gets right. 97 of 99 sites carry LocalBusiness schema, the check they mostly pass. 6 of 99 sites pass Google's phone test, 6 of the 82 that got a score. The 5 clients that rank are named as ranking. A hit piece does not publish the other side's wins. [schema-localbusiness-present] [psi-pass] [the-only-five-found]

Then walk their card if they want, and offer THE CLOSE.

Branch D: Fence-sitter ("I'm thinking of leaving them. What would you do?")

That decision deserves the audit, not a two-minute phone answer. Two things now, the rest on the call.
One: your own card, with your own numbers, is at the address I will give you. Two: before you decide anything, ask RankReallyHigh this: "Of your cannabis clients, how many rank top 3 on the weed search anywhere, and can I talk to three of them?" The file's answer is 5 of 280 storefronts, 5 of 99 clients. Listen for whether they give you a rate or a single hand-picked name. [headline-storefronts-found] [clients-ranking-somewhere]

Book the audit per THE CLOSE. The full question list is handed over at the audit.

Branch E: Press, another agency, or an onlooker

The file speaks for itself and I will not speculate past it. It is one of 3 books on the desk: 906 businesses measured, 131,285 map points so far. Everything we can prove is on the page; everything we cannot prove is not. [hub-books] [hub-businesses] [hub-map-points]

End politely. No audit offer; they are not the buyer.

Script 3: The free audit: opening and running it

Use at the start of a booked audit, either format. Goal: frame, their card on both searches, the two-front summary, honest scope, then the decision.

1. Frame (first 60 seconds)

Three ground rules so you know exactly what this is.
One: this is a free audit, a second opinion on your own row, and you get the benefit of it. The file about RankReallyHigh is free and public and it reads the same whether we ever work together or not. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included. [integrity-anchor-verbatim]
Two: we compete with RankReallyHigh and the file says so on its face. Weigh everything I say with that in mind, which is why every claim I make today is a link you can reopen after this call without me on the line. [book-boilerplate-verbatim]
Three: I am not here to pitch you. We do not work with everyone. If partway through it looks like a real fit for both of us, I will say so and you decide whether you want to hear about it. If it does not, you leave with the audit and nothing else.

2. Their card, both searches

Open their scan reports live. The book carries 560 of them, one per storefront on each of the two searches, all under one scan tag, so nothing was cherry-picked from separate sessions. [receipts-scan-reports] [receipts-scan-tag]

If NOWHERE: show the weed-search map first (their absence), then the "dispensary near me" map (wherever they land). Then:

Same storefront, same Google listing, two searches. The book's core argument is six words: "The agency optimized the smaller term." Google's own data says customers type "weed" about twice as often as "dispensary", every single week for the past year, not one exception; the biggest week of the year was 2026-04-19. You are ranked, where you are ranked at all, on the door with half the traffic. [argument-smaller-term-verbatim] [trends-weed-average] [trends-dispensary-average] [trends-weed-wins-every-week] [trends-peak-week]

Rep's receipt for the trends line (not spoken as raw numbers): "weed" averages 66 on the interest index, "dispensary" 35, "cannabis" 21; "weed" wins 53 of 53 weekly points; at the peak week "weed" hit 100 while "dispensary" sat at 42. [trends-cannabis-average] [trends-note-verbatim]

If PACK_WITH_TURF: concede the map win with the receipts, then pivot to product per Script 1. Not one client in the book holds both the map and a working website: 0 of 99, 0 of the 98 scanned. [rank-and-pass-zero]

If NOT_MEASURED: restate the disclosure from Script 1. Roster receipts only on the map side; walk the book-level tables. The website was measured and its lines stand as measured (WEAK, 2 of 6; the speed line is never said to this row).

3. The two-front summary, on the 99 base

Front one, the map. 275 of 280 storefronts are invisible on the search your customers actually type. On the search RankReallyHigh chose to build for, "dispensary near me", 139 of 280 crack the top 3, 141 sit at rank 4 or worse, and 7 never rank on a single spot of their own map on either search. [headline-storefronts-invisible] [dispensary-top3] [dispensary-beaten] [dispensary-unranked]
Front two, the website they built you. 98 of 99 sites fail most of their product checks, 78 of 99 on the checks that returned a reading. 96 of 99 sit on one WordPress theme, "weedpress". 95 of 99 run the same menu plugin and 92 of those are on the exact same version, 1.38.1. [product-failing-verdict] [template-family-01] [template-theme-weedpress] [plugin-joint-ecommerce] [plugin-version-cohort]
Speed: Google says a phone should show the main content in two and a half seconds. 98 of 99 sites miss that bar, 76 of the 77 that returned a load time, and the typical site takes 9.2 seconds, about 3.7 times the bar. 35 sites take more than 10 seconds. That is nine seconds of blank screen for a customer standing on a sidewalk. [speed-lcp-over-google-bar] [speed-median-load] [speed-bar-multiple] [speed-over-10-seconds]
The crawl: we read 24,229 pages across the 96 sites that let us in. 97 of 99 sites serve pages that tell Google not to index them, 95 of the 96 crawled; 4,736 pages in all. 97 of 99 sites have pages with no description under the blue link, 95 of 96 crawled; 4,335 pages. 97 of 99 have pages with no headline at all, 95 of 96 crawled; 2,518 pages. 95 of 99 repeat one page title across their own pages, 93 of 96 crawled; the worst case is one title on 275 pages of a single site. [onpage-pages-crawled] [onpage-nonindexable-sites] [onpage-nonindexable-pages] [onpage-missing-description-sites] [onpage-missing-description-pages] [onpage-no-h1-sites] [onpage-no-h1-pages] [onpage-duplicate-title-sites] [onpage-worst-title-repeat]
The menu, which is the product: on 54 of 99 sites a search engine following links never reaches a single product page, 52 of the 96 menus we could read. Where a product page is reachable at all, the typical site exposes 12. Of 14,060 menu pages in the book, 752 clear the five basics. The typical menu page carries 26 words. 1 of 99 homepages tells Google what the store sells. [zero-products-fault] [product-pages-median] [menu-pages-total] [menu-pass-basics] [menu-word-median] [schema-homepage-product-tagging]

Then the concessions, said out loud, because they make the rest land:

What they get right: 97 of 99 sites carry LocalBusiness schema. 6 of 99 pass Google's phone test, 6 of the 82 that got a score. 46 of 99 score 90 or better on accessibility, 46 of the 80 that returned a score. 78 of 99 sites have at least one menu page that clears the basics, 78 of 96 crawled. One. [schema-localbusiness-present] [psi-pass] [a11y-pass-bar] [menu-sites-with-passing-page]

Rep's note: the llms.txt line (99 of 99 without one) is a presence check and a fleet-uniformity receipt only. Never pitch it as invisibility to anything. [llms-txt-absent]

4. Honest scope

Now what the file does not say. It measures one search and six product checks plus a crawl. It does not measure your foot traffic, your revenue, your ad spend, or your in-store experience, and I will not pretend it does. What it proves is narrow and hard: on the search your customers type most, the roster you are on is invisible 98.2 percent of the time, and the market is not the reason. Across the 280 maps, 153 different businesses hold the top spot somewhere, a client of RankReallyHigh leads its own map exactly once, and the typical map has 39 competing businesses on it. Someone wins every map. [headline-invisible-share] [grid-leaders-distinct] [grid-leader-client-self] [dispensary-median-competitors]

5. Branches during the audit

"My agency responded to the file." Take their points one at a time against rebuttals.md, each with its live receipt.

"What would you do differently?" Stay diagnostic in the quick format; the plan belongs to the hour:

The file supports three statements. The demand is on "weed", not "dispensary", every week of the year. The maps are winnable, because someone wins each of them. And the website is fixable, because the failures are measured and listed, page by page. In the quick version, that is where I stop. In the hour, if we get to fit, the plan for your specific store is the second half of the call.

"What does it cost?" Never a number before scope:

I am not going to make up a number on a call. If we get to fit, pricing comes out of what your row actually needs, and that is exactly what this audit establishes. What I can give you for free is everything the file already measured.

"Can I get this in writing?" Yes, and in this order: their own listing page, a screenshot of their row, the third-party reports. Plus the owner's line, delivered as promised:

I can send you the list of sites literally identical to yours, same theme, same broken stats, and RRH doesn't care. [template-family-01] [plugin-version-cohort]

6. Decision close: the client moves off RankReallyHigh

Here is the decision in front of you, stated plainly. You are paying RankReallyHigh for a map result you do not have and a website that fails its own checks, same as nearly every client in their book. Staying means paying for that again next month. The fix is leaving them for something that works. If the fit is real on our side, that is us, and I will tell you so right now. If it is not, the audit is still yours, and the question list goes with you into the conversation with them. Which way do you want to go?

If they proceed with us: set the next working session verbally, on the call, and say what it involves. No forms, no promised automated anything.

If they want the agency conversation first: hand them the fence-sitter questions, then name the day you call back and call back. The door does not close.

7. What the 1-hour format adds

The 15-minute quick audit is: their card on both searches, the two-front summary at headline level, honest scope, the decision close. Every way RankReallyHigh is failing them, on their own numbers.

The 1-hour full audit adds, in this order:

  1. Education. How the map is measured (121 spots per store, the radius sized per market and printed: 140 of the 280 maps ran at 5 miles). Why the search phrase decides everything. How a crawler reads a menu and why a menu drawn in by script inside boxes that are not links is a menu Google never sees, on the 52 of 96 menus where that was measured. [book-scope-pins-per-grid] [scan-radii] [zero-products-mechanism]
  2. The identical-sites walk. Their site next to the others on the same theme and the same plugin version, page by page.
  3. Who we are and why we audit whole books. 3 books filed, 906 businesses, 131,285 map points. The anchors. [hub-books] [hub-businesses] [hub-map-points]
  4. Only if the fit is real, decided in the first part of the call: the presentation of our services and the plan for their store. If the fit is not there, this part does not happen, and the rep says so.
