THE PHRASE glasses near me · open the live book

Fence-sitter questions

EyeCarePro fence-sitter questions

For the wavering EyeCarePro client who is not ready to cancel but is ready to ask. Hand these to the prospect; each is a question to put to their incumbent agency in their next account call.

Design rule, enforced: every question ships a triple. (a) The data receipt behind it, cited to a stat id in Marketing/nobodyissafe-hub/sales/_ammo/eyecarepro.json via a bracketed ammo tag, so the client knows the question is loaded before they ask it. (b) The predicted dodge, so the client recognizes the dodge as a dodge in real time. (c) What a real answer would look like, so a good answer gets the credit it deserves. A question we could not receipt was cut.

How to arm the client: their own receipts live on their listing page (l/eyecarepro/{their-domain}.html) and in their linked third-party competitor report, one of 710 unique report links in the book [ammo: competitor-reports-710]. The files are free. Nobody named in a file can pay to change one. "Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included" [ammo: anchor-verbatim].

Q1. "Show me, on a map, where my practice ranks for 'glasses near me' around my own building, today."
  • (a) Receipt: the file measured every practice at 121 pin positions on its own neighborhood grid [ammo: pins-per-grid], and 358 of 710 practices sit outside the top 3 on their own map [ammo: outside-top3]. The client already holds a live 121-pin competitor report for their exact grid [ammo: competitor-reports-710].
  • (b) Predicted dodge: a pivot to an organic-keyword dashboard, a positions-tracked spreadsheet, or "rankings fluctuate daily so a snapshot is misleading."
  • (c) A real answer: a live map grid, pin by pin, on the phrase patients type, produced during the call. The client can compare it to the file's grid on the spot. A dashboard is not a map; if the vendor cannot show the map, the file remains the only map in the room.
Q2. "At how many of the 121 spots around my building am I the number one choice? Is there one street corner where I am first?"
  • (a) Receipt: 189 of 710 practices hold the number one spot at zero of their 121 pins [ammo: no-turf], and the median client is first at 4.96 percent of its own pins, about 1 in 20 [ammo: median-pin-shares].
  • (b) Predicted dodge: "Number one is not the goal, being in the pack is what matters," or an average-position stat that blends away the zeros.
  • (c) A real answer: a number out of 121, said out loud, with the map to back it. If the answer is zero, the honest version of it is an admission; anything that avoids giving the number is the dodge in costume.
Q3. "Of your 710 optometry clients, how many make the top 3 anywhere on their own grid, and how many lead it? What is my percentile?"
  • (a) Receipt: 352 of 710 make the top 3 somewhere [ammo: in-top3]; only 165 of 710 lead their own grid [ammo: grid-leaders]; 16 were never found at even one pin [ammo: never-found].
  • (b) Predicted dodge: a cherry-picked winner ("look at this client in Ohio"), or "we can't share other clients' data."
  • (c) A real answer: a base rate with a denominator. One winner out of an unstated many is the dodge; the file's answer has all 710 in it. Note the client-privacy excuse fails here: the aggregate rate discloses nobody.
Q4. "My website is one of 1,220 sites on the same template. What did my money buy that the other 1,219 didn't get?"
  • (a) Receipt: 577 of 708 measured client sites (81.5 percent) run on one shared template family [ammo: shared-template], and that family spans 1,220 measured sites book-wide [ammo: template-family-size].
  • (b) Predicted dodge: "The template is a proven, optimized framework; differentiation comes from your content." Note what this question is not: the file makes no claim that sharing a template suppresses rankings, and a vendor who answers "there is no template-uniqueness ranking factor" is answering a question nobody asked. This is an invoice question about a deliverable sold as custom work. Keep it there.
  • (c) A real answer: named, showable differences between the client's site and the other copies: distinct structure, distinct copy, distinct assets. If differentiation comes from content, see Q5, because the file measured the content too.
Q5. "Which sentences on my homepage were written for my practice, and which appear on hundreds of other practices' sites?"
  • Pre-check before arming this one: 208 of 708 measured sites are majority-recycled, which means for the other 500 the vendor can honestly show custom paragraphs and win the exchange. The recycled-copy finding line prints on exactly those 208 cards. Read your own listing first; ask this question only if your card carries the copy line. If it does not, lead with Q4 instead.
  • (a) Receipt: 208 of 708 measured sites have majority-recycled copy, and the median duplicate 8-word phrase share across the book is 27.88 percent [ammo: recycled-copy].
  • (b) Predicted dodge: "That is industry-standard best-practice copy," or an offer to "refresh the content" without acknowledging the recycling.
  • (c) A real answer: a side-by-side diff of the client's homepage against other client homepages, produced by the vendor, plus a rewrite commitment with a date. An offer to refresh without showing the diff concedes the finding while hiding its size.
Q6. "What is my site's mobile load time right now, and what is Google's bar? Run it while I watch."
  • (a) Receipt: 702 of 706 measured client sites (99.4 percent) load slower on a phone than Google's 2.5 second LCP bar [ammo: lcp-over-google-bar]; the median is 9,387 milliseconds, 3.75 times the bar [ammo: median-mobile-lcp]; 307 sites take over 10 seconds [ammo: lcp-tails]. Anyone can re-run this on Google PageSpeed Insights in 60 seconds.
  • (b) Predicted dodge: showing the desktop score (median desktop 65 vs mobile 49 in this book [ammo: median-mobile-lcp]), or "lab scores are vanity metrics; real users don't notice."
  • (c) A real answer: the mobile number, run live, with either a pass or a dated remediation plan. The bar is Google's, not ours, so "the metric doesn't matter" is an argument with Google, not with the file.
Q7. "Am I on your old platform build or the new one, and when were you going to tell me there is a difference?"
  • (a) Receipt: 576 client sites sit on the older build (sitebuilder-v3), 83 on the newer (sitebuilder-v4) [ammo: platform-split]. The difference is measurable from public page source: every llms.txt file found in the book ships on the newer build; the 576 older-build sites have zero [ammo: llms-only-on-v4].
  • (b) Predicted dodge: "you are scheduled for an upgrade" with no date, pretending the two builds are equivalent, or a debate about whether llms.txt matters. Do not take that last bait: llms.txt is cited here as the marker that proves two product generations exist, not as a ranking or AI-visibility claim, and the question survives whatever anyone thinks of the file itself.
  • (c) A real answer: which build the client is on, what the newer build adds, what the upgrade costs, and a date. The two-tier receipt is the trap in this question: the vendor demonstrably maintains a newer product, because 83 clients are on it, and has not told the other 576 which side of the line they are on or what it costs to cross.
Q8. "Is my Google Business Profile claimed, and can you show me the claim right now?"
  • Pre-check before arming this one: 619 of the 710 practices in this book DO have a claimed profile, and for them the vendor answers with a ten-second screen-share and walks away stronger. Check your own profile before asking; if it is claimed, skip this question. It is loaded only for the 91.
  • (a) Receipt: 91 of 710 practices have an unclaimed Google Business Profile while paying a local-search agency [ammo: gbp-unclaimed]. Claiming is free.
  • (b) Predicted dodge: "Of course it is claimed" without showing anything, or blaming the client for a verification step.
  • (c) A real answer: the profile's management screen on the vendor's own display, live. This is a ten-second proof for a vendor doing the work. For 91 clients in this book the truthful answer is no, which is why the question is worth asking out loud if you are one of them.
Q9. "Can a new patient book an appointment from my website? Show me the path."
  • Pre-check before arming this one: the file counts 211 of 710 sites with no booking path, which means the other 499 vendors demo the path comfortably and win the exchange. Try it yourself first, on your phone, from your homepage to a bookable slot. If you get there, skip this question. If you dead-end, it is armed, and the demo request below is fair whatever anyone counts as "booking".
  • (a) Receipt: 211 of 710 client sites have no booking path on the site [ammo: no-booking-path].
  • (b) Predicted dodge: "Patients prefer to call," or pointing to a contact form and calling it booking. The boundary between a form and a booking is arguable in the abstract, which is why the request is a demonstration, not a definition.
  • (c) A real answer: the vendor walks the booking flow on a phone, from homepage to a confirmed appointment slot, while the client watches. If the path does not exist, the marketing spend is landing patients on a page with no door.
Q10. "Which national chain is on my map, and what is your plan to beat them, with dates?"
  • (a) Receipt: 654 of 710 client grids (92.1 percent) have at least one national chain inside the top 10 [ammo: chain-top10]; 288 of 710 maps are led by a chain outright [ammo: chain-led-maps]; America's Best alone leads 82 client grids and sits top-10 on 332 [ammo: americas-best].
  • (b) Predicted dodge: "Chains have unlimited budgets, you cannot out-spend them," or "we focus on your organic presence" (a subject change from the map to somewhere else).
  • (c) A real answer: the chain named from the client's own grid, and a concrete plan against it with dates and measurable checkpoints. Locals do beat chains on these maps: 165 clients lead their own grid today [ammo: grid-leaders]. "It cannot be done" is contradicted by the vendor's own winners.
Q11. "Name one of your clients whose website passes all six of the file's product checks."
  • (a) Receipt: zero of 710 client sites pass all 6 checks; only 6 of 710 pass even 5 of 6 [ammo: zero-clean-sites]; zero earned the SOUND verdict [ammo: zero-sound-sites]; 94 practices fail every single check [ammo: fail-count-distribution].
  • (b) Predicted dodge: attacking the checks ("arbitrary grading by a competitor") instead of naming a site.
  • (c) A real answer: a named client site that passes, checkable by anyone. The checks are re-runnable from public surfaces: template detection, copy comparison, llms.txt presence, PageSpeed, schema. If the checks were rigged, one passing site would be easy to produce. There are zero. Attacking the referee is what a team does when it cannot point at the scoreboard.
Q12. "I rank fine. So why does my own file grade my website FAILING, and which one of those did you build?"
  • (a) Receipt: 300 practices rank top 3 AND run a FAILING or BROKEN site, 85.2 percent of everyone who ranks [ammo: winners-who-should-still-leave]. The book's cleanest proof that rank and product are separate: one practice ranks number one at all 121 of its 121 pins and still fails all 6 product checks [ammo: pattern-crossover-elkton]; another ranks number one on an 85-competitor grid while its phone load is 68,350 milliseconds [ammo: pattern-winner-68-seconds]; and the only PageSpeed 100 in the book still ranks 80th of 148 [ammo: pattern-perfect-site-still-buried].
  • (b) Predicted dodge: "The rank proves the strategy works; those grades are a competitor's opinion." The sharper vendor turns the file's own exhibits around: "their perfect-map-broken-site cases prove the product grades have no effect on rank, so this is a website-rebuild pitch dressed as an audit."
  • (c) A real answer: an explanation of what, specifically, the vendor built that produced the rank, and a defense of the product numbers on their own terms, since the speed and template findings re-run publicly. If they play the turnaround, let them finish, because it concedes the question: the file never claims the product produces rank; the crossover exhibits are printed to prove the two are separate purchases. So if the product does not produce your rank, the thing the vendor builds and bills for is not what ranks you, and the invoice question stands naked: what did the product line buy, and why does the deliverable itself fail Google's public bar like 702 of 706 measured sites here [ammo: lcp-over-google-bar]? Either the product matters and it fails its checks, or it does not matter and you are paying for it anyway. The file's larger point sits behind this question: in this book, 710 of 710 practices have at least one measured deficiency [ammo: both-fail]. Nobody came back clean, which is why the question is never whether something is wrong, only whether the vendor will say what.
Reading the answers

Three outcomes, all useful:

  1. Real answers to most questions: the client keeps a vendor who can show maps, name numbers, and commit to dates. The file did its job either way.
  2. Dodges recognized as dodges: the client watched the pivot to dashboards (Q1), the missing denominator (Q3), the desktop screen (Q6), the referee attack (Q11). Each dodge was predicted in writing before the meeting. That is usually the moment a fence-sitter stops sitting.
  3. Silence or anger: an answer too.

The record stays public and free regardless of what the client decides. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included [ammo: anchor-verbatim].

Rebuttals

EyeCarePro rebuttals

Objections an EyeCarePro client will actually raise, with the desk's answer. Every number cites a stat id in Marketing/nobodyissafe-hub/sales/_ammo/eyecarepro.json via a bracketed ammo tag. A rebuttal is never an argument alone: each one ends with the live-receipt move, something you open on the call so the prospect is looking at evidence, not listening to a salesperson.

The standing live-receipt move, used in every rebuttal below: open the prospect's own listing page (l/eyecarepro/{their-domain}.html, all 710 exist) and from it their own third-party competitor report, one of 710 unique links published in the book [ammo: competitor-reports-710].

Ethics spine, verbatim where the ethics come up: "Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included". The files are free. Nobody named in a file can pay to change one. [ammo: anchor-verbatim]

R1. "My agency says your data is wrong or stale."

Why it lands: it is the incumbent's cheapest defense, one sentence, no evidence required.

Rebuttal: "Good, then this is easy to settle, because neither of us has to be believed. Your page links a live competitor report on a third-party scanning service. It is not our chart, it is not their chart, it is a scan of your own neighborhood we both can open [ammo: competitor-reports-710]. And the file audits itself in public: 28 grids flagged too tight to contain a real contest, 5 scans purged, 2 sites that could not be captured, every limit disclosed inside the book [ammo: self-audit-disclosed]. Ask your agency one thing: if the data is wrong, show a fresher scan of the same grid, 121 pins, same phrase, as a map. Not a dashboard export. A map."

Live-receipt move: open their competitor report together and read the scan date and the pin grid off the screen. If they repeat "stale", ask them to point at the pin nearest their front door and say what it shows.

R2. "They do more than maps. Maps are just one part of what they do."

Why it lands: it is true that the invoice has more line items than maps, so the client feels the file is measuring a fraction.

Rebuttal: "Agreed, they do more than maps, and the file measured that too. The other thing they deliver is the website, so here is the website ledger: zero of 710 client sites earned the SOUND verdict, not one [ammo: zero-sound-sites]. 603 of 710 grade FAILING or BROKEN [ammo: failing-or-broken]. 702 of 706 measured sites are slower on a phone than Google's own 2.5 second bar [ammo: lcp-over-google-bar], and 577 of 708 run on one shared template, a family of 1,220 near-identical sites [ammo: shared-template, template-family-size]. So the defense is: judge us on more than the map. The file did. The more failed harder."

Live-receipt move: on their listing page, read their own product finding lines aloud [ammo: every-listing-has-findings]. Then run their site through Google PageSpeed live and let the number land on their screen.

R3. "Switching is painful. Rebuilding everything is not worth it."

Why it lands: it is the real cost. Vendor migrations burn owner time.

Rebuttal: "Let us price the pain of staying with the same honesty. Today you are on a site that is one of 1,220 copies of a template [ammo: template-family-size], and 655 client sites carry the agency's own logo asset on the client's page [ammo: agency-logo-on-client-sites]. On the map side, half the book sits outside the top 3 at its own front door [ammo: outside-top3]. Staying is not neutral; it is paying monthly to keep those numbers. And note what actually belongs to you either way: your Google Business Profile, your reviews, your patient base. The parts worth keeping are portable. The part that is painful to leave is the part the file grades FAILING."

Live-receipt move: open their competitor report and ask: "Which part of this map does staying fix, and by when? If the agency has given you a date, hold them to it. If they have not, that is your answer about the cost of staying."

R4. "We rank fine. I checked and we show up."

Why it lands: the owner searched from their own desk, logged in, standing inside their own building, and saw themselves.

Rebuttal: "Where you searched from is the whole trick. Google's answer changes street by street, which is why the file measured 121 positions around your building, not one [ammo: pins-per-grid]. Across the book, 352 of 710 do make the top 3 somewhere, and the file says so [ammo: in-top3]. But only 165 of 710 lead their own grid [ammo: grid-leaders], and the median client is first choice at 4.96 percent of its own pins, roughly 1 in 20 street corners [ammo: median-pin-shares]. Showing up at your own desk is not a market position. It is a home game with one fan."

Live-receipt move: open their competitor report and point at the pins two or three streets out. "Here is you at your desk. Here is you four blocks away. Which one does a new patient see?"

R5. "You are their competitor. This is a hit piece."

Why it lands: it is the correct starting suspicion, and pretending otherwise would deserve to fail.

Rebuttal: "We are their competitor, and the file says so on every page, verbatim: 'not affiliated with, endorsed by, or compensated by any agency named in these files; operated by a competitor who publishes the receipts' [ammo: anchor-verbatim]. Now look at what a hit piece would not do. It would not concede that 352 of 710 clients make the top 3 [ammo: in-top3]. It would not publish its own flaws: 28 flagged grids, 5 purged scans, 2 uncaptured sites [ammo: self-audit-disclosed]. And it would not link 710 third-party reports anyone can open to check the desk's own numbers [ammo: competitor-reports-710]. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included [ammo: anchor-verbatim]. Judge the motive if you like; check the receipts either way."

Live-receipt move: open their competitor report, which is hosted by a third-party scanning service, not by us, and let them verify the numbers match their file.

R6. "Our website looks fine to me."

Why it lands: the owner looked at it on an office desktop, where it does look fine.

Rebuttal: "On a desktop it probably is fine; the median desktop PageSpeed in this book is 65 against 49 on mobile [ammo: median-mobile-lcp]. Patients are not on your desktop. On a phone, the median client site in this book takes 9,387 milliseconds to paint, 3.75 times Google's 2.5 second bar [ammo: median-mobile-lcp], and 702 of 706 measured sites miss that bar [ammo: lcp-over-google-bar]. 645 sites take over 4 seconds and 307 take over 10 [ammo: lcp-tails]. Count to nine out loud. That is a new patient holding their phone, waiting for your homepage."

Live-receipt move: run their domain through Google PageSpeed Insights on the call, mobile tab, and compare the live result to the number on their listing page. The two should agree, which also quietly re-proves R1.

R7. "We have been with them for years. They know our practice."

Why it lands: loyalty plus sunk cost, and a real human relationship with an account manager.

Rebuttal: "Then the years are the evidence. After years of knowing your practice, the deliverable is a site on a template shared by 577 of 708 measured clients, one of 1,220 copies [ammo: shared-template, template-family-size], and across the book 208 sites have majority-recycled copy, with a median duplicate phrase share of 27.88 percent [ammo: recycled-copy]. Knowing you and building for you are different services. The file can only measure the second one."

Live-receipt move: open their listing page next to another EyeCarePro practice's listing page from a different state and compare the finding lines. Same template family, same failure pattern, different city.

R8. "Rankings fluctuate. One scan proves nothing."

Why it lands: it is technically true that map results move, and the incumbent knows it sounds expert.

Rebuttal: "Fluctuation is exactly why the file does not rest on one data point. Your practice alone was measured at 121 positions [ammo: pins-per-grid]; the book holds 85,910 map points across 710 practices, every one on the same single phrase, 'glasses near me', so no result is a phrase-shopped best case [ammo: map-points, one-phrase]. Fluctuation moves a pin or two. It does not move 358 practices out of the top 3 at their own front door [ammo: outside-top3], and it does not explain 16 practices never found at even one of their 121 positions [ammo: never-found]. Noise is small and random. This is large and consistent."

Live-receipt move: open their competitor report and count the red together. Then ask: "How much fluctuation would it take to turn this grid green?"

R9. "We are in a small town. Maps do not matter here."

Why it lands: the owner pictures an empty market where everyone already knows them.

Rebuttal: "The file measured your town, not a theory about towns. The median client grid in this book holds 98 competing businesses, and the 90th percentile holds 203 [ammo: grid-crowding]. Grids were calibrated per market, most at 1 to 2 miles [ammo: grid-radius-calibration], so nobody's result is an artifact of a big-city radius. And the demand is measured too: 'glasses near me' averaged 71 against 23 for 'optometrist near me' in Google Trends over the past year and won all 53 of 53 weeks [ammo: trends-demand-gap, trends-53-of-53]. Small towns still search. The map still decides who they call."

Live-receipt move: open their competitor report and count the competing businesses on their own grid with them. Their number is on their listing; it is rarely small.

R10. "If your data is so good, why is it free? What are you selling?"

Why it lands: nobody trusts a free lunch from a competitor.

Rebuttal: "The file is free because charging for it would kill what makes it useful. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included [ammo: anchor-verbatim]. Nobody named in a file can pay to change one, and that includes your agency. What the desk offers next is separate, and it is free too: a full audit, a second opinion on what to do with what the file shows, fifteen minutes or an hour, your call, with no pitch unless the fit is real for both sides. The record does not change whether you ever hire us or not. That separation is the point, and it is also your protection: the file grades our future work by the same public standards it graded theirs."

Live-receipt move: show that their file and their competitor report are publicly reachable with no login, no email gate, no payment, right now, on their own phone.

R11. "Our patients come from referrals, not Google."

Why it lands: for established practices it is partially true, and it flatters the practice.

Rebuttal: "Keep the referrals; nobody is proposing to replace them. But the searches are happening with or without you: 'glasses near me' out-searched 'optometrist near me' every week for a year, 53 of 53, and peaked at its maximum the week of 2026-02-08 [ammo: trends-53-of-53, trends-peak]. In this book, 288 of 710 client maps are led by a national chain [ammo: chain-led-maps], and America's Best alone leads 82 of them [ammo: americas-best]. The chain is not waiting for referrals. Every referred patient who searches your name still lands on a site the file grades, and 211 client sites in this book have no way to book at all [ammo: no-booking-path]."

Live-receipt move: open their grid and name the chain sitting in their top 10, if one is there (654 of 710 grids have one [ammo: chain-top10]). A named competitor on their own map beats any abstraction.

R12. "Fine, but my rank is good. Why would I leave a vendor that ranks me?"

Why it lands: for the 352 who rank, the map argument alone genuinely is not enough [ammo: in-top3].

Rebuttal: "If you rank, the question changes from rank to what you are paying for. In this book, 300 practices rank top 3 while running a FAILING or BROKEN website, which is 85.2 percent of everyone who ranks [ammo: winners-who-should-still-leave]. Even the champions: of those 300, 144 are the number one name on their grid, with a median phone paint time of 9,919 milliseconds [ammo: leaders-on-broken-sites]. The book's sharpest case is a practice that holds the number one spot at all 121 of its 121 pins and still fails all 6 product checks [ammo: pattern-crossover-elkton]. The file does not claim the website produced your rank; the crossover cases are printed to show the opposite, that rank and product are separate purchases. That is the point. The part of the retainer the vendor actually builds is the part that fails its checks, so ask what the product line of the invoice is buying."

If the vendor flips the file's own honesty, "their exhibits prove the product grades do not move rank, so the grades do not matter": take the concession. If the product is not producing the rank, the vendor cannot claim credit for the rank through the product either, and the deliverable itself still fails Google's public bar on 702 of 706 measured sites [ammo: lcp-over-google-bar]. Either the product matters and it fails its checks, or it does not matter and the client is paying for it anyway.

Live-receipt move: open their listing page and read their product lines next to their rank. If they are one of the 300, the contradiction is on their own screen in their own file.

Call scripts

EyeCarePro call scripts

NIS sales desk. Agency set: EyeCarePro (710 practices measured, optometry, phrase "glasses near me"). Every number in this document traces to a stat id in Marketing/nobodyissafe-hub/sales/_ammo/eyecarepro.json. Citations appear as bracketed ammo tags naming the stat id. A number without a citation is a bug; report it, do not say it.

House rules for every call
  1. The anchor, verbatim, whenever the ethics of the file come up: "Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included". The files are free. Nobody named in a file can pay to change one. [ammo: anchor-verbatim]
  2. We are a declared competitor. The book's own boilerplate, verbatim: "not affiliated with, endorsed by, or compensated by any agency named in these files; operated by a competitor who publishes the receipts". [ammo: anchor-verbatim]
  3. Funnel honesty. No sending infrastructure exists. Never promise an email, a sequence, a reminder, a confirmation, or a calendar invite. Never direct a prospect to submit the site's booking form as the primary path; book the free audit verbally on the call and record it yourself. The only live surface is the preview URL. [ammo: funnel.honesty]
  4. The live surfaces you may give out loud:
  • The book: eyecarepro/ [ammo: funnel.bookUrl]
  • The prospect's own listing page: l/eyecarepro/{their-domain}.html (all 710 exist; verified by directory count of Marketing/nobodyissafe-hub/site/l/eyecarepro/ = 710 on 2026-08-21)
  1. No PII. Business names and public measured results only. Never read out anyone's personal email, phone, or an owner's personal name from any internal sheet.
  2. Quote the file, do not assert. The book is published; you are reading a public record aloud, not making claims. [ammo: headline-verbatim]
Pre-call prep (mandatory, 2 minutes)
  • [ ] Pull the prospect's row in the hub finder or listings data. Note the verdict word: NOWHERE, POCKET_NO_REACH, PACK_WITH_TURF, or PACK_NEVER_FIRST. [ammo: verdict-distribution]
  • [ ] Note their finding lines (every one of the 710 listings carries at least one measured problem line). [ammo: every-listing-has-findings]
  • [ ] Copy their competitor-report URL from the book (every practice has its own third-party report link, 710 unique). [ammo: competitor-reports-710]
  • [ ] Have their listing page URL ready to read aloud.
  • [ ] Have Google PageSpeed Insights open in a tab in case the site-speed receipt is needed live.

SCRIPT 1: Cold call to a listed practice

Goal: get the decision maker to open their own file while you are on the phone, then book the free audit.

1.0 Gatekeeper

"Hi, this is [first name] calling about [Practice Name]'s public listing in the Nobody Is Safe files. It is a published measurement of the practice's Google Maps visibility. Who handles the practice's marketing decisions?"

  • IF gatekeeper asks "what is this about": "There is a public file that measured this practice's map ranking at 121 spots around your building [ammo: pins-per-grid]. It is free and it is already published. I want to make sure the owner has seen their own page."
  • IF "they're with a patient / not available": "Understood. When is a better window? I will call back then." Record the window. Do NOT promise to email anything. Go to end.
  • IF put through: go to [1.1].
1.1 Opener (decision maker)

"Thanks for taking the call. Straight version: I work for the desk that published the Nobody Is Safe files. We are a competitor of your marketing vendor, and I will say that plainly up front. We measured all 710 EyeCarePro client practices, including yours, on one phrase, 'glasses near me', at 121 map positions around each practice's own building. 85,910 map points total. Your practice has its own page in that file. Have you seen it?" [ammo: total-practices, one-phrase, pins-per-grid, map-points]

  • IF "yes, I saw it": go to SCRIPT 2 at [2.1] (treat as inbound-aware).
  • IF "no": "Then let me read you what it says, it takes thirty seconds. Are you near a screen? The file is public and free; I would rather you look at it than take my word." Go to [1.2].
  • IF "not interested / is this a sales call": go to [1.3].
1.2 Verdict branch

Read the prospect's verdict word from prep, then branch.

1.2A Verdict is NOWHERE or POCKET_NO_REACH (invisible story; 175 + 183 = 358 practices) [ammo: verdict-distribution, outside-top3]

"Your file says your practice sits outside the top 3 on your own neighborhood map for 'glasses near me'. You are one of 358 of the 710, that is half the client list [ammo: outside-top3]. And 16 practices in the file were never found at even one of their 121 positions [ammo: never-found]. Here is the part that matters: the file links a live third-party competitor report for your exact grid. Type this in and we can look at it together." Read the listing URL. Then:

"[Pause while they open it.] That map is your neighborhood. Every dot is a spot where a patient searched 'glasses near me'. Where do you see your name?"

  • IF they engage with the map: "That is the whole pitch. I did not make that map, the scan did, and the phrase is the one patients actually type: Google Trends has 'glasses near me' averaging 71 against 23 for 'optometrist near me' over the past year, and it won all 53 of 53 weeks [ammo: trends-demand-gap, trends-53-of-53]. If you want it read properly, the audit is free: fifteen minutes or an hour, your call." Go to [1.4].
  • IF "my agency handles that": "The map you are looking at IS your agency's work, measured. If they had an answer to it, half their book would not be outside the top 3 [ammo: outside-top3]." Then use rebuttals.md R2 as needed. Go to [1.4] or [1.3].

1.2B Verdict is PACK_WITH_TURF or PACK_NEVER_FIRST (they rank somewhere; 338 + 14 practices) [ammo: verdict-distribution]

"Your file actually shows you ranking, and I will not pretend otherwise, 352 of the 710 make the top 3 somewhere on their grid [ammo: in-top3]. Two things the file says next. First, fewer than 1 in 4 clients lead their own neighborhood, 165 of 710 [ammo: grid-leaders], and the median client is first choice at about 1 in 20 of its own map positions [ammo: median-pin-shares]. Second, the product: 300 practices rank top 3 AND run a website the file grades FAILING or BROKEN. That is 85.2 percent of everyone who ranks [ammo: winners-who-should-still-leave]. Open your own page with me and let us see which side of that you are on." Read the listing URL.

  • IF their product lines show speed or template findings: "Your listing's own lines say it. Not one of the 710 sites in the book earned the SOUND verdict. Zero [ammo: zero-sound-sites]. Ranking today is not the same as being protected tomorrow: 654 of the 710 grids already have a national chain inside the top 10 [ammo: chain-top10], and 288 grids are led by a chain outright [ammo: chain-led-maps]."
  • IF "we rank, so we're fine": use rebuttals.md R4. Then go to [1.4].
1.3 Pushback branch (cold)
  • "Not interested." "Understood. One sentence and I am gone: the file is free, it is public, it stays published either way, and your page is at [read listing URL]. If you ever want it read properly, call this number back and we will set the free audit on the call. Good day." End. Do not chase.
  • "Send me something by email." "I am not going to promise an email, and here is why: I would rather you see the public record than trust an attachment from a competitor. The address is short, can you write it down right now?" Read the listing URL slowly. IF they refuse: "Then search the practice name in the finder at ./. It is your page, not my pitch." End.
  • "Is this a scam / who are you really?" "Fair question. We are a competitor and the file says so on every page, verbatim: 'not affiliated with, endorsed by, or compensated by any agency named in these files; operated by a competitor who publishes the receipts' [ammo: anchor-verbatim]. Nothing in it is for sale. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included [ammo: anchor-verbatim]. The measurements come with live third-party report links you can open yourself [ammo: competitor-reports-710]."
  • "How did you get my number?" "From the practice's own public listing. Nothing in this call used private data, and your file contains none either: business name and public measured results only."
1.4 Close (cold)

"Here is what I can offer, and it is free. A full audit of your exact file: your grid, your competitor report, and your site's product lines. We can do this two ways. If you want the quick version, I can show you in fifteen minutes every way EyeCarePro is failing you. If you have more time, an hour: I can tell you more about us, why we are auditing other agencies' entire books of business, and how we can help fix the problems EyeCarePro is charging you for. It is a working session, not a slideshow, and nobody pitches you unless the fit is real for both sides. What day this week works?"

  • IF they give a time: repeat it back, record it yourself. "I have you down for [day, time]. To be straight with you: I am not sending a calendar invite or a confirmation email, so please write it down on your end too." End warmly.
  • IF "maybe later": "The file does not expire and neither does the offer. Your page again: [listing URL]. When you are ready, call this number back; audits are set person to person, on the call." End.
  • IF hard no: "Understood. The file stays up because it is a public record, not leverage. Good day." End.

SCRIPT 2: Inbound "I saw the file" call

Goal: triage the caller's state, keep every integrity anchor intact, convert worry or anger into a booked free audit or a clean exit.

2.0 Triage

"Thanks for calling. So I take you to the right place: did you see your own practice's page, and what did the verdict line say?"

Branch on tone and content:

  • Worried or curious ("is this real?"): go to [2.1].
  • Angry ("take this down"): go to [2.2].
  • Skeptical ("my agency says your data is wrong"): go to [2.3].
  • Shopping ("what do you sell?"): go to [2.4].
2.1 Worried: "Is this real?"

"It is real and it is checkable, which matters more. Your page links a live competitor report on a third-party scanning service, one of 710 unique links in the book [ammo: competitor-reports-710]. Open it while we talk." [Walk them to their listing URL, then the report link.]

"What you are looking at is a 121-point scan of your own neighborhood on 'glasses near me' [ammo: pins-per-grid, one-phrase]. The file also audits itself in public: 28 grids are flagged as too tight to contain a real contest, 5 scans were purged, and 2 sites could not be captured, and all of that is disclosed inside the book [ammo: self-audit-disclosed]."

  • IF "what does my verdict mean?": explain their word using the map verdicts (NOWHERE, POCKET_NO_REACH, PACK_WITH_TURF, PACK_NEVER_FIRST) and product bands (SOUND, WEAK, FAILING, BROKEN), and note the two are measured separately, so a map winner can still be BROKEN [ammo: vocabulary.verdictWords].
  • IF "what should I do?": go to [2.5].
2.2 Angry: "Take this down."

"I hear you, and I will give you the desk's actual position rather than soften it. The file is a measured public record. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included [ammo: anchor-verbatim]. Nobody named in a file can pay to change one. That rule is what makes the file worth anything, and it protects you too: your competitors cannot pay to change theirs."

"Two honest things I can point you to. First, everything about your practice in the file is a public measured result, business name and scan data, nothing personal. Second, every number links to a third-party report you can open and check yourself [ammo: competitor-reports-710]. If you believe a measurement is factually wrong, say exactly which line, and check it against the linked report while I am on the phone."

  • IF they check and the number stands: "The scan is the scan. What I can offer is a free audit, a second opinion on what to do about the result, not an edit to the record." Go to [2.5].
  • IF they claim it is stale or wrong: use rebuttals.md R1 verbatim.
  • IF legal threats: "Understood. I will note it. The record states its methodology and its own limits in public [ammo: self-audit-disclosed], and every claim carries its receipt. I am not the person to argue law with, and I will not pretend to be." End politely. Flag internally.
2.3 Skeptical: "My agency says your data is wrong or stale."

Use rebuttals.md R1. Short form for the live call:

"Do not take my word and do not take theirs. Your page links a live third-party competitor report; open it now and we will read it together [ammo: competitor-reports-710]. Same phrase for all 710 practices, 'glasses near me', so nobody was measured on a trick phrase [ammo: one-phrase]. And the demand data is Google's own: 'glasses near me' averaged 71 against 23 for 'optometrist near me', winning all 53 of 53 weeks in the past year [ammo: trends-demand-gap, trends-53-of-53]. If your agency has a fresher scan, ask them to show it as a map grid, pin by pin, the way the file does."

2.4 Shopping: "What do you sell?"

"The file is free and stays free, and so is the audit. Nothing on this call is for sale. We are a competitor of the agencies named in the files, and the book says so on every page [ammo: anchor-verbatim]. We can do the audit two ways. The quick version, fifteen minutes: I show you every way EyeCarePro is failing you, across your grid, your competitor report, and your product lines. The full version, an hour: I tell you more about us, why we are auditing other agencies' entire books of business, and how we can help fix the problems EyeCarePro is charging you for, and that last part only if the fit is real for both sides. If you want that, we book it right now on this call." Go to [2.5].

2.5 Close (inbound)

"Let us set the audit. Fifteen minutes or an hour, your call. What day works?"

  • IF booked: repeat the time back, record it. "Write it down on your end as well; I do not send confirmations, and I would rather tell you that than fake it." End.
  • IF not yet: "Then do one thing before you decide anything: open your competitor report and look at where your name is on your own map. The link is on your page: [listing URL]. That report is worth more than anything I could say." End.

SCRIPT 3: Audit opening (booked free audit)

Goal: open the booked audit cleanly, set the honest frame, and hand off into the working session. This script covers the first ten minutes.

3.0 Frame and disclosure

"Before we look at anything, three sentences about who we are, because the file says them and I want you to hear them from a person. One: this desk is 'not affiliated with, endorsed by, or compensated by any agency named in these files; operated by a competitor who publishes the receipts' [ammo: anchor-verbatim]. Two: the files are free, and nobody named in a file can pay to change one. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included [ammo: anchor-verbatim]. Three: nothing in this session edits your file. The record is the record. What we are here to do is decide what you do about it."

3.1 Walk the three receipts (screen share or side-by-side)
  1. The grid. Open their listing page, then their competitor report [ammo: competitor-reports-710]. "This is your neighborhood, 121 positions, one phrase: 'glasses near me' [ammo: pins-per-grid, one-phrase]. Tell me what you see."
  2. The market. "Two facts about the field you are playing on. The median client grid in this book holds 98 competing businesses [ammo: grid-crowding]. And 654 of the 710 grids have a national chain inside the top 10; America's Best alone leads 82 client grids [ammo: chain-top10, americas-best]."
  3. The product. Read their own finding lines aloud from their listing [ammo: every-listing-has-findings]. Then the book-wide context: "Zero of 710 sites in this book earned the SOUND verdict [ammo: zero-sound-sites]. 702 of 706 measured sites load slower on a phone than Google's 2.5 second bar, and the median is 9,387 milliseconds [ammo: lcp-over-google-bar, median-mobile-lcp]. If you want, we run PageSpeed on your site right now and watch the number come back live."
3.2 Discovery branch

3.2A They rank (PACK verdicts)

"You are one of the 352 that make the top 3 somewhere [ammo: in-top3]. The question for this hour is whether your rank is built or inherited. In this book, 300 practices rank top 3 while running a FAILING or BROKEN site, and 144 of those are the number one name on their grid [ammo: winners-who-should-still-leave, leaders-on-broken-sites]. Rank without product means the next competitor with both takes your turf. So: what do you believe is producing your rank today?"

Listen. Then: "And if your agency were producing it, would the median ranking client be first choice at 1 in 20 of its own pins [ammo: median-pin-shares]?"

3.2B They are invisible (NOWHERE or POCKET_NO_REACH)

"Your file puts you with the 358 outside the top 3 [ammo: outside-top3]. I want to be precise about what that costs. 'Glasses near me' out-searched 'optometrist near me' every single week for a year, 53 of 53, averaging 71 to 23 [ammo: trends-53-of-53, trends-demand-gap]. The demand peaked at 100 the week of 2026-02-08 [ammo: trends-peak]. Every week you are absent, that traffic books somewhere. Walk me through what your agency has told you they are doing about the map, in their words."

3.2C They are loyal to the incumbent

"Then use this hour as a second opinion, and take the questions home instead of my pitch. We keep a list of questions your agency should be able to answer, each one tied to a measured number in the file, and what a real answer looks like. If they answer them well, you will know. If they dodge, you will know that too." Hand over fence-sitter-questions.md content.

3.3 Handoff and guardrails

Hand off to the auditor for the working session. Before you do, the guardrails, said out loud if needed:

  • Do not promise follow-up mechanics that do not exist. No "we'll email you the plan", no sequences, no reminders. If the prospect wants the material, they leave with the URLs written down.
  • Do not promise edits, removals, re-scans on a schedule, or any change to the file. The record is independent of the sale. That separation is the product.
  • Do not name any client's owner personally, read out any private contact data, or reference internal sheets. Business names and public measured results only.
  • Next-step commitments must be things that exist today: another call at a set time, or, only if the fit is real for both sides, the desk's proposal delivered in a way you can personally guarantee.
