THE PHRASE chiropractor near me · open the live book

Fence-sitter questions

DemandBoost fence-sitter questions

NIS sales desk. Twelve questions a wavering DemandBoost client asks the agency, on the record, before deciding to stay or go. Generated 2026-08-21. Every question is armed with the file: the receipt that makes it hard, the dodge the agency will reach for, and what a real answer would look like. Every number traces to Marketing/nobodyissafe-hub/sales/_ammo/demandboost.json (cited as [ammo: stat-id]); each stat there carries its on-disk source and extraction command.

How to use this list. Ask on your next agency call, with your own file open: your listing at l/demandboost/{your-domain}.html and the competitor report it links. Ask, then stop talking. A dodge is an answer. You do not need all twelve; three unanswered is a decision.

Why these are fair questions. The desk that built them is a competitor and says so on every page. But the questions are not opinions; each one stands on a measured, published number, and nobody named in a file can pay to change one. The files are free. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included. If the agency believes a number is wrong, the hub's dispute page is open to them. The desk's standing rule: if the file is wrong, the file changes on the record with a dated note.

1. "Show me, on a live map, where my practice ranks for 'chiropractor near me' around my own building. Right now, on this call."
  • Receipt: this is exactly what the file already did: a 121-point geo-grid over every scanned

    • client's own neighborhood, same phrase, same instrument, all 95 clients [ammo: grid-methodology]. The median client sits at position 8.9 on its own map, and the pack patients see is positions 1 to 3 [ammo: median-position]. Your card links your own live third-party report; the agency has no excuse not to have the equivalent.

  • Predicted dodge: a pivot to a dashboard, a keyword-position PDF, or "rankings fluctuate,

    • maps are not reliable." A dashboard is not a map. The pivot IS the answer.

  • A real answer looks like: a live geo-grid on screen, your neighborhood, the phrase

    • 'chiropractor near me', today's date, your pin positions readable. If they cannot produce one, open yours: it is linked from your listing card.

2. "Of your 97 chiropractic clients, how many actually own 'chiropractor near me' on their own local map? Give me the number."
  • Receipt: the file's headline, published and verbatim: "One agency, 97 chiropractic

    • clients, one recycled template. 82 of the 95 clients scanned do not own “chiropractor near me” on their own local map, and the median scanned client holds a 5.8% share of it." [ammo: book-headline-verbatim, not-own-phrase] The answer is 13 of 95. They know it or they should.

  • Predicted dodge: a cherry-pick: one or two winner names offered as proof. The file

    • pre-empts this: the winners exist (13, publicly listed) and their common trait is a median of 200 Google reviews against a roster median of 49, an asset patients built [ammo: winners-13, winners-carried-by-reviews].

  • A real answer looks like: a roster-wide count with a definition attached, and an honest

    • account of the 82. Any answer that is a story about one client instead of a number about 95 confirms the base rate.

3. "Who is the number one result around my own building, and why is it not me?"
  • Receipt: only 8 of 95 scanned clients lead their own grid; the other 87 grids are led by

    • somebody else, and 54 distinct businesses hold those leads [ammo: own-area-leader-8, distinct-leaders-54]. The Joint Chiropractic, a franchise that is not even on the roster, leads 23 of the 95 [ammo: joint-leads-23]. Your own leader's name is printed on your competitor report.

  • Predicted dodge: "franchises have deep pockets, nobody beats them." False on this very

    • roster: 72 of the 95 grids are led by someone other than The Joint, and 54 distinct businesses hold the 87 leads clients do not [ammo: distinct-leaders-54].

  • A real answer looks like: the leader named without you telling them, plus a specific,

    • dated plan for closing the gap on your grid. If they cannot name your leader, they have never looked at your map.

4. "How many seconds does my website take to show its content on a phone, and what is Google's bar?"
  • Receipt: all 97 of 97 client sites fail Google's 2.5 second mobile bar; the fastest on the

    • entire roster is 2.87 seconds; the median is 10.5 seconds; 52 of 97 take more than 10 seconds; the slowest takes 52.7 [ammo: speed-all-97-fail, speed-median, speed-over-10s, speed-worst]. Your own number is one of the finding lines on your listing card.

  • Predicted dodge: a desktop score. The roster's desktop median is 2.4 seconds and 60 of

    • 100, against 10.5 seconds and 48 on the phone; the desktop number is the flattering one and the irrelevant one, because patients search on phones [ammo: speed-desktop-gap].

  • A real answer looks like: the mobile number, stated next to the 2.5 second bar, with a

    • remediation date. You can verify any answer yourself in 60 seconds on Google's own PageSpeed tool.

5. "How many other practices run my exact website template? And does your own homepage run it too?"
  • Receipt: 83 of the 97 client sites run one of just 12 shared static templates. The largest

    • template family holds 23 client sites AND DemandBoost's own homepage [ammo: templates-83-of-97, agency-on-same-template]. 50 client sites load a stylesheet literally named css/demandboost.css; View Source on your own homepage and search for it [ammo: css-named-after-agency].

  • Predicted dodge: "we use a proven framework, that is industry standard." A framework is

    • code. A template family is the same design, same structure, same stylesheet, sold separately to 23 practices as their website.

  • A real answer looks like: an exact count, an admission of which family your site is in,

    • and what about your site was actually built for your practice. If the answer to the second half is yes, ask what your monthly fee is buying that their own homepage gets for free.

6. "Which sentences on my homepage were written for my practice, and which appear word for word on other clients' sites?"
  • Receipt: 172 distinct sentences of 15 or more words appear word for word on 2 or more

    • different client domains [ammo: sentences-172]. One example sentence appears verbatim on chirocarenw.com, oaklandpaininjurycenter.com and sanleandrochiropractors.com [ammo: copy-clusters-triplets]. 8 title formulas are stamped across the roster with only the city swapped [ammo: city-swap-titles].

  • Predicted dodge: "some duplication is normal in local SEO" or "that is placeholder copy."

    • Duplicate content at this scale is a measured liability, and the page title, the most visible SEO element there is, being a mail-merge field is not a placeholder.

  • A real answer looks like: a sentence-level audit of your own homepage against the rest of

    • their book, with a count. If they will not run it, the file already did.

7. "What phrase did you optimize my practice for, and what is its search demand next to 'chiropractor near me'?"
  • Receipt: 36 clients were rescanned on a narrow niche phrase; 25 of 36 hold a winning share

    • there while holding little or nothing on 'chiropractor near me'; 24 clients hold zero percent of the money phrase alongside a real share of a niche one. Sharpest case: 100% of 'auto accident chiropractor', 0% of 'chiropractor near me' [ammo: niche-dominance-25, wrong-phrase-24]. Demand side: 'chiropractor near me' averages 56 against 29 for 'back pain near me' on Google Trends and leads in 48 of 53 weeks [ammo: trends-category-vs-symptom, trends-weeks-lead].

  • Predicted dodge: "long-tail phrases convert better." Conversion of traffic that does not

    • exist is zero. The book's own line: "We measured the door with the traffic, and 36 of the 95 clinics scanned are invisible on it." [ammo: trends-book-tie]

  • A real answer looks like: the phrase named, its volume shown next to the category phrase's

    • volume from a public source, and a defensible reason the smaller door was chosen. "It was easier to rank" is an admission, not a strategy.

8. "Walk me through my invoice. Which line item was supposed to get me into the map pack, and what did that line item measurably do?"
  • Receipt: the outcome side is measured: the median scanned client holds a 5.8% share of

    • 'chiropractor near me' on its own grid, 82 of 95 do not own the phrase, and 48 of 95 never hit number one at even one of their 121 points [ammo: median-share, not-own-phrase, never-rank-one]. Whatever the invoice says, this is what it bought at the median.

  • Predicted dodge: bundle language: "holistic SEO", "it all works together", "brand

    • building." Bundles are how no single line ever has to answer for a result.

  • A real answer looks like: line item, metric, before, after. Any vendor confident in the

    • work can map money to movement. If no line item owns the map, nobody is being paid to win it, and your grid shows what that buys.

9. "If I cancel today, what exactly do I keep? The website, the content, the domain, the reviews. Itemize it."
  • Receipt: 79 of the 83 audited client sites run on the agency's custom static stack, so the

    • site is theirs, not yours [ammo: platform-static-79]. What you would be walking away from is measured: a site failing Google's phone bar like all 97 on the roster, on a median 10.5 second load [ammo: speed-all-97-fail, speed-median], with 172 sentences shared across the roster [ammo: sentences-172]. What you keep is the asset that actually correlates with winning: reviews. The book verbatim: "Those reviews are an asset their patients built. It belongs to the practice, and it moves with the practice, not with the invoice." [ammo: reviews-move-with-you, winners-carried-by-reviews]

  • Predicted dodge: vagueness: "we can discuss an export", "there is a transition process."

    • Vagueness on ownership is the lock-in talking.

  • A real answer looks like: a written list: domain yours or theirs, site portable or not,

    • content licensed or owned, profile and reviews controlled by whom. Every item they cannot put in writing is an item you already do not own.

10. "Why does my website's footer link to demandboost.com, and whose search ranking is that helping?"
  • Receipt: 90 of 97 client homepages carry a footer link back to demandboost.com, and 89

    • print a visible DemandBoost attribution line on the client's own page [ammo: footer-links-90, attribution-89]. Scroll to your own footer mid-call and look.

  • Predicted dodge: "industry-standard credit line." The file measures presence, not what

    • the link passes: 90 of 97 client homepages carry it, on pages the clients pay for. A credit can be a name without a link; ask why it needs to be a link to their domain at all.

  • A real answer looks like: an immediate offer to remove it, plus a straight answer about

    • why the agency's name on your page needs to point at their domain. Watch how fast a thing that is "standard" can be deleted when a client asks on the record.

11. "Can a patient book an appointment on my website right now? How many of your client sites have no online booking at all?"
  • Receipt: 36 of the 83 audited client sites have no online booking path at all

    • [ammo: no-booking-36]. So even a patient who finds the site and waits out a 10 second phone load [ammo: speed-over-10s] may hit a dead end where revenue should be.

  • Predicted dodge: "chiropractic patients prefer to call." Preference data for your patients

    • will not be produced, because the missing feature is being reframed as a choice.

  • A real answer looks like: yes, demonstrated on your phone during the call, from your

    • homepage to a booked slot. If it is no: a date by which it becomes yes, and an explanation of what the monthly fee has been covering instead.

12. "Your winning clients: what do they have in common? Your work, or their reviews?"
  • Receipt: the file measured exactly this. The 13 winners hold a median of 200 Google

    • reviews against a roster median of 49; 12 of 13 sit above the roster median. The best result in the book, a Livermore practice at 90.08% share, has 451 reviews, more than 9 times the median [ammo: winners-carried-by-reviews, top-winner-profile]. And the agency's work does not even hold up inside the winner set: every one of the 13 winner cards still carries a speed flag, including a winner at 73.55% share on a 25.7 second phone load [ammo: winner-cards-still-flagged, speed-even-winners-fail].

  • Predicted dodge: claiming the reviews as their program's output, or "correlation is not

    • causation." Then ask which invoice line generated your patients' reviews, and why the same program left 82 of 95 clients not owning the phrase [ammo: not-own-phrase].

  • A real answer looks like: an honest attribution: what the agency built, what the patients

    • built, and evidence for the first category that is not the second category wearing a badge. If the honest answer is "the winners brought their own moat", you have your decision.

Three dodges is a pattern. The file, your listing, and your competitor report stay up and stay free whatever you decide, and if any number in them is wrong, the dispute page changes the record with a dated note. When you want the numbers walked, the desk offers a free audit, fifteen minutes or an hour, your choice; nobody pitches you unless the fit is real, and the people who measured your grid will be the ones reading it with you.

Rebuttals

DemandBoost rebuttals

NIS sales desk. Objections a DemandBoost client will actually raise, with the rebuttal, the receipt, and the live move. Generated 2026-08-21. Every number traces to Marketing/nobodyissafe-hub/sales/_ammo/demandboost.json (cited as [ammo: stat-id]); each stat there carries its on-disk source and extraction command.

The standing live-receipt move. Every rebuttal below ends the same way: open THAT client's own competitor report on the call (pattern https://www.localrankingtracker.com/competitor-report/{scanId}/fe26e61e3a458c7/, linked from their card; every one of the 95 scanned clients has one [ammo funnel]). We do not win arguments; the grid does. Exception: the 2 Not measured clients have no report; for them the live receipt is their listing page and the book's roster numbers.

The standing disclosure. If any rebuttal drifts toward "trust us": stop. We are the competitor who publishes the receipts, the files are free, and nobody named in a file can pay to change one. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included.

1. "My agency says your data is wrong or stale."

What is underneath: they want permission to not look.

Rebuttal:

"Then let's not use my data. Let's use the receipt. Your card links a live third-party report:
a 121-point grid scan of your own neighborhood, the same instrument for all 95 scanned clients,
same phrase, no exceptions. The desk's exported data file independently reproduces the book's
core counts: 36 invisible clients, 48 clients with zero number-one pins, The Joint leading 23
grids. Two files, two methods, same numbers. And if any number is actually wrong, the hub's
dispute page holds the desk to it in print: disputes re-measured, corrections dated, on the
record, no silent edits. If the file is wrong, the file changes on the record with a dated
note. Ask your agency to file the dispute. Wrong data gets corrected; correct data gets
called stale."

Receipts: [ammo: grid-methodology] 121 points, one phrase, every client. [ammo: csv-crosscheck] 36 / 48 / 23 independently confirmed in a second file. Live move: open their competitor report and let them count their own pins. A stale-data claim does not survive a live scan page with a date on it.

2. "You're a competitor. Of course you'd say this."

What is underneath: motive as a reason to dismiss the math.

Rebuttal:

"Correct, and we print it on every page: operated by a competitor who publishes the receipts.
Motive is why you should check the numbers, not why you should ignore them. So check: the file
names 13 of your agency's clients Market winners, publicly, with their winning shares from
53.72% up to 90.08%. A hit piece does not publish the other side's wins. And nothing here is
for sale: the files are free, and nobody named in a file can pay to change one. Nobody pays to
be excluded and nobody pays to be included. Our motive does not move a single pin on your grid."

Receipts: [ammo: winners-13] 13 winners published with SOLV range. [ammo: winner-cards-still-flagged] winners get honest cards too. Live move: open their competitor report. Then open one winner's listing to show the file praises where praise is measured.

3. "They do more than maps. Website, content, SEO, everything."

What is underneath: bundle language covering for no line item having a measured result.

Rebuttal:

"Let's audit the bundle piece by piece, from the file. The website: all 97 of 97 fail Google's
2.5 second phone bar, median load 10.5 seconds, and 83 of the 97 run one of just 12 shared
templates. The content: 172 sentences repeat word for word across different client sites. The
SEO: 82 of 95 scanned clients do not own 'chiropractor near me' at their own front door, and
the median client holds a 5.8% share. Which part of 'more than maps' is working?"

Receipts: [ammo: speed-all-97-fail, speed-median, templates-83-of-97, sentences-172, not-own-phrase, median-share] Live move: open their competitor report for the maps claim, then View Source on their own homepage for the template claim: 50 sites load a stylesheet named css/demandboost.css [ammo: css-named-after-agency].

4. "I rank fine. My agency sends me reports."

What is underneath: agency-authored reporting vs third-party measurement.

Rebuttal:

"Then the reports and the map should agree. On this roster, the median client's average
position on its own grid is 8.9, and the pack patients see is positions 1 to 3. 42 of 95
scanned clients average worse than position 10 around their own building, and 48 of 95 never
hit number one at a single one of their 121 points. If your report says number one, ask: number
one where, on what phrase, measured by whom? Your card's report is measured by a third party at
121 named points. Put them side by side."

Receipts: [ammo: median-position, worse-than-10, never-rank-one] Live move: open their competitor report next to the agency's PDF. Same practice, same city. One of them is a map.

5. "My agency ranked me number one for my specialty phrase."

What is underneath: real rankings on the wrong door.

Rebuttal:

"The file saw that pattern and measured it. 36 clients were rescanned on their narrow niche
phrase; 25 of the 36 hold a winning share there while holding little or nothing on
'chiropractor near me'. 24 clients hold zero percent of the money phrase and a real share of a
niche one. The sharpest case in the book: a clinic at 100% of 'auto accident chiropractor' and
0% of 'chiropractor near me'. The agency can rank things. It ranked the phrase nobody types:
on Google Trends, 'chiropractor near me' out-scores 'back pain near me' 56 to 29 and leads in
48 of 53 weeks. A perfect score on the empty street is still an empty street."

Receipts: [ammo: niche-dominance-25, wrong-phrase-24, trends-category-vs-symptom, trends-weeks-lead] Live move: open their competitor report, which is scanned on the money phrase, and ask which phrase their number one certificate was for.

6. "Switching is painful. I'd lose my website and start over."

What is underneath: fear that leaving costs more than staying.

Rebuttal:

"Inventory what you would actually lose. The website: 79 of the 83 audited sites run on the
agency's custom static stack, so yes, it stays with them, and the file says what you would be
keeping: a site that fails Google's phone bar like all 97 on the roster, on a median 10.5
second load. The content: 172 sentences of it are shared word for word with other practices.
Now the asset that actually wins maps on this roster: reviews. The 13 winners hold a median of
200 Google reviews against a roster median of 49. The book's own words: 'Those reviews are an
asset their patients built. It belongs to the practice, and it moves with the practice, not
with the invoice.' The thing that works is the thing you keep."

Receipts: [ammo: platform-static-79, speed-all-97-fail, speed-median, sentences-172, winners-carried-by-reviews, reviews-move-with-you] Live move: open their competitor report, then their Google profile: their review count is the portable asset, on screen, next to the map the site never won.

7. "My website looks fine to me."

What is underneath: they checked it on a desktop.

Rebuttal:

"It probably does, on the screen you checked it on. The roster's desktop median is a 2.4 second
load with a 60 score. The phone median is 10.5 seconds with a 48 score. These sites were built
for the screen patients do not search on. 52 of 97 take more than 10 seconds on a phone; the
slowest, a clinic in Pleasanton, takes 52.7 seconds. Pull your own phone out, load your site on
cellular, and count."

Receipts: [ammo: speed-desktop-gap, speed-over-10s, speed-worst] Live move: their competitor report plus a live phone load of their own homepage, counted out loud on the call.

8. "I get patients from referrals. The map doesn't matter for me."

What is underneath: the map's value has never been shown to them in their own numbers.

Rebuttal:

"Referrals are yours; nobody measured those and I will not pretend to. The map is a separate
door, and Google's own data sizes it: 'chiropractor near me' runs 56 against 29 for 'back pain
near me' on Trends, ahead in 48 of 53 weeks. That demand walks into somebody's practice every
week. On your grid it walks into whoever leads it, and on this roster the grid leader is the
client themselves only 8 times out of 95. If the door were worthless, The Joint Chiropractic
would not be spending to hold the top of 23 of your agency's client maps."

Receipts: [ammo: trends-category-vs-symptom, trends-weeks-lead, own-area-leader-8, joint-leads-23] Live move: open their competitor report and name the practice at the top of THEIR grid. "That is who your referral moat is up against, every day, for free."

9. "Nobody can beat The Joint anyway. It's a national franchise."

What is underneath: learned helplessness, often planted by the incumbent.

Rebuttal:

"The file says otherwise twice. First: The Joint leads 23 of the 95 grids, which means 72 are
led by someone else, and 54 distinct businesses hold leads across this roster. The franchise
is one name on a leader list of 54. Second: 13 of your agency's own clients DO own their maps,
with shares up to 90.08%. The market is winnable; it is being won, on your agency's own roster,
mostly by practices whose patients built them a review moat: median 200 reviews against 49.
The question is not whether the map can be won. It is why your retainer has not won yours."

Receipts: [ammo: joint-leads-23, distinct-leaders-54, winners-13, winners-carried-by-reviews, top-winner-profile] Live move: their competitor report. If their leader is The Joint, open the book's leader table and show that 72 of the 95 grids are led by someone else, 54 distinct names. If their leader is a local practice, the franchise excuse is dead on arrival.

10. "I've been with them for years. They know my practice."

What is underneath: loyalty priced as personal service.

Rebuttal:

"Then the work should look personal. The file measured that: 83 of 97 sites run one of 12
shared templates. The largest family holds 23 client sites plus DemandBoost's own homepage.
One sentence appears word for word on three different practices' homepages, and there are 172
such repeated sentences across the roster. 37 sites share a single accessibility-widget vendor
account ID. 8 title formulas are stamped across the book with only the city swapped. That is
not a team that knows your practice; that is a mail merge that knows your city name."

Receipts: [ammo: templates-83-of-97, agency-on-same-template, copy-clusters-triplets, sentences-172, userway-shared-account, city-swap-titles] Live move: their competitor report, then read the triplet sentence aloud and name the three domains carrying it: chirocarenw.com, oaklandpaininjurycenter.com, sanleandrochiropractors.com. Ask whether their homepage has a sibling too.

11. "I already paid for the year. I'll look at it at renewal."

What is underneath: sunk cost plus no felt urgency.

Rebuttal:

"The invoice is sunk either way. The question is what the months between now and renewal cost.
Every month, per the file: your site loads past Google's bar like all 97 on the roster, your
grid is led by whoever leads it today, and 36 of the 83 audited sites cannot even take a
booking online. Meanwhile 90 of 97 client homepages carry a footer link to demandboost.com,
pointed at their domain from a page you pay for, while you wait. The
audit is free, fifteen minutes or an hour, and does not require cancelling anything. Waiting is
the only option on the table with a monthly price."

Receipts: [ammo: speed-all-97-fail, no-booking-36, footer-links-90] Live move: their competitor report, then scroll their own footer together to the demandboost.com link [ammo: attribution-89].

12. "Your file even shows winners. Maybe I'll be one eventually."

What is underneath: the 13 winners read as proof the model works if you wait.

Rebuttal:

"Look at what the winners have in common, because the file did. The 13 winners hold a median
of 200 Google reviews against a roster median of 49, and 12 of the 13 sit above the roster
median. The single best result in the book, a Livermore practice at 90.08%, has 451 reviews,
more than nine times the median. The winners are carried by an asset the agency did not build. And even
they are not being served: every one of the 13 winner cards still carries a speed flag; one
winner holds 73.55% of its map on a site that takes 25.7 seconds to load. Waiting inside this
roster does not make you a winner. Reviews made the winners, and reviews are yours already."

Receipts: [ammo: winners-carried-by-reviews, top-winner-profile, winner-cards-still-flagged, speed-even-winners-fail] Live move: their competitor report next to their own review count. Chart their own path on the call: the winners' median is 200; ask what number they are at today.

Call scripts

DemandBoost call scripts

NIS sales desk. Agency set: DemandBoost (hub code DB-097, chiropractic, 97 clients). Generated 2026-08-21. Every number in this document traces to a stat in Marketing/nobodyissafe-hub/sales/_ammo/demandboost.json (cited inline as [ammo: stat-id]); each stat in that sheet carries its own on-disk source file and extraction command. If a number is not in the ammo sheet, it is not said on a call.

Ground rules for every call

Who we are, said plainly. We are the desk that measured the file. The book's own boilerplate, verbatim: "not published by or affiliated with DemandBoost" and "not affiliated with, endorsed by, or compensated by any agency named in these files; operated by a competitor who publishes the receipts." Say the competitor part yourself before the prospect finds it. It lands better from us.

The anchors, verbatim, whenever the integrity of the file comes up:

  • "Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included."
  • The files are free.
  • Nobody named in a file can pay to change one.

Funnel truth box. What exists and what does not:

Exists nowDoes NOT exist. Never promise it
The book at demandboost/Email follow-up, sequences, reminders. No sending infrastructure exists
The hub at ./Delivered form submissions. The booking form endpoint is owner-owed and may be empty
A listing page for all 97 clients at l/demandboost/{domain}.html [ammo: per-listing-pages]Confirmation emails or calendar invites
A live third-party competitor report per scanned client, pattern https://www.localrankingtracker.com/competitor-report/{scanId}/fe26e61e3a458c7/ [ammo funnel]nobodyissafe.com. The real domain is dark. Only the preview URLs above are live
The dispute page at dispute/ (its printed docket: "Disputes re-measured · Corrections dated, on the record · No silent edits")Any paid path to change a file

Consequence: every link is walked to LIVE on the call, read aloud character by character if needed. Every audit is set verbally on the call and the rep records it in the desk calendar. Say "I am writing this time down now"; never say "you will get a confirmation email."

PII rule. Business names and public measured results only. Never read out an owner's personal name, an email address, or a phone number from any internal sheet.

Pre-call prep, no exceptions:

  1. Open the prospect's listing page and their competitor-report link.
  2. Note their verdict: Market winner (13 clients), Partial (46), Invisible (36), or Not measured (2) [ammo: segment-split].
  3. Note their two listing finding lines: the phone-load line (on 95 of 97 cards) and the

    4. "Found at N of 121 map spots" line (on 82 cards) [ammo: listing-card-lines].

  4. Pick your branch below by verdict before dialing.
Script 1: Cold call to a listed practice

Opening (identical for all verdicts)

"Hi, this is [rep] calling from the Nobody Is Safe desk. We are not selling anything on this
call and I will be straight with you about who we are: we publish measured records of marketing
agencies' client rosters, and your practice is one of 97 in the DemandBoost file. Your listing
is public and free. I am calling because it is your data, and you probably have not seen it.
Do you have ninety seconds?"

IF gatekeeper answers:

"It is about a published measurement of the practice's Google Maps visibility. Nothing to buy.
Who handles the practice's marketing decisions, and when are they at the desk?"
  • Get a name and a time. Do not pitch the gatekeeper. Do not leave the URL with the gatekeeper;

    • the finding lines are the decision maker's to see first.

IF decision maker: continue by verdict branch.

Branch V-INV: verdict Invisible (36 clients)

"The file scanned 121 map points around your own building on the phrase 'chiropractor near me'.
Your practice showed up in the top 3 at zero of them. Not slipping. Absent. And you are not
alone: 36 of the 95 DemandBoost clients scanned came back Invisible."

[ammo: invisible-36, grid-methodology]

If they go quiet or say "that can't be right", go to the live-receipt move below.

Branch V-PART: verdict Partial (46 clients)

"The file scanned 121 map points around your own building on 'chiropractor near me'. You show
up in some spots and are gone in the rest. The median DemandBoost client holds a 5.8% share of
that phrase around its own office, and sits at position 8.9 on its own map. The map pack
patients actually see is positions 1 to 3."

[ammo: median-share, median-position]

"One more number: 82 of the 95 clients scanned do not own that phrase on their own local map."

[ammo: not-own-phrase]

Branch V-WIN: verdict Market winner (13 clients)

Do not pretend they are losing the map. The file calls them a winner and so do we.

"Your practice is one of only 13 in the file that actually owns 'chiropractor near me' around
its own building. The file says so publicly; winners are called winners. I am calling about the
other line on your card. Every one of the 13 winner cards still carries a speed flag. Your site,
like all 97 of 97 on this roster, fails Google's 2.5 second phone-load bar. The fastest site on
the entire roster loads in 2.87 seconds."

[ammo: winner-cards-still-flagged, speed-all-97-fail]

"And the file's own read on WHY you win: the 13 winners have a median of 200 Google reviews
against a roster median of 49. Those reviews are an asset your patients built, not the agency."

[ammo: winners-carried-by-reviews]

Branch V-NM: verdict Not measured (2 clients)

"Your practice is in the file but is one of 2 of the 97 not yet map-scanned, so I will not
claim a single number about your map. What the file does show is the roster you are on: 82 of
the 95 clients that were scanned do not own 'chiropractor near me' around their own building,
and all 97 websites, including the 2 unscanned clients' sites, fail Google's phone speed bar."

[ammo: clients-total, not-own-phrase, speed-all-97-fail]

  • No competitor report exists for these 2. The live receipt is the listing page and the book.

Common branches (any verdict)

IF "Is this a sales call?"

"It is a call from a competitor, and I told you that up front. But nothing on this call is for
sale. The file is free, and nobody named in it can pay to change it. Nobody pays to be excluded
and nobody pays to be included. What I want is for you to read your own page. If it makes you
want to talk, the audit is free, fifteen minutes or an hour, your choice, and nobody pitches
you unless the fit is real. That is the whole funnel."

IF "How did you get this data?" or "Says who?"

"Every number has a receipt. Your card links a live third-party competitor report: a 121-point
grid scan of your own neighborhood run through localrankingtracker.com. This book alone is
15,851 measured map points, and the same numbers are cross-checked in a second exported data
file. I can open your report with you right now."

[ammo: map-points-total, csv-crosscheck, grid-methodology]

  • Then do the live-receipt move.

IF "I'm busy."

"Understood. One sentence and I am gone: your practice has a public measured file at nobody is
safe, and I would rather you read it than hear it from me. Can I read you the address of your
page, or set ten minutes this week?"
  • If neither: log the attempt, do not re-pitch. No follow-up mechanics exist to promise.

IF "My agency handles this. I'll ask them."

"Good. Ask them with the file open. And ask them the one question that matters first: open my
competitor report with me, live, on a map. If they show you a dashboard instead of a map, that
is the answer. The desk keeps a full question list for exactly that conversation; I walk you
through it at the audit."
  • Route them to the fence-sitter questions in the audit. Do not read all 12 on a cold call.

The live-receipt move (the spine of the call)

"Do you have a browser open? I will read you a link. It is your own report, not our website."

Walk them into their competitor report. Narrate what they see:

"That grid is your neighborhood. Each pin is one of the 121 points we measured. The name at
the top is the practice that leads your map. Is it you?"

Only 8 of 95 scanned clients lead their own grid; 87 grids are led by someone else, and 54 distinct businesses hold those leads. The Joint Chiropractic, a national franchise that is not even a DemandBoost client, leads 23 of the 95 [ammo: own-area-leader-8, distinct-leaders-54, joint-leads-23]. Whatever name they see, you have the number for it.

Close

"Two things, both free. One: your listing page, read it tonight. Two: a full audit, a second
opinion on your own numbers. We can do it two ways. The quick version, fifteen minutes: I show
you every way DemandBoost is failing you. The full version, an hour: I tell you more about us,
why we are auditing other agencies' entire books of business, and how we can help fix the
problems DemandBoost is charging you for, if the fit is real. I can set a time with you right
now, on this call. What does Thursday look like?"
  • Set the time verbally. Record it in the desk calendar yourself, on the call.
  • Say: "I am writing this down now. If anything changes, this number reaches me."
  • Do NOT say: "you'll get an invite", "I'll email you", "we'll send a reminder." None of that

    • infrastructure exists.

Script 2: Inbound "I saw the file" call

Someone calls the desk having found their listing, the book, or a share card. First job: identify which caller you have. Ask:

"Glad you called. Tell me what you were looking at, and is it your practice?"

Branch I-1: A listed client, worried or curious

"Then you have seen your two lines. Let me give you the context around them."

Give the roster frame in three numbers, then stop:

"82 of the 95 clients scanned do not own 'chiropractor near me' on their own local map. The
median client holds a 5.8% share. And all 97 websites on the roster fail Google's phone speed
bar. Your card sits inside that picture."

[ammo: not-own-phrase, median-share, speed-all-97-fail]

Then the live-receipt move from Script 1: open THEIR competitor report together while they are already warm. Close on the audit, same mechanics as Script 1.

IF they ask "what do I do about it?"

"That is the audit. On this call I will not pretend to diagnose you in five minutes. The file
tells you what is true; the audit is where the people who measured your grid walk your numbers
and what to do about them. It is free, fifteen minutes or an hour, your choice, and nobody
pitches you unless the fit is real."

Branch I-2: Angry. "Take my listing down."

Stay flat. Read the policy, do not improvise it:

"I hear you, and I will tell you exactly how the file works. The files are free. Nobody pays
to be excluded and nobody pays to be included. Nobody named in a file can pay to change one.
There is one path that changes a file: if the data is wrong. The hub has a dispute page, and
the docket printed on it reads: disputes re-measured, corrections dated, on the record, no
silent edits. The desk's standing rule behind that page: if the file is wrong, the file
changes on the record with a dated note. If your numbers are wrong, I want that dispute filed
today."
"But before you file, look at the receipt with me. The numbers on your card are not our
opinion; they come from a 121-point scan of your own neighborhood, and the report is a live
third-party page. Can I open it with you?"

[ammo: grid-methodology]

IF still angry after seeing the report: offer the dispute URL, read it aloud, end politely. Never argue past the receipt. The receipt argues.

IF "I'll sue" or legal threats:

"Everything published is a measured number with a public receipt, and the desk stands behind
the record. I am not the person for legal questions, and I will not pretend to be. The dispute
page is the correction channel and it works."

Log the call. Escalate to the owner. Do not free-style legal opinions.

Branch I-3: Skeptical. "Is this data even real?"

"Right question. Here is the shape of it: one instrument, a 121-point geo-grid on one phrase,
'chiropractor near me', run identically for all 95 scanned clients. No exceptions, so nobody
can call it an unfair test. 15,851 measured map points in this one book. Every scanned client
card links its own live third-party report, and the desk's exported data file independently
reproduces the book's core counts. And notice the file calls 13 clients winners. A rigged file
does not print winners."

[ammo: grid-methodology, map-points-total, csv-crosscheck, winners-13]

Then the live-receipt move. A skeptic in front of their own grid stops being a skeptic.

Branch I-4: DemandBoost, or someone calling for the agency

"Then you should have the policy exactly. The record is measured, published, and free. It is
not published by or affiliated with DemandBoost, and it says so on the page. Nobody named in a
file can pay to change one. If any number is factually wrong, the dispute page is open to you
like anyone else. The desk's standing rule: if the file is wrong, the file changes on the
record with a dated note. That is the whole conversation available to us."
  • Nothing else. No debate, no negotiation, no preview of future files. End courteously.

Branch I-5: A chiropractor who is NOT in the file

"The file covers the DemandBoost roster, so you will not have a card. What I can give you is
the pattern we measured: 82 of 95 scanned clients of one agency not owning 'chiropractor near
me' at their own front door, and all 97 of their websites failing Google's phone speed bar.
If you want your own numbers looked at, that is a free audit, fifteen minutes or an hour, your
choice, and I can set a time now."

[ammo: not-own-phrase, speed-all-97-fail]

  • Do not promise a free scan, a report, or an email. Only the audit, set live.
Script 3: Full audit opening (first ten minutes)

The prospect booked. This script covers the opening only: framing, disclosure, and the walk through their file. Prep: their listing page, their competitor report, and the book open in tabs before the call starts.

Minute 0 to 2: frame and disclosure

"Before anything else, the disclosure, because it is on every page we publish: this desk is
operated by a competitor who publishes the receipts. Not affiliated with, endorsed by, or
compensated by any agency named in the files. The file about your agency is free, it stays up,
and nobody named in it can pay to change it. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be
included. You are not paying for this audit either. Fair?"
"Here is how I want to spend the time: first your numbers, from your own file, no
interpretation. Then what they mean. Then, only if you want it, what doing something about it
would look like. Stop me anywhere."

Minute 2 to 6: their file, read aloud

Read their listing lines verbatim off their card. Then place them in the roster:

  1. The verdict: "The file's verdict on your practice is [Market winner / Partial / Invisible].

    2. Of the 97 clients: 13 winners, 46 partial, 36 invisible, 2 not measured." [ammo: segment-split]

  2. The phrase: "Everything is measured on one phrase, 'chiropractor near me', because that is

    3. where the demand is: it out-scores 'back pain near me' 56 to 29 on Google Trends across the last year, and leads in 48 of 53 weeks." [ammo: trends-category-vs-symptom, trends-weeks-lead]

  3. The map: open THEIR competitor report. "This is the receipt. 121 points around your building.

    4. Here is where you appear, and here is who leads your map." [ammo: grid-methodology]

  4. The website: "Your phone-load line says [X seconds]. Google's bar is 2.5. All 97 sites on

    5. this roster fail it; the median is 10.5 seconds. If you checked your site on a desktop and it felt fine, that is the desktop gap: median 2.4 seconds on desktop against 10.5 on the phone, and patients search on phones." [ammo: speed-all-97-fail, speed-median, speed-desktop-gap]

Minute 6 to 10: the one question, then branch

"Before I go further: what were you told you were paying for each month?"

Let them answer in full. Their answer picks the branch.

IF "SEO / getting found": put the map result against it.

"The book's own line: 'We measured the door with the traffic, and 36 of the 95 clinics scanned
are invisible on it.'" [ammo: trends-book-tie]

IF "my website": put the photocopier against it.

"The book's headline on that: 'They paid for marketing. They got a photocopier.' 83 of the 97
sites run one of just 12 shared templates. The largest template family holds 23 client sites
and DemandBoost's own homepage. 172 sentences repeat word for word across different client
sites. 50 sites load a stylesheet literally named css/demandboost.css; you can View Source on
your own page and read it."

[ammo: photocopier-headline] [ammo: templates-83-of-97] [ammo: agency-on-same-template] [ammo: sentences-172] [ammo: css-named-after-agency]

IF "I don't really know": that is itself the finding.

"Then the invoice question is the audit. 90 of 97 client homepages carry a footer link back
to demandboost.com and 89 print a visible attribution line. The clearest thing your monthly
payment measurably produces is on their site's behalf, not yours."

[ammo: footer-links-90, attribution-89]

IF they dispute a number at any point: never argue from memory. Open the stat's receipt (their report, or the book section) and read it together. If they still dispute it, give them the dispute page and move on. The record self-corrects; the audit does not need to.

Hand off to the auditor's own agenda from here. The opening's job is done when the prospect has seen their own grid, their own load time, and their own footer, and knows the desk sells nothing on this call except the conversation they are already in.

NIS video · Homepage · "Opening statement"

Fills: the homepage evidence locker, Item 001. The live page already frames this exact video: locker bar reads "Evidence locker · Item 001 · Opening statement", film tag reads "James · Why we publish", caption reads "Direct to camera · Who is doing this, and why". Source: Marketing/nobodyissafe-hub/site/index.html (hero locker block, lines 44 to 54, re-verified by read 2026-08-26).

Job: answer "who is doing this and why" fast, per the locked desk-c "Red file" spec (_concepts/concepts-notes.md): the page behaves like the object it publishes; the video must answer who-and-why before the visitor reaches a single stat. The agency viewer should feel "there is a folder with my name on it"; the client viewer should get sobriety, not menace.

Laws in force for this script: zero em dashes, zero en dashes; integrity anchor spoken verbatim; every number traces to the claim ledger at the bottom; no follow-up promises (no sending infrastructure exists; form endpoint, scheduler, and desk email are owner-owed); the only live surface is the nis-v2 preview at (answered 200 on 2026-08-26), and the real domain is dark, so no URL is spoken on camera; the offer is the free audit in two formats and the superseded offer word is never spoken or shown; no client emails, phones, or owner personal names.

Concepts

Concept H1 · "Opening statement" (RECOMMENDED)

  • Angle: James delivers the desk's opening statement seated at the desk, the three case folders in frame. Who, what, and why land inside the first fifteen seconds. The video is the spoken version of what the page already says, which is exactly what the film-frame toast on the live page promises: "The film records what this page already says."
  • Setting: the desk. Dark room, one warm key light, black surface, three manila folders with red tabs (ECP-710, DB-097, RRH-146) stacked in reach. Nothing else in frame.
  • Tone: flat, procedural, unhurried. Court-record register, not pitch register. No music until the last ten seconds, and then low.
  • Length: 60 to 80 seconds.

Concept H2 · "The scan you can stand on"

  • Angle: cold open outdoors. James stands on an ordinary street corner, runs a live map search on his phone, shows the screen, and says the desk did this at 131,285 map points. Then cut to the desk for the who-and-why.
  • Setting: sidewalk in any commercial strip, phone in hand; second setup at the desk.
  • Tone: field report. Slightly faster than H1.
  • Length: 75 to 90 seconds.
  • Tradeoff: needs a second location and a clean phone-screen capture. Strong method proof, but who-and-why arrives later than the brand spec wants on this page.

Concept H3 · "Cold open on the number"

  • Angle: black screen, white type: 275 of 280. James's voice reads the RankReallyHigh finding before his face appears, then the reveal and the who-and-why.
  • Setting: the desk, plus one full-screen type card.
  • Tone: severe. The most cinematic of the three.
  • Length: 60 to 75 seconds.
  • Tradeoff: leads the whole-desk page with one agency's file. H1 leads with the desk itself, which is the homepage's job.
Recommended script · H1 · target 75 seconds (about 185 words)

Delivery notes: conversational pace, around 145 words per minute. Pauses marked with (beat). Read it like testimony, not like ad copy. Numbers are spoken exactly as written; the matching on-screen stat cards carry the same figures.

[b-roll: overhead, hands set three manila folders on the desk one at a time, red tabs up: ECP-710, DB-097, RRH-146]

JAMES (V.O., then on camera): I'm James Compton. I own marketing agencies, and I built SGEN, the software stack they run on. The agencies in these files are my competitors.

[cut to: James at the desk, direct to camera]

So read this page knowing exactly who's saying it. (beat)

This desk measures what marketing agencies actually deliver. Not their three best logos. The whole client book. Every business gets the same treatment: its rank recorded on a grid of real map points around its own front door, a speed test on its site, and a read of the page source to see if the custom website is custom. Then we publish all of it.

[b-roll: grid pins landing on a map, one by one, fast; then a listing card with its finding lines]

Three books so far. 906 businesses measured. 131,285 map points, rank recorded at every pin.

[cut back to James, slower]

Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included. (beat) The files are free, and nobody named in one can pay to change it.

If your agency is in these files, your numbers are already here. Find your listing. Read what your monthly report left out. Then take the free audit, fifteen minutes or an hour. Taking it never means a pitch; if the fit is real, you'll hear how we fix it. The receipts are free either way.

NIS video · About page · "The full story"

Fills: the About page evidence locker, Item 002. This is the spoken version of the page's own "The story, in writing" and "The record behind the desk" sections, fact-locked to that copy: SEOGuarantee, then Dispenza, then MediMarketing, then SGEN, and the one billion dollars a year claim only in the About page's own wording ("more than a billion dollars a year in client sales runs through its agencies... by their own books").

James Compton is the desk's published byline on this page and is the on-camera voice.

Concepts

Concept A1 · "The long version, direct to camera" (RECOMMENDED)

  • Angle: one setup, one take feel. James tells the story in the same order the page tells it: the problem, the realization, the desk, where he stands, the record behind him, why it's fair, the invitation to check. Because the video sits directly above the written version, spoken and written must agree line for line where they overlap.
  • Setting: the desk, slightly wider frame than the homepage video, so the two read as the same room but a longer sitting. The four track-record beats get numbered type cards (01 to 04) instead of set changes.
  • Tone: measured, personal, zero swagger. This is the one video where James is allowed to sound like a person before an operator.
  • Length: 2:45 to 3:15.

Concept A2 · "Four folders, four chapters"

  • Angle: the track record as physical objects. James places four items on the desk one at a time (cards or folders labeled SEOGuarantee, Dispenza, MediMarketing, SGEN) and gives each its chapter, then sweeps them aside for the declaration.
  • Setting: the desk, overhead insert shots between talking segments.
  • Tone: show and tell, procedural.
  • Length: 3:00 to 3:30.
  • Tradeoff: stronger visual rhythm, more edit work, and the personal story gets less room than the resume.

Concept A3 · "The invitation"

  • Angle: the whole video framed around one line from the page: if the numbers were wrong, the fastest way to end this desk would be to check one. Opens on that sentence, then earns it: story, record, method, and back to the invitation.
  • Setting: the desk.
  • Tone: confident, almost quiet.
  • Length: 2:15 to 2:45.
  • Tradeoff: the most memorable single idea, but it buries who-James-is deeper than an About page should.
Recommended script · A1 · target 3:05 (about 450 words)

Delivery notes: about 145 words per minute. This one is allowed pauses. The four record beats are separated by numbered type cards; James does not change position. Speak the anchor sentence exactly.

[open: James at the desk, wider frame than the homepage video. No folders. Quiet.]

JAMES: I'm James Compton. I spent years inside local marketing. Building sites, running campaigns, and reading the monthly reports agencies send their clients. And I kept seeing the same thing. The report and the invoice come from the same desk. And the client has no way to check the report. (beat) When the people grading the work are the people selling it, the grade is an ad.

Here's the strange part. Checking is possible. A business's map ranking isn't a secret. It's sitting in public, at every street corner, for anyone who scans it. A website's speed is measurable by anyone with the URL. Whether a custom site is one of hundreds of copies of the same template is right there in the page source. None of that needs inside access. It needs instruments, patience, and no financial reason to look away.

[b-roll: slow push on a map grid; a speed test running; page source scrolling]

So that became this desk. I measure an agency's entire client book. Every client I can find. Not a sample, not the three success stories on the agency's homepage. Same instruments, applied the same way, to every business. Then I publish the whole thing. If an agency's results are good, the file says so. (beat) So far, the files have said something else.

And yes. I compete with the agencies in these files. I want you to weigh that, so here is the record behind the desk, in the order it was built.

[type card: 01 · SEOGuarantee]

It started with SEOGuarantee. An SEO agency with the promise built into the name. Measured results, guaranteed, and the measuring came first. Every habit this desk has, measure first, publish the number, stand behind it, was learned there.

[type card: 02 · Dispenza]

Then Dispenza. Cannabis marketing for dispensaries nationwide. Five hundred plus dispensary sites built and run, about five years, all on SGEN. Dispenza reports 1.2 billion dollars in sales influenced across its portfolio, and 3.8 billion impressions, as Dispenza reports it.

[type card: 03 · MediMarketing]

Then MediMarketing. Medical and dental, optometry to chiropractic, pricing printed on the public site and a guarantee behind it. Its method stakes everything on two numbers: how fast a site paints, and how much of the local map a business holds. The same two numbers every file on this desk measures.

[type card: 04 · SGEN]

And under all of it, SGEN. The software stack built under those agencies, now a platform in its own right. The entire WordPress ecosystem, rebuilt from scratch. Sites, SEO tooling, the operating system the whole book runs on.

[cut back to James, close]

Put together, more than a billion dollars a year in client sales runs through those agencies, by their own books. Every vertical in these files is a market my agencies compete in. Dispensaries. Eye care. Chiropractic. I'm not reviewing strangers. I'm measuring the competition. (beat) That's the disclosure. It's also the credential.

So here's the deal, printed on the door. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included. I hold no stake in any agency named here. No agency has ever paid this desk, and no agency can pay to be excluded, softened, or included. A file that can be bought is worthless, so the files are not for sale, in either direction. What is for sale, openly, is what comes after the file. If you read your listing and want a second opinion, the audit is free, fifteen minutes or an hour, your call, and nobody pitches you unless the fit is real. The receipts are free either way.

And if you think a number in a file is wrong: good. Check it. The method is published with every dossier, and every figure can be recomputed by a stranger. If the numbers were wrong, the fastest way to end this desk would be to check one. (beat) That's an invitation.

[hold two seconds. Cut to black. White type: NOBODY IS SAFE]

Concepts

Concept R1 · "Read the file aloud" (RECOMMENDED)

  • Angle: James opens the printed book at the desk and reads it: docket and scope, the headline, the flip, the trends line, the lawn company and the spam listing, the website they sold, the pattern, who publishes and why, the open door. The device is that he is quoting a published file, not making claims. Ends on the free audit, two formats.
  • Setting: the desk, RRH-146 folder open, printed pages turned on camera. Insert shots of the actual live book on a screen.
  • Tone: deliberate. Never humble. Let the numbers do the acceleration.
  • Length: 2:45 to 3:15.

Concept R2 · "The two doors"

  • Angle: built entirely around the book's core argument: two phrases, two doors, and the agency optimized the smaller one. Props or graphics: a big door labeled "weed", a small door labeled "dispensary" (the big one draws roughly twice the search interest [trends-note-verbatim]), the roster stacked at the small one.
  • Setting: the desk plus one graphic device.
  • Tone: explainer, patient.
  • Length: 2:00 to 2:30.
  • Tradeoff: cleanest single idea in the file, but it spends the runtime on the argument and leaves the website findings on the floor (98 of the 99 sites fail a majority of the product checks, 78 of 99 measured [product-failing-verdict]).

Concept R3 · "The lawn company"

  • Angle: cold open: "A lawn care company outranks paying dispensaries on the phrase weed near me. Eleven times." [grid-leader-weed-man-lawn-care] Then earn it backwards through the file.
  • Setting: the desk.
  • Tone: dry. The joke tells itself; James never smiles.
  • Length: 2:15 to 2:45.

Recommended script · R1 · target 3:05 (about 450 words)

Delivery notes: about 145 words per minute. Numbers spoken exactly as written, twins included. Where a line is the book's own rendered copy it is read as a quote, and the frame shows the actual page. No URL, no personal name other than the speaker's own.

[open: the desk. The RRH-146 folder. James opens it.]

JAMES: Case RRH-146. RankReallyHigh, a marketing agency for cannabis retail. A docket number, not a count. The count: 99 client dispensaries, 280 storefronts, 33,880 map points, one phrase, "weed near me." Filed August ninth, amended August twenty-sixth. (beat) Every number I say is printed in the file.

[insert: live book on screen, headline visible]

The headline: 275 of 280 storefronts are invisible on "weed near me." Type the search customers actually type, anywhere around the store: it never shows. Not fourth. Not twentieth. Not there. 5 of 280 show up.

Same 280 storefronts, same Google listings, scanned on the phrase the agency chose instead: "dispensary near me." There, 139 of 280 crack the top three and 67 are number one. (beat) The scan works. What they sold doesn't.

[insert: book's trends chart]

Google Trends, past twelve months: "weed" draws roughly twice the search interest of "dispensary," every single week for a year, 53 weeks out of 53. The file's own line: the agency optimized the smaller term. (beat) They ranked the book on the smaller door.

Who wins instead? Weed Man Lawn Care, a lawn care franchise, leads 11 of those maps. A spam listing typed in look-alike characters leads 10. (beat) A lawn company and a spam listing, beating the people paying for this.

[insert: product section of the book]

The website they sold. 96 of 99 sites are one theme with the logo swapped. Google's bar on a phone is two and a half seconds; 98 of 99 miss it, 76 of the 77 that returned a load time. The typical site takes 9.2 seconds: a blank screen while the customer opens the next dispensary. On 54 of 99 sites, 52 of the 96 menus that could be read, Google never reaches a product page.

The pattern: 39 clients run more than one storefront; 39 of 39 are invisible at every one. The 5 clients that rank anywhere all fail the website checks, 5 of 5. Rank and pass, both: 0 of 99, 0 of the 98 scanned. (beat) Nobody on this roster gets both.

[James closes the folder]

The book's verdict, printed on the page: Rank Really High? 275 of 280 storefronts rank really nowhere.

I'm James Compton. I own marketing agencies. RankReallyHigh is a competitor; these are their receipts, one of three agency books this desk has filed. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included. The file is free, and nobody named in it can pay to change it.

If your dispensary is in this book, your map is already in the file. Read it, then take the free audit. Two ways. Fifteen minutes, and I show you every way RankReallyHigh is failing you. Or an hour: the whole audit, why we audit other agencies' entire books, and, only if the fit is real, how we fix what RankReallyHigh is charging you for. Taking the call doesn't mean a pitch. (beat) Your listing is at the link with this video.

[hold. Cut to black. White type: CASE RRH-146 · FILED · AMENDED]

NIS video · Dossier: EyeCarePro (CASE ECP-710)

Walks: the EyeCarePro book's own numbers, from the ammo sheet sales/_ammo/eyecarepro.json (every figure below carries a stat id; every id carries an extraction command run 2026-08-21). Book: 710 client practices, 85,910 map points, one phrase, "glasses near me", filed 2026-08-07 (hub card).

Placement fact: no film slot exists on the book pages or the dossier cabinet (verified 2026-08-21). This video is a shareable asset: it travels next to the book link in DMs, texts, and socials, and as a free-audit warm-up. The live surface is the preview URL eyecarepro/; the real domain is dark, so no URL is spoken on camera.

Laws in force: zero em dashes; anchor verbatim where echoed; every number traces to the claim ledger; no follow-up promises; business names and public measured results only (practice names in this book are their published Google Business Profile identities, per the book's own privacy note).

Concepts

Concept E1 · "Half the book, and the half that ranks" (RECOMMENDED)

  • Angle: the honest two-act structure the file itself uses. Act one: the map, half the book outside the top 3 at its own front door, chains leading 288 maps. Act two: the concession that flips, the half that ranks is standing on failing websites, and zero of 710 sites came back sound. The concession makes the second act land as measurement, not attack.
  • Setting: the desk, ECP-710 folder, screen inserts of the live book.
  • Tone: even. This book's power is breadth, not outrage.
  • Length: 2:45 to 3:15.

Concept E2 · "The chain across the street"

  • Angle: built around the villain the file names: national chains lead 288 of 710 client maps, one discount chain alone leads 82 and is top 10 on 332. The agency's clients are not losing to the optometrist across town; they are losing to corporate, while paying for local search.
  • Setting: the desk plus one map graphic device.
  • Tone: stakes-first.
  • Length: 2:15 to 2:45.
  • Tradeoff: strong for NOWHERE and POCKET prospects, but it leaves the product half of the book unspoken, and this book's most absolute number (zero SOUND sites of 710) lives there.

Concept E3 · "Count to nine"

  • Angle: cold open: James counts to nine, out loud, against a blank phone screen. Then: that's the median wait before an EyeCarePro client site shows a patient anything. 9,387 milliseconds. Then the file.
  • Setting: the desk, phone in frame.
  • Tone: patient, almost uncomfortable. The pause is the point.
  • Length: 2:30 to 3:00.
  • Tradeoff: unbeatable open, but speed is one check of six; the map story has to be compressed to fit.
Recommended script · E1 · target 3:05 (about 450 words)

Delivery notes: about 145 words per minute. Numbers spoken exactly as written. Quotes are read off the page on screen.

[open: the desk. ECP-710 folder. James opens it.]

JAMES: This is case ECP-710. EyeCarePro. A marketing agency for eye care practices. This file measures their entire client book. 710 practices. Every one scanned at 121 map points on its own neighborhood grid, on one phrase: "glasses near me". That's 85,910 measured map points in one dossier. (beat) Every number I'm about to say is published, and every practice in the file has its own third-party report link. You don't have to trust me. That's the design.

[insert: live book headline]

The headline, straight off the page: 358 of 710 EyeCarePro practices sit outside the top 3 on their own neighborhood map. (beat) That's half the book. A coin flip at your own front door, and that's what the retainer buys.

Who's beating them? Mostly not the practice across town. 288 of the 710 maps, four in ten, are led by a national chain. One discount chain alone, America's Best, leads 82 client grids and sits in the top 10 on 332 of them. And the median client grid holds 98 competing businesses. (beat) This is a crowded fight, the chain is already inside it, and the agency's clients are paying for local search while corporate takes the map.

Now the honest part. Half the book does rank. 352 practices make the top 3 somewhere. So the map alone isn't the whole case. (beat) The websites are.

[insert: product section]

The file ran six product checks on every site. Zero of 710 came back sound. Not one. 603 of 710, five in six, graded failing or broken. 577 of 708 measured sites sit on one shared template family, and that family spans 1,220 sites book-wide. So when a patient compares two practices, there's a real chance they're comparing the same website with different logos. And speed: 702 of 706 measured sites fail Google's two and a half second phone bar. The median site takes 9,387 milliseconds to show a patient anything. Count that out sometime. (beat) 307 of them take over ten seconds.

Which is why ranking isn't safety here. 300 practices rank top 3 and still run a failing or broken site. The file even has the perfect case: a practice that holds the number one spot at all 121 of its 121 map points, and fails all six product checks. (beat) The map and the product are separate purchases, and this book shows a client can be sold either one without the other.

[James closes the folder]

If your practice is in this book, you were measured. Your map, your speed test, your template, they're already in the file, free, with your own report link. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included. Nobody named in the file can pay to change it. (beat) Find your listing at the link with this video, read your finding lines, and when you want a second opinion, the audit is free: fifteen minutes or an hour, your call, and nobody pitches you unless the fit is real.

[hold. Cut to black. White type: CASE ECP-710 · FILED]

NIS video · Dossier: DemandBoost (CASE DB-097)

Walks: the DemandBoost book's own numbers, from the ammo sheet sales/_ammo/demandboost.json (every figure below carries a stat id; every id carries an extraction command run 2026-08-21, and the full DATA cluster was re-extracted from the book render on 2026-08-21). Book: 97 chiropractic clients, 95 scanned on 121-point grids, one phrase, "chiropractor near me", filed 2026-08-05.

Placement fact: no film slot exists on the book pages or the dossier cabinet (verified 2026-08-21). This video is a shareable asset: it travels next to the book link in DMs, texts, and socials, and as a free-audit warm-up. The live surface is the preview URL demandboost/; the real domain is dark, so no URL is spoken on camera.

Laws in force: zero em dashes; anchor verbatim where echoed; every number traces to the claim ledger; no follow-up promises; business names and public measured results only. The insight CSV's owner_name and phone columns are off-limits and unused.

Concepts

Concept D1 · "The photocopier" (RECOMMENDED)

  • Angle: built on the book's own lede: "They paid for marketing. They got a photocopier." James walks the map failure first, then proves the photocopier with receipts anyone can check in their own page source: one template family shared with the agency's own homepage, a stylesheet literally named after the agency, 172 identical sentences across "independent" practices. Ends on the reviews finding and the open offer.
  • Setting: the desk, DB-097 folder, screen inserts of the live book and of real page source.
  • Tone: forensic. The View Source moment should feel like evidence handling.
  • Length: 2:45 to 3:15.

Concept D2 · "The door with the traffic"

  • Angle: the wrong-phrase pattern as the spine. The agency can rank things: 25 of 36 niche rescans show winning shares on narrow phrases. It ranked the door nobody walks through. Sharpest case: one clinic holds zero percent of "chiropractor near me" and one hundred percent of "auto accident chiropractor". Trends data closes it: the category phrase leads 48 of 53 weeks.
  • Setting: the desk plus one two-door graphic.
  • Tone: explainer.
  • Length: 2:15 to 2:45.
  • Tradeoff: the smartest argument in the file, but it needs the map baseline established first and leaves the template forensics mostly unused.

Concept D3 · "The asset that moves with you"

  • Angle: open on the 13 winners the file concedes, then the file's own finding: the winners' median review count is 200 against a roster median of 49. The one asset doing the work belongs to the practice, not the agency, and it moves with the practice. Built for fence-sitters afraid of switching costs.
  • Setting: the desk.
  • Tone: calm, almost reassuring.
  • Length: 2:00 to 2:30.
  • Tradeoff: the best closing argument in the book, but as a lead it skips the map and speed evidence that make it land.
Recommended script · D1 · target 3:05 (about 450 words)

Delivery notes: about 145 words per minute. Numbers spoken exactly as written. The two verbatim book lines are read off the page on screen.

[open: the desk. DB-097 folder. James opens it.]

JAMES: This is case DB-097. DemandBoost. A marketing agency whose chiropractic book is 97 clients. This file measures all of them: 95 scanned on a 121-point grid around their own building, every grid on one phrase, "chiropractor near me". Filed August fifth. (beat) Every number is published, and every scanned client has its own third-party report link in the file.

[insert: live book headline]

The finding of record: 82 of the 95 clients scanned do not own "chiropractor near me" on their own local map. That's 86 percent. The median scanned client holds a 5.8 percent share of the phrase. (beat) One pin in twenty. Around their own building.

36 clients are invisible. Zero top-3 appearances at any of their 121 points. Four of them never appear at all, at any pin, at any rank. And the median client's average position is number 8.9, on a map where patients see three. (beat) Meanwhile, The Joint Chiropractic, a national franchise that is not a client, is the reigning leader on 23 of these 95 grids.

So what did the monthly invoice buy? The file opens with its answer, and I'll read it: They paid for marketing. They got a photocopier.

[insert: template receipts table]

That's not an insult, it's a count. 83 of the 97 client websites run one of just 12 shared templates. The largest template family holds 23 client sites, and one more site: DemandBoost's own homepage. The agency and its clients run the same photocopy. 50 client sites load a stylesheet literally named after the agency, demandboost dot css. 172 distinct sentences, fifteen words or longer, appear word for word on two or more different client sites. (beat) You can check this one yourself. Right click your own site. View source.

And the speed. All 97 of 97 client sites fail Google's two and a half second phone bar. Not most. All. The fastest site on the entire roster takes 2.87 seconds. The median takes ten and a half. 52 sites take over ten seconds. The slowest takes 52.7. (beat) More than a third of these practices are invisible on the map, and every one of the 97 is paying for a page that fails the bar.

One more finding, and it's the one that matters if you're thinking of leaving. The 13 clients who do win their maps have a median of 200 Google reviews, against a roster median of 49. The file's conclusion: the winners are carried by an asset the agency didn't build. Their patients built it. (beat) And reviews move with the practice. Not with the invoice.

[James closes the folder]

If your practice is in this book, your grid is in the file, pin by pin, free. Nobody pays to be excluded and nobody pays to be included. Nobody named in the file can pay to change it. (beat) Find your listing at the link with this video. Read your own numbers. And when you want a second opinion on the map, the audit is free: fifteen minutes or an hour, your call, and nobody pitches you unless the fit is real.

[hold. Cut to black. White type: CASE DB-097 · FILED]
